--- Page 3 ---
Preface

The European VAT Handbook aims to provide detailed and essential information about VAT in Europe for
practitioners who need to know about VAT issues in their every day work. In addition to practitioners involved
with VAT issues, the book could can be used by students, firms or and academic institutions teaching the subject.

In this book a detailed VAT structure, in an easy-to-understand style, is given per European country to enable
comparison. The book also includes Annexes which provide a good overview of the VAT structure, rates,
thresholds, reverse charges and simplification measures applicable in the various European countries.

The European VAT Handbook consists of country chapters of 27 EU countries, namely Austria, Belgium,
Bulgaria, Croatia, Republic of Cyprus, the Czech Republic, Denmark, Estonia, Finland, France, Germany,
Greece, Hungary, Ireland, Italy, Latvia, Lithuania, Luxembourg, Malta, the Netherlands, Poland, Portugal,
Romania, Slovakia, Slovenia, Spain and Sweden are included in the book. Included are also the chapters of
three non-EU countries: Switzerland, Norway and the United Kingdom.

The chapters of the countries follow the standard outline and cover a.o. the following topics:
+ How and where to register
+ Registration thresholds

+ VAT grouping

+ Fiscal representation

+ When is the VAT due (new)
+ Cash accounting (new)

* Consignment and call-off stock
+ Simplified triangulation

+ Transfer of business

+ Installation supplies

+ Distance sales

+ Acquisition thresholds

+ Use & Enjoyment rules

* Bad debt relief

* Cash discounts

+ Importation and postponed accounting
* VAT warehousing

+ Customs warehousing

+ Reverse charges

+ VAT rates

+ Exemptions

* Which VAT is recoverable
+ Adjustment periods

+ Invoice requirements

+ Simplified invoices

+ Self-billing

* VAT Compliance

* EC Sales Listing

+ Intrastat

+ Penalties

This edition contains a few new subjects such as the VAT treatment of returnable packaging, liabilities and
supplies that are made free of charge (i.e. the threshold for ‘gifts of small value’).

The information per country was provided by experienced VAT advisors from other than the Big4 companies.
The names and of the authors and their contact details are provided at the end of each of the chapters
covering the relevant countries. Most of them are also partners in VAT Forum. For information about VAT
Forum, please refer to the following link: https://vatforum.com/. Vat Forum is an intemational network of
independent, seasoned VAT advisors who provide a highly appreciated alternative to the big accounting firms.
Special thanks for my colleague, Dr. Jeroen Bijl who helped me edit this edition.

--- Page 4 ---
This book is based on information available up to 1 March 2025.
Marja van den Oetelaar

The VAT Consultancy Firm
www. thevatconsultancyfirm.com
Naarden

Marja van den Oetelaar has a university degree in tax law. After working for 13 years as an indirect tax
consultant for 2 of the BIG4 firms, she started her own firm, Het BTW Advies Kantoor
(www.thevatconsultancyfirm.com) established in Naarden in the Netherlands. Since 2002 Het BTW Advies
Kantoor B.V. has been advising internationally operating companies on VAT issues. The firm is also big in
compliance and fiscal representation.

On behalf of several tax institutions, she lectures on VAT. She has also published several books and articles
on VAT. Marja is the Managing Director of VAT Forum,

Austria

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Austria through the “Umsatzsteuergesetz 1994”.
VAT is referred to in Austria as “Uimsatzsteuer” or “USt” or “Mehrwertsteuer” or “MwSt”.

if Taxable persons

1a Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

The Austrian Umsatzsteuergesetz 1994 does not contain any specific rules regarding registration obligations.
Thus, there is no registration threshold for domestic businesses (with a seat or fixed establishment) in
Austria. The requirement to register derives from the rules regarding administrative obligations (filing of VAT
returns etc.). Therefore, taxable persons have to, in general, register for VAT purposes in Austria as soon as
taxable transactions occur. Consequently, a small business would also need to register for VAT purposes if its
annual turnover exceeds EUR 55,000 (EUR 35,000 until 31 December 2024). The threshold of EUR 55,000
(EUR 35,000 until 31 December 2024) includes certain VAT exempt transactions.

From the time the annual turnover is exceeded, the small business exemption no longer applies. However, if
the turnover is not exceeded by more than 10%, the tax exemption is still applicable for the current year.

The tax office responsible for the registration of the taxable person is determined according to the place where
the taxable person has established its business. The taxable person must provide the following information for
the registration:

+ a questionnaire (form Verf 15 for companies, Verf 24 for natural persons);
+ a specimen signature in original (form Verf 26);

+ acopy of the exiract from the Chamber of Commerce; and

+ the articles of association.

The forms can be downloaded at the following link:
Attps://inyurl.com/27r4ky9k

1.2 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

There is no registration threshold for foreign businesses (without a seat or fixed establishment in Austria).
Consequently, foreign taxable persons that supply goods or services in Austria are required, in general, to
register even if the supply is exempt (zero rated) or if it is subject to the special withholding scheme,

However, there is no need for the foreign taxable person to register in Austria:

+ if the recipient of the supply is liable to pay the VAT to the tax authorities due to the applicable reverse
charge mechanism; or

+ if the foreign taxable person is obliged to pay the VAT for received services because of the applicable
reverse charge mechanism but is simultaneously entitled to fully deduct that VAT.

From 1 January 2025, the tax exemption for small businesses (annual turnover below EUR 55,000) has been
extended to foreign companies established in other Member States. In addition to the national threshold of
EUR 55,000, the EU threshold of EUR 100,000 must also not be exceeded.

The tax office in charge of the registration and for the monthly/quarterly and annual VAT returns of a foreign
taxable person with no seat or fixed establishment in Austria is the tax office Osterreich Dienststelle Graz-Stadt.
Otherwise if the foreign taxable person has a fixed establishment in Austria, he has to register at the tax office
where his fixed establishment is located. Usually the VAT registration process is completed within one month.

Austria | 43

--- Page 44 ---
When registering, the following information must be submitted:

+ a questionnaire (form Verf 19);
+ a specimen signature in original (form Ver/'26);

+ a copy of the extract from the Chamber of Commerce and/or the articles of association; and

+ a cettificate of status of being a taxable person, in original, not older than one year (form U 70).

13 VAT grouping

The Austrian VAT legislation provides a possibility for VAT grouping. When two or more legal entities
are established or they have a fixed establishment in Austria which are closely bound to one another

by financial, economic and organizational links, then those legal entities are treated as a single taxable
person “Organschaft” for VAT purposes. Consequently, the supplies of the goods or services within the
“Organschaft” are not subject to the Austrian VAT and, therefore, invoices within the “Organschaff” are
regarded as internal documents. An invoice issued by a third party supplier to an “Organgeselischaft” entitles
the “Organtrdger” to deduct the input VAT based on that invoice.

As already mentioned, the Austrian term for VAT grouping is “Organschaf?”. The group of legal entities
controlled by another entity is called “Organgesellschaf?”. The controlling entity is called “Organtrdger”. The
Organgesellschaft” must be a legal entity, however the tax authorities also accept partnerships under certain
conditions (e.g, GmbH & Co KG). The “Organtrdger” may be a natural or legal taxable person or a taxable
association. Due to the fact that “pure” holding companies are non-taxable persons, they are not able to act as.
“Organtréiger” or be patt of the “Organschaft”.

Foreign companies which are not resident in Austria cannot be included in the VAT group. However, a
non-resident company can be included in the VAT group with its fixed establishment in Austria as well

as the “Organgesellschaften” established in Austria of a non-resident “Organtriger”. The effects of the
“Organschaft” are nevertheless limited to transactions between the established “Organgesellschaften” and the
Austrian fixed establishment.

The financial link is assumed if more than 75% of the shares of the members are directly or indirectly held by
the same person(s). An organizational link exists if the members are under common management or where
the management of one member is actually subordinate to that of another. An economic link exists when

the activities of the members are mainly aimed to achieve the same economic goal or are carried out for the
benefit of the other members.

Please be aware that in Austria the “Organschaf?” comes into effect automatically at the initiative of the
Austrian tax authorities when all the mentioned preconditions are fulfilled. It is not possible to opt for it.
Furthermore, the effects of the “Organschaf?” are limited to transactions between domestically located
“Ongangeselischafien” and the Austrian fixed establishments of a non-resident entity.

All the formal obligations (filing of VAT returns etc.) must be met by the “Organtrdiger”. Consequently, the

“Organgesellschafien” are not entitled to file VAT returns separately. All the members of the “Organschaft”
ate jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT debts of the “Organschaff”. The “Organschaff” gets a

44 | Austria

--- Page 45 ---
new common VAT number. Nevertheless, the “Organgesellschaften” can also use their separate VAT numbers
(e.g, for intra~Community transactions).

14 Fiscal representative

Fiscal representatives only need to be appointed by taxable persons from non-EU Member States where
Austria does not have a mutual assistance agreement for the event that the foreign taxable person carries

out taxable supplies to non-taxable persons, intra-Community supplies or intra~Community acquisitions in
Austria. Consequently, taxable persons from EU Member States do not need to appoint a fiscal representative
for VAT purposes.

Only chartered public accountants, tax consultants, lawyers and notaries with seat or fixed establishment
in Austria can act as a fiscal representative. Forwarding agents who are members of the Austrian Federal
Economic Chamber ((sterreichische Wirtschaftskammer) are entitled to act as fiscal representatives,

if they are provided by a power of attorney to receive any documents of the Austrian tax authorities
(Zustellvolimacht) by the taxable person.

A fiscal representative is in general not jointly and severally liable with a client for VAT debts.
2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Articles 19 and 29 of the EU VAT Directive are not directly implemented in the Umsatzsteuergesetz 1994.
Nevertheless, in case the Austrian Reorganization Tax Law applies, the transfer of business or part thereof, is
out of scope of VAT.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

The supply of goods or services free of charge may also be subject to VAT as deemed supply of goods/
services, Taxation of deemed supply of goods/services occurs in general if the acquisition of goods and
services entitled to a full or partial input tax deduction.

Under Austrian VAT law, there is no taxable deemed supply of goods in the case of gifts of small vatue.

This is in line with Art. 16 (2) of the EU VAT Directive, which exempts gifts of small value from VAT. The
Austrian tax authorities consider gifts of small value if the total value of the items given to the same recipient
does not exceed EUR 40 (excluding VAT) in a calendar year.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra~Community supply

It is possible to apply an exemption on the importation of goods into Austria if it is followed by an intra-
Community supply (ef. also paragraph 7.1.1).

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Postponed accounting on importation (ie. deferred payment of import VAT by reporting the import VAT on
the VAT return instead of payment to the authorities at the moment when the goods are released from customs
control) is possible in Austria. Payment of the VAT due on importation is debited on the VAT account. At this

point, it can be offset against any credit balance resulting from the deduction of import VAT on the relevant
VAT return.

For the application of the postponed accounting, a VAT registration of the taxable person in Austria is needed,
but no security is required,

Austria | 45

--- Page 46 ---
3. Place of Supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

With effect of 1 January 2020 the EU introduced the “Quick Fixes” imposing homogenous FU-legislation
regarding cross-border issues, also in the context of call-off stock arrangements. Thereupon the regulations
on consignment and call-off stocks were implemented in Austrian VAT Law which comply with the EU
regulation and replaced the previous regulations which only existed at administrative level. According to the
tax authorities a tolerance limit of 5% for destruction, loss and theft of goods is provided with the result that
the simplification for consignment and call-off stocks is not applicable if the tolerance limil is exceeded.

3.2 _ Installation supply

In Austria, the place of supply rule for supplies with installation or assembly according to Article 36 of the
EU VAT Directive is not explicitly implemented. Therefore, there is no formal definition of the notion “supply
with installation or assembly”. Nevertheless, certain construction and installation supplies are treated as
supplied where the right to dispose as owner is transferred, i.e, where they are constructed or installed. For
such a transaction, called “Werklieferung” or “Bauleistung”, the reverse charge may apply (see also paragraph
8.1.1 and 8.1.2).

3.3 Distance sales

Until July 2021, country-specific thresholds were used to determine the VAT liability in each country.
However, these country-specific thresholds have been replaced by a harmonized single threshold for distance
sale transactions within the EU. If the threshold of EUR 10,000 is exceeded, taxation must take place in the
Member State of destination.

The threshold includes all electronically supplied services within the LU.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

When the BU introduced the “Quick Fixes”, the EU also imposed homogenous EU-legislation regarding
issues in the context of chain transactions. Austria has implemented the regulations for chain transactions in
Austrian VAT Law which are in line with Art. 36a of the EU VAT Directive.

However, deviating from the EU VAT Directive on chain transactions, the Austrian VAT Law also includes
the following regulations:

+ Where the same goods are dispatched or transported by the first supplier, the dispatch or transport shall be
ascribed only to the supply made by the first supplier.

+ Where the same goods are dispatched or transported by the last customer, the dispatch or transport shall
be ascribed only to the supply made to the last customer.

+ The simplified chain transaction rules are not only applicable for chain transactions within the EU, but
also for chain transactions involving third countries (which means including import/export).

Austria has further implemented the simplified triangulation in the Austrian VAT law. The rules are in line
with Article 141 and Article 197 of the EU VAT Directive.

In case the middleman (B) is registered in the country of departure and/or in the country of arrival, the
simplification rule is applicable provided that the middleman (B) uses the VAT ID number of another EU
Member State.

Since 1 January 2023, the simplified triangulation rule has been extended to apply within a chain

transaction involving more than three parties. If all the requirements for a triangular transaction are met, the
simplification can be applied at any point in the chain.

46 | Austria

--- Page 47 ---
3.5 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons only engaged in
exempt activities and flat-rate farmers from suppliers established in another EU Member State amounts to
EUR 11,000.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Austria implemented the following effective use and enjoyment rules:

The place of supply is shifted to a non-EU Member State if the effective use and enjoyment of the following
services takes place in that non-EU Member State:

+ supply of staff and
* hiring out of means of transport.

The place of supply is shifted to Austria if the effective use and enjoyment of the following services takes
place in Austria and not in the non-EU Member State:

*  telecommunication services;

+ radio and television broadcasting:

* hiring out of movable property, except of all means of transport;

* sports betting and lottery (as well as intermediation of these services) according to the Austrian gambling law;

* Services according to Sec 3a (14) Austrian VAT Law (Article 59 VAT Directive) if they are provided to a
legal entity of public law which is a non-taxable person.

4. Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes chargeable at the time of the supply (i.e. when
the right to dispose of the goods was handed over to the acquirer or when the services have been cartied
out). The VAT becomes due, in principle, at the end of the calendar month in which the goods are supplied
or the services have been carried out, However, the VAT due will be postponed by maximum one month in
case the invoice was issued after the end of the month in which the supply or service was performed. This
postponement does not apply to services subject to reverse charge under Article 44/196 of EU Directive
2006/1 12.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer or the services are performed.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a longer period are difficult to handle. In specific cases, for instance
for services due to a subscription contract, maintenance contract or licence contract etc., it is possible to
determine the tax point according to the payment or invoices issued.

44 Cash accounting

In certain cases, it is allowed to pay VAT on the basis of cash received during the respective VAT period

as opposed to making payment on the basis of supplied goods and services during the period. The cash
accounting regime is applicable for liberal professionals (e.g. lawyers) and utilities (e.g. for gas, water,
electricity and heat supplies) regardless of their tumover, and all other taxable persons who do not fall under
the mandatory accounting or whose tumover in one of the preceding 2 years did not exceed FUR 110,000.

Austria | 47

--- Page 48 ---
5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

‘Acorrection of the VAT due is possible when the agreed compensation for the taxable supply of goods or
services becomes uncollectible which must be proven by the taxable person (i.e. by status of insolvency of the
customer etc). If there is only doubt rather than certainty regarding the collection of receivables, a correction
of the VAT due is not possible.

The correction needs to be made for the tax period in which the agreed compensation finally becomes
uncollectible (e.g, court decision or out-of-court settlement). The correction must be declared in course of the
monthly/quarterly VAT return (at the latest in the annual VAT return of the respective year). Possible evidence
can be the court decision of the insolvency of the customer or the out of court settlement.

5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
20% VAT EUR 200
Total to be paid EUR 1,200

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In case of cash discounts, no credit note needs to be issued.
However, the supplier/customer must amend the tax base respectively with the input VAT deduction in the
respective VAT return.

5.3 Open Market Value

In the event a supply of goods or services takes place between related parties (with no full right to input VAT
deduction) for reasons outside business purposes or for use of staff, the ‘open market value’ must be applied.
Related parties also include an employer and an employee, or a close family tie.

Open market value means the full amount that a customer at the same marketing stage at which the supply of
goods or services takes place, would have to pay, under conditions of fair competition, to a supplier at arm’s
length, in order to obtain the goods or services in question at that time.

In line with Article 80 of the EU VAT Directive, Austria has implemented the following transactions for

which ‘the open market value’ as the taxable amount must apply:

+ where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the recipient of the supply does not have
a full right of deduction;

+ where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the supplier does not have a full right of
deduction and the supply is subject to an exemption under Sec 6 (1) No 7 to 26 and No 28 of the Austrian
VAT Law;

+ where the consideration is higher than the open market value and the supplier does not have a [ull right of
deduction.

5.4 Returnable Packaging

‘Austria does not have a special VAT treatment for returnable packaging.

48 | Austria

6.1 Standard rate
The standard rate in Austria is 20%. A special rate of 19% applies in Jungholz and Mittelberg.

* assistance dogs;

* repair services regarding bicycles, shoes, leather goods, clothing and household linen;

+ passenger transport; and

+ medication.

‘The reduced rate of 13% applies for example on:

+ wine produced by farmers under certain circumstances;
* hotel accommodation;

* circus tickets;

* cinema tickets; and

* tickets for sport events.

63 Super-reduced and zero rate

installations of photovoltaic modules purchased after 31 December 2023 and before 1 January 2026,

In order to qualify for the zero rate, the photovoltaic system in total must not exceed a bottleneck capacity of
35 kW (peak). Furthermore, the photovoltaic system must be operated on or near a private or public building,

711 Intra-Community supplies

In order to be able to claim the VAT exemption on an intra-Community supply, the supplier must fulfill
special formal requirements, respectively the supplier has to provide the tax authorities with an accounting
proof and a proof of transportation according to regulation VO BGBI 1996/401. Consequently, the supplier
must have a combination of documents that provide sufficient proof that the goods in question were
transported to another EU Member State. Such documents are:

* copy of the invoice;

+ documentation of the validation of the VAT identification number of the customer;

+ a Verbringungserkldrung-declaration of the customer who picks up the goods that the goods in question
will be transported to another EU Member State; or

+ a Empfangsbestatigung-declaration of the customer in the other EU Member State that he has received the
goods which were transported by the supplier; or

+ a.commercial transport document from the carrier responsible for removing the goods (e.g. a completed
CMR waybill).

Due to the introduction of the “Quick Fixes” on 1 January 2020, it is also possible (o provide a proof of
transport according to Art 45a Implementing Regulation.

Furthermore, the intra-Community supply must be declared in the monthly or quarterly recapitulative
statements. In the context of the introduction of the “Quick Fixes” on | January 2020, a valid VAT
identification number of the customer and a correct declaration in the recapitulative statement became two
further compulsory preconditions for the exemption for intra~Community supplies.

7.1.2 Export supplies

In order to be able to claim the VAT exemption on the export, the supplier must also provide the tax
authorities with accounting proof and proof of transportation. In case the transport is arranged by the
customer by using his own means of transport, the supplier needs to have documentary evidence from the
customer that the goods were exported outside the European Community e.g. via a written or electronic
export declaration provided with a customs exit confirmation. In case the transport is arranged by the
customer by mandating a carrier, the supplier should obtain a dispatch-document (CMR etc.) or an export
certification issued by a carrier situated in the European Union or a written export declaration provided with a
customs exit confirmation.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse
There are no special provisions applying to VAT warehouses.

7.1.3.2 Bonded warehouse

‘Austria provides for special rules regarding bonded warehouses. It is possible to exempt the importer from
having to pay the import VAT by placing the goods under the customs warehouse regime. The entry of
goods in these warehouses does not trigger a liability to pay import VAT. Import VAT is triggered once the
goods are released for free circulation, Provided such goods are supplied within the bonded warehouse, a
domestic supply of goods is effected. Austrian VAT is due on such a domestic supply of goods. Sec 24
Umsatzsteuergesetz 1994 provides that the Austrian Federal Minister of Finance may issue a decree that
transactions inside these warehouses are exempt from VAT, however, such a decree has not yet been issued,

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Certain hospital services, medical and paramedical care, cultural activities, ambulance services, educational
services, services of a cultural or social nature, some services connected with sports, etc. are exempt without

the right to deduction. Some exemptions are granted on the condition that the supplier is a public body or a
non-profit making institution.

7.2.2 Other exemptions

services, certain games of chance, postal and telecommunication services, leasing or letting of immovable
property for business purposes, the supply of immovable property and services of small businesses (see also
paragraph 1.1), Regarding the possibility to opt for taxation please refer to paragraph 7.3

7.3 Option for taxation

‘The supplier may opt for taxation in the case of the following services (the standard VAT rate of 20% applies):

+ granting of credit;

+ supplies in connection with credit cards;

+ leasing or letting of immovable property for business purposes;
+ supply of immovable property:

+ supply of goods and services of small businesses; and

+ supply of investment gold.

The option to taxation is at the discretion of the supplier. Nevertheless, under Sec 6 (2) of the VAT Law, the
option for taxation of the leasing and letting of immovable property is limited to situations in which the tenant
uses the immovable property solely (min. 95%) for transactions that entitle him to deduct input VAT.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

In Austria there are several reverse charge procedures. If the conditions for a certain reverse charge are
fulfilled, the application is required, Once the reverse charge is applicable, a reference on the invoice must be
stated, e.g. “Sec 19 Austrian VAT Law; VAT due by the recipient of the service”.

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism - non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)

Certain transactions provided by non-established suppliers (e.g. with no seat or intervening fixed
establishment in Austria) fall under the reverse charge mechanism if the customer is:

+ a taxable person according to paragraph | (even non- resident/established or identified in Austria);
* anon-taxable legal entity of public law established in Austria; or
* any non-taxable VAT registered legal entity (respective/established in Austria or not).

The application of this reverse charge is mandatory and for the application itis irrelevant if the non-
established supplier is registered for VAT in Austria (ie. has an Austrian VAT identification number) or not as
long as the costumers is covered by the above 3 bullet points.

Transactions covered by the reverse charge mechanism are:
+ all kinds of services (excluding services of road tolls, letting of immovable property and services in
respect of admission to cultural, artistic, sporting, scientific, educational, entertainment or similar events);

and
+ all work supplies “Werklieferungen” (see also paragraph 3.2).

Supplies of goods are not covered but follow a special withholding scheme (see also paragraph 11.1).

On the invoice a reference should be made to the application of the reverse charge, e.g. “VAT due by the
recipient under Sec 19 Austrian VAT Law”, “Ubergang der Steuerschuld auf den Leistungsempfiinger”.

Austria | SI

--- Page 52 ---
8.1.2 Specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

For the following supplies and/or services provided by established or non-established taxable persons the
domestic reverse charge applies:

+ supply of construction work, including repair, cleaning, maintenance, alteration and demolition services
in relation to immovable property by a subcontractor for a contractor;

+ supply of staff engaged in the construction sector;

+ supply of goods provided as security by one taxable person to another in execution of that security;

+ supply of goods following the cession of a reservation of ownership to an assignee and the exercising of
this right by the assignee;

+ supply of immovable property sold by a judgment debtor in a compulsory sale procedure;

+ supply of old, used materials, scrap, waste and specific services:

+ supply of CO, Emission Rights;

+ supply of mobile phones and computer chips for which the taxable amount is equal to or higher than EUR
5,000;

+ supply of video game consoles, laptops and tablet computers, where the transaction exceeds BUR 5,000;

+ supply of gas, including gas in cylinders, and electricity to dealers, i.e. taxable person whose principal
activity in respect of purchases of gas and electricity is reselling those products and whose own
consumption of those products is negligible;

+ transfer of gas and electricity certificates;

+ — supply of metals (both raw and semi-finished metals), with the exception of scrap metals or metals that are
subject to the margin scheme under paragraph 24 of the VAT Law (if the amount invoiced is less than EUR
5,000, the suppliers can charge VAT to their customers at the rate of 20%; i.e. opt out of reverse charge); and

+ the taxable supply of investment gold within the meaning of section 24a (5) and (6) of the VAT Law.

As with the general reverse charge a reference for the specific reverse charge should be made on the invoice.

8.2 di nt and several liability

In Austria, according to the general Tax Code (BAO) managing directors may be held jointly and severally
liable for the tax debts of the company they manage. This liability only arises when the company’s taxes
remain unpaid and there has been a culpable breach of duty on the part of the managers.

There is a joint and several liability for VAT grouping. The VAT liability exists for debts of each member of
the VAT group for the entire period during which the entity in question was a member of that VAT group (see
paragraph 1.3)

9. Recovery of input VAT

OL Non-recoverable input VAT

The input VAT is not recoverable:

+ where the consideration does not consist principally of deductible expenses (costs) within the meaning of
Austrian Law on Income Tax or of the Austrian Law on Corporation Tax (e.g. expenditure on luxuries,
amusements and entertainment);

+ if the supply of goods or services is connected with the purchase, lease or use of cars, dual-purpose
vehicles or motorcycles; or

+ _ if goods or services are used less than 10% for business purposes.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT is recoverable and refunded by the VAT authorities from the first day of the month
following the tax period.

93 Capital goods - adjustment periods

+ Immovable property - 19 years following the year of first use
+ Movable property - 4 years following the year of first use

Austria has no adjustment period for services.

4 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

The refund application must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authorities of the
country in which the taxable person is established.

Annual minimum limit for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year is
EUR 50. Interim minimum limit for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months is
EUR 400.

Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless it is a
remainder of the year. Maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year.

Deadline for making the claim is 30 September of the year following the year over which a claim is made.

Supporting documents (e.g. the original invoices / copies of invoices) are generally not required. However, in
certain cases, the tax authorities may require additional information.

The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within 4 months of receipt of the claim. The
period in which the authorities must make a decision will be extended to 6 months or 8 months when the
authorities request additional information.

Ifa refund is granted, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account number
provided to the tax authorities, Time limit for appeals is 1 month following the decision.

Refund or deduction of input VAT is possible only where such input VAT is deductible according to the
Austrian VAT Law (see paragraph 9.1).

95 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

Austria does not require reciprocity when refunding the VAT to non-EU businesses. In principle, the VAT is
refunded to the businesses from all non-EU countries.

To submit a VAT refund request, the foreign entity must be registered in Austria. Registration is carried out
by completing form ““Verf 18-E” which can be downloaded at the following link: https://tinyurl.com/yxnyd994

The refund application form is Form “U 5” issued by the tax authorities. The forms of any other EU Member
States are also accepted.

The link where application forms can be downloaded is:

Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in 1 calendar year, unless it is a
remainder of the year. Maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year.

Deadline for making a claim is 30 June of the year following the year over which the claim is made.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content
A VAT invoice must contain the following information:

+ date at which the invoice is issued;

+ invoice number (sequential);

+ VAT identification number under which the supply or services is performed;

+ name and address of the supplier or service provider;

+ name and address of the recipient;

+ VAT identification number of the recipient of the supply if the invoiced amount exceeds EUR 10,000;

+ aclear description of the delivered goods or the rendered services as well as the quantity;

+ the date at which the supply or service took place;

+ the taxable amount;

+ the applied VAT rate;

average monthly exchange rate as published by Austrian Ministry of Finance, latest exchange rate by

+ the VAT identification number of the customer in case of an intra~Community supply:

10.1.2 Time limits

A taxable person is obliged to issue an invoice within 6 months after the supply was carried out to a taxable
person or to a non-taxable legal person. If the taxable person has carried out a work supply of goods or
services in connection with immovable property to non-taxable persons, then the issuance of an invoice is
required as well. A taxable person has to issue an invoice within 15 days following the month in which he has
carried out:

* — intra-Community supplies; or

* services subject to the B2B general rule according to Article 44 of the VAT Directive with the place of
supply in another BU Member State,

10.1.3. Currency

Invoices are allowed to be issued in foreign currency, nevertheless, the VAT amount must also be stated in

Euros.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

If the invoice is issued in foreign currency, the VAT amount in EUR is mandatory. For the conversion,
the average monthly exchange rate as published by Austrian Ministry of Finance, latest exchange rate by
European Central Bank or daily exchange rate if proven by a bank statement can be used.

10.1.5  Self-billing
Self-billing is allowed in case there is a (mutual or written) agreement between the two patties.
10.1.6 Simplified invoice

In the case of invoices for small amounts not exceeding a total of EUR 400, the following (limited)
information is needed:

+ date of issue;

+ identity of the supplier of goods and/or services:

+ aclear description of the delivered goods or the rendered services as well as the quantity;
+ the date at which the supply or service took place;

+ taxable amount and tax amount in a one-off sum; and

+ VAT rate.

From | January 2025, small businesses (annual tumover below EUR 55,000) will have the option of issuing
simplified invoices, regardless of the amount

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices need to be kept available for 7 years. Invoices relating to immovable property must be kept fora
period of 22 years.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing
The taxable person can choose any form of electronic invoicing as long as the authenticity of origin, integrity

--- Page 56 ---
of content and readability are guaranteed. ‘I'he use of an electronic invoice needs to be accepted by the
customer,

10.2 VAT compliance

Please be aware that there are no differences regarding the VAT compliance for transactions carried out in
Austria by foreign or local taxable persons.
10.2.1 VAT return period

‘The standard VAT return period is 1 month. Taxable persons whose turnover in the previous year did not
exceed EUR 100,000 may file the VAT return quarterly unless a monthly return is required by the tax
authorities. However, the taxable person can opt for the monthly VAT return period even if his turnover in the
previous year did not exceed EUR 100,000. In addition, all taxable persons must file annual VAT returns (i.e
summary returns) next to the monthly or quarterly VAT retumns.

The VAT returns must be filed electronically.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

Filing of a VAT return: the VAT return (form U30) must be filed electronically ultimately on the 15" day of
the second month following the VAT return period (i.e. the VAT return for May 2025 must be filed by 15 July
2025).

‘The annual VAT return (form U1) must be submitted electronically by 30 June of the year following the year
in question (i.e. the annual VAT return for 2024 has to be filed electronically by 30 June 2025 at the latest).
‘The payment of the VAT due must be made ultimately on the 15% day of the second month following the VAT
return period (i.e. the payment for the VAT due for May 2025 must be made by 15 July 2025 at the latest).

‘The forms can be downloaded at the following link:
https:/tinyurl.com/ypw6drt6
10.2.3 European sales listing

The filing periods for the European sales listing coincide with the filing periods of the VAT retumns.
Consequently, if the taxable person must file the VAT returns on a monthly/quarterly basis, then the European
sales listing must also be filed monthly/quarterly, The deadline for transmission is the end of the month
following the month/quarter in which the supply or service was rendered (i.e, the European sales listing for
May 2025 must be filed by 30 June 2025).

In general, the European sales listing needs to be filed clectronically via the electronic system FinanzOnline.
The paper form (form U13) can be downloaded at the following link:

https://inyurl.com/fc84rmp5

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

There are no national recapitulative statements in Austria.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

‘The tax assessment period in Austria is one year.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

In Austria there are no penalties for late registration. The tax authorities are only able to impose a default
penalty and a late payment fine if the VAT return or the European sales listing is not filed on time.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

Ifa VAT return is not filed on time, the Austrian tax authorities may impose a fine amounting to 10% of the
settled tax. The default fine will not be imposed in the event that the amount does not exceed EUR 50.

56 | Austria

--- Page 57 ---
If the European sales listing is not filed on time, the Austrian tax authorities are able to impose a fine
amounting to 1% of the taxable amount to be declared with a maximum of EUR 2,200 for each listing,

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities
If the VAT is not paid on time, the tax authorities may issue a late payment fine of 2% of the VAT due,

The late payment fine will not be imposed in the event that the amount does not exceed EUR 50.

10.3.4 Defaul€ penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

If the VAT is not reported correctly and, thus, not paid on time, the VAT authorities may impose late payment
fines (2% of the VAT due). Furthermore, if the taxable person has not reported the VAT correctly by intent,
consequences regarding criminal tax law may occur.

10.3.5 Offence penalties

In case of gross negligence, a penalty up to 100% can be levied. In case of intentional act, up to 200% can be
levied, Imprisonments can be imposed in terms of a judicial criminal proceeding in special circumstances.

10.4 Intrastat

In Austria, a monthly Intrastat return needs to be submitted to the Statistik Austria. It is also obligatory to file
an Intrastat return for distance sales.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
‘The Intrastat threshold for both, the arrivals and the dispatches, is EUR 1,100,000 as of | January 2022.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported
‘The information which needs to be reported for both the arrival and the dispatches statistics is:

+ the nature of the transaction (i.e. arrival or dispatch);

* the EU Member State of dispatch or destination;

+ means of transport (e.g. by road or by train);

+ the nature of the transaction (e.g. purchase, sale, return of goods, reparation etc.);
+ the country of origin (e.g. where the goods are produced, made or processed);

+ the HS code (8 digit harmonized commodity code according to Combined Nomenclature (CN));
* the quantity of the goods (i.e. net weight in kilograms);

+ other measure for certain goods (e.g. liters, m?, or number of pieces);

+ VAT ID of the recipient;

+ the invoice value of the goods; and

+ — the statistical value.

il. Additional compliance requirements

11.1 Special withholding scheme according to Sec 27/4 Umsatzsteuergesetz 1994

Sec 27/4 Umsatzstenergesetz 1994 provides a special withholding (split payment) scheme which must
be considered for supplies of goods carried out by foreign taxable persons in Austria in special cases.
Accordingly, the customer of goods is obliged to withhold the VAT on the transaction and to pay it to the
Austrian tax authorities in the name and on behalf of the supplier if the following conditions are fulfilled:

+ supplier is a foreign taxable person (neither seat nor establishment in Austria according to Sec 29 Austrian
Fiscal Code);

* supplier carries out a supply of goods which is subject to Austrian VAT;

+ customer is a (Austrian or foreign) taxable person; and

+ customer purchases the goods for the purposes of his business.

In case the withholding scheme applies, the customer must transfer the net consideration to the supplier

Austria | 57

--- Page 58 ---
whereas the VAT must be paid to the supplier’s responsible tax authority. If the customer does not meet his
obligations he can be held liable by the tax authority.

11.2 Special VAT identification number for forwarding agents

According to the Austrian tax authorities a simplification scheme is applicable to cases where goods are
imported from a non-EU Member State to Austria and subsequently transported outside Austria in the course
of an intra-Community supply by foreign taxable persons. As both, the import as well as the intra‘Community
supply is VAT exempt, foreign taxable persons do not need to register for such a transaction, in the case that
the following conditions are fulfilled:

+ the foreign taxable person does not have an Austrian VAT identification number;

+ adomestic forwarding agent acts for customs purposes as an indirect representative of the foreign taxable
person;

+ the domestic forwarding agent is responsible for the customs clearance of the goods:

= the domestic forwarding agent uses his special VAT identification number, under which his client (i.e. the
indirectly represented foreign taxable person) can carry out intra-Community supplies and make use of
the VAT exemption for that intra-Community supply;

+ the VAT identification number of the final customer is declared in the customs declaration. The final
customer’s VAT identification number must be issued by the EU Member State where the goods are
located at the end of the transport;

+ the invoice issued to the final customer must contain the forwarding agents’ special VAT identification
number as well as a reference indicating that the intra~Community supply of goods is VAT exempt; and

+ intra~-Community sales listing is donc by the forwarding agent.

Belgium

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Belgium through the “Wetboek van 3 juli 1969
van de belasting over de toegevoegde waarde/Code du 3 juillet 1969 de la Taxe sur la Valeur Ajoutée ’
VAT is referred to in Belgium as “btw / TVA”.

1, Taxable persons

Lt Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Belgian small businesses can opt for VAT relief. This concession applies to all taxable persons - private
individuals, companies or other non-legal entities (e.g. partnerships), however VAT Groupings are explicitly
excluded. The annual turnover taxable in Belgium may not exceed a threshold of EUR 25,000. The exempted
small business must register with the VAT authorities, but neither accounts for VAT nor files VAT returns. It
must have a clear bookkeeping in order to keep track of whether its turnover is exceeding the threshold or not.
This special scheme is not applicable in case of:

* sale of new buildings and new vehicles within the framework of an economic activity;

+ work on immovable property;

+ supplies of goods and services in the catering sector for which they are obliged to give the customer a till
receipt using a registered till system;

+ — supplies of used materials, used materials unsuitable for re-use in the same state, industrial and non-
industrial waste, waste for re-use, partly processed waste and scrap;

+ transactions made by farmers subject to the special farmer scheme;

* transactions subject to the special regime for tobacco and for fish, shellfish and mollusks which are sold
by public auction at the communal fish market on the docks; and

+ as of January 1, 2025 VAT payers established in other Member States can also opt in their own Member
State to apply the special exemption regime for small businesses for Belgium if they do not exceed the
threshold of EUR 25,000 for turnover taxable in Belgium nor exceed the threshold of turnover taxable in
BU of BUR 100,000.

The registration for VAT of a domestic business is usually started through the registration in the Crossroads
Bank for Enterprises “Kruispuntbank van ondernemingen (KBO) /Banque-Carrefour des Entreprises (BCE)”.
The following information must be provided for the registration:

+ aregistration form; and
+ the articles of association (for companies) or a copy of the identity card (for individuals).

‘The tax authorities usually assign the VAT number within a couple of weeks after receiving all the required
documents. There is no need to apply for a separate VIES registration.

13. VAT grouping

Where two or more persons established or having a fixed establishment in Belgium are closely bound to one
another by financial, economic and organizational links, those persons can at their own request be treated as a
single taxable person for VAT purposes.

The VAT grouping is optional. This means that a taxable person may choose whether or not to register a VAT
group or whether or not to join a VAT group, even if all the conditions of the VAT grouping are fulfilled.

Financial links exist if more than 10% of the shares of the members are directly or indirectly held by the same

person(s). Organizational links exist if the members are under common management or where the management
of one member is actually subordinate to that of another. Economic links exist where the activities of the mem-
bers are mainly aimed at achieving the same economic goal or catried out for the benefit of the other members.

Only taxable persons can form part of a VAT group, which excludes “pure” holding companies, unless they
actually control the decision-making process within the group.

Foreign companies which are not established in Belgium cannot generally be included in the VAT group.
However, the non-resident company with a fixed establishment in Belgium can be included in the VAT group.
A subsidiary of the non-resident company can also be included in the VAT group. The members of a VAT group
cannot file VAT returns separately. The VAT group files one VAT return. The filing of the EU sales listing must
be done for each member of a VAT group separately under their own “sub” VAT identification number.

The members of a VAT group are jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT obligations of the group.

60 | Belgium

--- Page 61 ---
14 Fiscal representative

A foreign non-EU company (except when established in Norway or the United Kingdom) must appoint a
fiscal representative if the company performs taxable transactions in Belgium for which it is liable to pay
Belgian VAT.

Taxable persons from EU Member States can opt to appoint a fiscal representative when performing taxable
transactions in Belgium. A direct VAT registration is more favorable in most cases. The advantage of
postponed accounting at importation (see 2.3) is not lost in case of a direct VAT registration.

A fiscal representative must comply with the following requirements:
* competent to conclude contracts:

+ established in Belgium;

+ has a sufficient solvency; and

+ agrees to represent the taxable person in question.

In addition, a guarantee (bank guarantee or deposit in cash) is required from a fiscal representative.

A fiscal representative, together with a foreign taxable person, is jointly and severally liable for payment of
the VAT, late interest payments and penalties.

In Belgium, a global fiscal representative for importation followed by an intra-Community supply (VAT
identification number beginning with BE796.5) and for VAT warehousing (VAT identification number
beginning with BE796.6) is also possible.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Articles 11 and 18, 3 of the Belgian VAT Law implement Articles 19 and 29 of the BU VAT Directive. If
transferred assets qualify as a whole business or as an independent branch of activity, the transfer is not
considered to be a supply and is, therefore, not subject to VAT.

A transfer of a whole business or a branch of activity (i.e. an independent part of the business) qualifies for

the exemption of Article 11 of the VAT Law, provided the following conditions are met:

+ the transfer relates to a whole business or to a branch of activity;

* both parties involved in the transaction are VAT registered;

+ the transferee continues the business previously performed by the transferor (but not necessarily in an
identical way); and

+ the transferee would have been able to deduct the VAT charged by the transferor if the transfer had been
subject to VAT.

The exemption js automatically applicable if the above-mentioned conditions are met. Therefore, it is not a
legal obligation to officially inform the Belgian VAT authorities or request confirmation of the transfer.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Article 12 of the Belgian VAT Law states that a supply of goods free of charge is to be taxed as a deemed
self-supply if the taxable person had an initial right to deduct VAT on the purchase or cost of manufacturing.
‘This is however not the case for trade samples, nor for gifts of small value to business relations. The Belgian
tax authorities consider a gift of small value if the total value of the items given to the same recipient does not
reach EUR 50 (exchiding VAT) in a calendar year.

Article 19 of the Belgian VAT Law states that the use free of charge of goods of a business, for other purposes
than the business’ own economic activities, is to be taxed as a deemed self-supply if the taxable person had an
initial right to deduct VAT on the purchase or cost of manufacturing. Furthermore work related to real estate

Belgium | 61

--- Page 62 ---
that is carried out for free by a taxable persons, and not for their own business purposes is deemed to be a
taxable self-supply.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

An exemption of VAT on the importation of goods into Belgium applies if it is followed by an intra~
‘Community supply. The main condition is that the intra-Community supply must be performed by the
importer. The conditions for an exempt intra~Community supply must be fulfilled as well. No license is
needed. The importation document mentions the Belgian VAT identification number of the importer and the
VAT number of the buyer of the goods (other than a Belgian VAT identification number).

For this specific transaction, foreign taxable persons can be represented by a global fiscal representative (VAT
identification number beginning with BE796.5).

2.3.2. Postponed accounting on importation

Belgian legal entities and Belgian fixed establishments can apply for a special license in cases of the
importation of goods from non-EU countries. With this license, importers avoid payment of VAT at the time
of importation. Instead, the importer must report the VAT due in its Belgian VAT return (taxable amount in
box 87; VAT due in box 57). If the importer is entitled to a full deduction, the VAT paid can be reclaimed in
box 59 of the same VAT return. On balance, there is no “monetary” payment of VAT, If a taxable person is
entitled to recover the VAT incurred on import, the VAT deferral can be advantageous for the taxable person.

Taxable persons that are not established in Belgium can use the VAT deferral license if they file periodical
VAT returns (regardless of whether they appointed a fiscal representative or registered for VAT directly).

A foreign taxable person can also appoint a so-called global fiscal representative (under a BE796.5 VAT ID
number) for the import of goods in Belgium, followed by a subsequent supply of the same goods. In that case
it is also possible to apply for the VAT deferral license.

3. Place of Supply

3 Consignment and call-off stock

‘As a general rule, a non-established company bringing stock to Belgium and supplying the goods from this
stock must register for VAT in Belgium.

If certain conditions are met, the supplier does not perform a transfer of own goods followed by a local
supply, but an intracommunity supply to his customer. The Belgian VAT legislation is in line with the articles
introduced as of January 1, 2020 with the “Quick Fixes” imposing homogenous EU-legislation regarding
cross-border issues, also in the context of call-off stock arrangements. newly introduced in the FU VAT
Directive. The comments of the Federal Public Service of Finance laid down in a Circular of 2 April 2020 are
the same as the content of the EU Explanatory Notes with regard to the 2020 Quick Fixes.

For consignment/call-off stock related to importation, the simplification is included in the VAT law. The
consignee can act as an addressee of the goods at importation. In principle, the general reverse charge rule
avoids the foreign company’s having to register for VAT purposes in Belgium for the local sale.

3.2 Installation supply

Certain construction and installation supplies are treated as supplied where they are constructed or installed.
In Belgium, there is no formal definition of the notion “supply with installation or assembly”. It is assumed
that if the attached goods retain their identity, an installation supply occurs.

Ifa good is supplied and incorporated in an immovable good, 50 it forms part of the immovable good or,
cannot be rernoved without great effort and damaging the immovable property, itis considered a service of
work related to immovable property” which is taxable in Belgium if the immovable property is located in
Belgium.

33 Distance sales

Until July 2021 the distance sales threshold for Belgium was EUR 35,000.

However, these country-specific thresholds have been replaced by a harmonized single threshold for distance
sale transactions within the EU. If this threshold of EUR 10,000 of distance sales made from the single
country of establishment is exceeded, taxation must take place in the Member State of destination,

The threshold includes also all electronically supplied services, broadcasting service and telecommunication
services within the EU.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Under Belgian VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the FU
VAT Directive. In addition to this, the simplified triangulation rules can be applied if:

* Belgium is the country of arrival for the goods; and

* _ Poth the middleman (B) and the person to whom the subsequent supply is rendered (C) are VAT registered
in Belgium; and/or

* the middleman (B) is VAT registered in the country of dispatch.

However, the simplified triangulation rules cannot be applied if Belgium is the country of arrival of the goods
and the middleman is established in Belgium.

Where B is established and/or VAT registered in the EU Member State of dispatch of the goods (Member
State |) or in a BU Member State other than the Member State of dispatch or arrival of the goods (Member
State 2), B may apply the simplification, provided that all the conditions for this are fulfilled ond the transport
is allocated to the first supply (A-B) (see ECJ judgment Buhler),

3.5 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, by taxable persons that do not have
the right to deduct input VAT (taxable persons falling under the exemptions without right to deduct and small
businesses using the special scheme) and flat-rate farmers from suppliers established in another EU Member
State is for Belgium EUR 11,200,

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Belgium has implemented effective use and enjoyment rules based on Article 59(a), sub a and b of the
EU VAT Directive for B2B services in relation to the transport of goods and thereto related services,
Subsequently, even if the place of supply is Belgium according to the B2B main rule of Article 44 of the EU
VAT Directive, they are not subject to Belgian VAT if the effective use and enjoyment of those services takes
Place outside the EU, i.e. ifthe full distance of the transport of goods takes place outside the EU. On the
other hand, even if the place of supply is outside the EU according to the B2B main rule of Article 44 of the
BU VAT Directive, they are subject to Belgian VAT if the effective use and enjoyment of those services takes
Place in Belgium, i. if the full distance of the transport of goods takes place in Belgium.

--- Page 64 ---
4, Taxable event & Chargeability

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods are put at the
disposal of the acquirer of the goods. The VAT due on services, in principle, becomes chargeable at the time
the services are supplied. The supply of goods, other than the hire of goods for a certain period or the sale

of goods on deferred terms, that involves successive statements of account or successive payments, shall be
regarded as being completed on expiry of the periods to which such statements of account or payments relate.

These principles, however, are overruled: in case of the obligation to issue an invoice, VAT becomes
chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice, Even ifthe invoice is issued before the taxable transaction
takes place. In case the invoice has not been issued timely, VAT shall become chargeable on the 15th day of
the month following that during which the chargeable event occurs. The general rule as to the time when the
VAT becomes payable provides that a taxable person must, by the 20* of the month following the end of each
tax period, file a VAT return indicating the amount of VAT which became due by lim during the tax period
concerned

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than one calendar month which are dispatched or
transported from Belgium to another EU Member State and which ate supplied VAT exempt or which are
transferred VAT exempt, shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such
time as the supply comes to an end.

For intra EU B2B services taxed according to main rule of place of supply of Article 44 of the VAT Directive,
where services are ongoing over a period of more than 1 year that do not give rise to statements of account or
payments in that period and on which the tax is incurred by the customer on the basis of Article 51(2)(1) of
The VAT Code, the services are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each calendar year up to the time
the provision of the services ceases.

4.4 Cash accounting

Im some cases, VAT is paid over to the Belgian state on the basis of cash received during the respective VAT
period as opposed to making payment of VAT on the basis of supplied goods and services during the period.
The cash accounting regime is available to persons engaged in the supply of taxable services or goods almost
exclusively to private persons and for which there is no obligation to issue an invoice.

For supplies of goods and services made to government bodies, a system of cash accounting is provided in
order to avoid the pre-financing of VAT by the suppliers involved (Art. 17(4) and 22bis(4) VAT Code), The
VAT becomes chargeable when a payment has been received from the contract partner. The VAT due becomes
deductible at the same moment. The issue of the invoice will not influence the chargeability of the VAT except
when the VAT is due by the contract partner.

It is not foreseen in the VAT legislation to mention ‘cash accounting’ on the invoice.

64 1 Belgium

--- Page 65 ---
5. Taxable amount

5. Bad debt relief

In Belgium, bad debt relief is available to taxable persons in case of bankruptcy of the customer. The right to
refund exists from the date of the court’s decision of opening of bankruptcy procedure. The general rules of
refund apply, i.e. the refund can be reported in a VAT return filed by the end of the third year after the year the
customer was declared bankrupt and thus the VAT becomes refundable. As evidence one can use the court’s
decision.

5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier intends to send the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
21% VAT EUR 210
Total to be paid EUR 1,210

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, a cash discount of 2 % applies”.

Whether payment has been received within 14 days or not, the taxable base will be EUR 1,000 — 2% = EUR
980 (Art. 28, 1° Belgian VAT Law).

The invoice will be:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000.00
Taxable amount EUR 980.00
21% VAT EUR 205.80
Total to be paid EUR 1,185.80

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, a cash discount of 2 % applies”.

5.3 Open Market Value

In the event of a supply of goods or services between some related parties the ‘open market value’ must be
used as the taxable amount for VAT. Related parties may also include the relationship between an employer
and an employee, a close family tie, or partner, member or director of a company or legal entity. It is applied
where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the recipient of the supply does not have a
full right of deduction.

Open market value means the full amount that a customer at the same marketing stage at which the supply of
goods or services takes place would have to pay, under conditions of fair competition, to a supplier at arm’s
length, in order to obtain the goods or services in question at that time.

The open market value is also the minimum taxable basis for the supply of new buildings and the related land
if VAT applies.

Some buildings can as from 1 January 2019 be leased B2B under VAT if opted by lessor and lessee. If the
lessee has no full right to deduct VAT and parties are related, the open market value is applied where the
consideration is lower than this value.

5.4 Returnable Packaging

Provided the packaging is considered to be a normal type of packaging and a supplier engages himself to
reimburse the customer for the value of the packaging if returned, the amount charged for the packaging is
not part of the taxable basis and thus not subject to VAT. There is however no obligation that the packaging
indeed will be retuned.

Belgium | 65

--- Page 66 ---
6. VAT rates

61 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate in Belgium is 21%.

6.2 Reduced rate
Belgium has reduced VAT rates of 0% (see under 6.3), 6% and 12%.

The reduced rate of 6% applies for instance to:

+ the supply of food and beverages (except alcoholic beverages);

+ hotel accommodation;

+ paper or digital, books, magazines and newspapers (under certain conditions 0 % for magazines and
newspapers);

+ passenger transport;

+ medication (0% for the supply of COVID-19 vaccines and COVID-19 in vitro diagnostic medical devices
as well as for services closely linked to those devices and vaccines);

+ small repair services: the repair of bicycles, shoes and clothing;

* the renovation of houses older than 15 years;

+ the renovation of houses older than 10 years for own use; and

+ demolition and rebuilding of houses in major cities, and for 2021-2022 under certain conditions in the
whole of Belgium.

The rate of 12% applies for instance to restaurant and catering services (except drinks) and margarine.
The list of goods and services subject to 6% and 12% can be found in annex A and B to the Royal Decree N° 20.

63 Super-reduced and zero rate

A rate of 0% applies to periodicals e.g. newspapers, both on paper and digital and certain COVID-19 related
vaccines and medical devices.

7. Exemptions

7A Exemptions with the right of deduction

Belgium has exemptions (with the right of deduction) for the following supplies and services (non-exhaustive

list):

+ exports and intra-Community supplies of goods;

+ goods supplied to travelers residing outside the EU under retail export schemes or through tax-free shops;

+ services in the framework of inward processing on behalf of residents outside the EU;

+ services of intermediaries relating to transactions carried out outside the EU;

+ vessels used for navigation on the high seas and airplanes used by airlines operating for reward chiefly on
international routes, as well as related goods and services;

+ supplies made to customers with (diplomatic) tax immunity;

+ supplies of monetary gold to central banks;

+ transport of goods to and from Madeira and the Azores;

+ goods supplied under customs control or VAT warehousing arrangements;

+ international passenger transport by seagoing vessels and airplanes;

+ provisioning of seagoing vessels and airplanes; and

+ — services to goods under customs control.

1 Intra-Community supplies

In order to be entitled to the exemption in the case of intra-Community supplies, the following is required:
+ the supplier must have a combination of documents, such as a copy of the sales invoice, proof of payment

66 | Belgium

--- Page 67 ---
(from abroad), a purchase order, correspondence with the customer (if available), and most importantly,
transport documents. These documents, or a sufficient combination thereof, must provide irrefutable
evidence that the goods in question were transported to another EU Member State. The use of a document
of destination (in Dutch “bestemmingsdocument”, in French “document de destination”) i.e. a document
signed by the customer who declares the goods have arrived in another EU Member State, is possible;

+ the invoice must include a reference that a supply is exempt e.g. “VAT exempt intra-Community supply,
Article138 of Directive 2006/112/EC”, in Dutch, “vrijgesteld wegens intracommunautaire levering 39bis
WBTW’” or in French: “exempté de TVA - livraison intracommunautaire — article 39bis CTVA;

+ the valid VAT number of the customer; and

+ correct and timely filing of the European Sales Listing

The above rules can still be used in order to prove the transport besides the presumptions of transport as
mentioned in Article 45a Implementing Regulation.

7.1.2 Export supplies

« In order to prove the exemption in the case of exports, the company must have the required evidence in its
company records that the goods have left the EU e.g.: an export declaration with a confirmation of exit;

+ transport documents (bills of lading/CMR) signed by the customer on receipt of the goods;

* import documents stamped by the non-EU customs authorities;

* payment from outside the EU;

* invoices from providers of transport and customs procedures; and

+ correspondence with the customer.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Belgium has introduced VAT warehouses, A VAT warehouse is always a clearly indicated physical place (e.g.
part of a building, a factory, a pipeline, an industrial site, silo, or tank). For the use of the VAT warehouse
regime, a distinction is to be made on the basis of the “type” of transaction based on which the goods are
entered into the VAT warehouse.

All goods that are imported can be entered into a VAT warehouse. Goods supplied locally in Belgium or
which are subject fo an intra~Community acquisition in Belgium can only be entered into a VAT warehouse,
in case they are mentioned in Royal Decree no. 54. These are in most cases certain unfinished products/bulk
goods (¢.g. goods that need further processing in order to be sold for final consumption). These goods include,
e.g,, tin, copper, mineral products, certain chemical products, cocoa beans, raw sugar and raw cotton,

Some other goods that are not mentioned in Annex V of the VAT Directive, are in Belgium included in the list
of goods for which a VAT warehouse is possible:

Raw tobacco (CN Code: 2401)
Wood pulp (CN Code: 4703)

Raw cotton (CN Code: 5201)

White granulated sugar (CN Code: 1701 99)
Tellurium (CN Code: 2804 50)

Selenium (CN Code: 2804 90)

Biodiesel and its blends (CN Code: 3826 00)
Iridium (CN Code: 7110 41)

Ruthenium (CN Code: 7110 41)

Cobalt (CN Code: 8105)

Bismuth (CN Code: 8106)

Cadmium (CN Code: 8107)

Antimony (CN Code: 8110)

Germanium (CN Code: 8112 30)

Participants in transactions with entering goods into or withdrawing goods from a VAT warehouse need
not be established or registered in Belgium. A non-resident taxable person can apply for an individual VAT

Belgium | 67

--- Page 68 ---
identification number (in case he is not established in the EU then an individual fiscal representative also
needs to be appointed) or can use a global fiscal representative (under a BE796.6 VAT identification number).
The taxable person trading in goods which are located in the warehouse does not need to be registered for
VAT purposes in Belgium.

The supply of goods where the goods are entered into the VAT warehouse or where the goods remain under
the VAT warehouse regime are zero-rated (exempt - however this is a preliminary exemption, which only
becomes definite depending on the subsequent transaction). The warehouse keeper must apply for a VAT
warehouse license.

When the goods are released from the VAT warehousing regime by the supplier, the goods fall back into the
scope of the general rules for supplies of goods (local supplies, intra-Community supplies and exportation).
In case the goods are released from the VAT warehousing regime by the customer/owner of the goods, in
principle, the VAT on the previous transaction becomes due by the customer.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

Where products subject to excise duty or goods intended for the production or processing of products subject
to excise duty are allowed to be entered under above VAT warehousing arrangements, and are stored or
located in a place situated in Belgium which is considered to be an excise warehouse within the meaning of
Article 3(11) of Directive (EU) 2020/262, they shall be deemed to be under the VAT warehousing procedure.
The procedure shall cease when the goods are declared for use for excise duty or when the goods are actually
removed from the excise warehouse.

7.1.3.3. Bonded warehouse
It is possible to put the goods in a bonded warehouse in Belgium.

Ifthe goods coming from a third country are directly placed under a bonded warehouse regime, no customs duties
and VAT become due. When the goods are released from the warehouse for free circulation, duties and VAT
become due. A buyer who declares the goods into free circulation becomes responsible for the payment of the VAT.

The release of goods from a bonded warehouse regime does not constitute an importation if the goods are
subsequently placed under another customs regime.

The sale of goods while still in bond is not subject to Belgian VAT. Evidence that the sales took place in the
bonded warehouse should be kept in the company records (i.e. list of the transactions from the warehouse
keeper, customs documents, the agreements, the invoices, etc.).
