73 Option for taxation

The supply of immovable property and the transfer of immovable rights ¢.g. usuftuct related to new buildings
and the related land is automatically subject to VAT if it is made by a professional trader in immovable goods.
For all other persons even when they do not have any other taxable operations, the supply of immovable
property and the transfer of immovable rights related to new buildings and the related land can be subject to
VAT upon option in the first deed.

The letting of a building or part of a building, which is used by the tenant solely for the economic activity
which gives him the status of a taxable person, can be subject to VAT provided that the landlord and the tenant
jointly choose to tax. If a building or part of a building is rented at the same time with the corresponding land,
the option must be exercised in respect of both immovable properties together. The option applies for the
entire duration of the agreement.

The option may only be applied to contracts relating to buildings or parts of buildings for which the VAT

on works with respect to the building itself and contributing to the erection of the building at the earliest on

1 October 2018 for the first time was chargeable. This condition of age of building shal! not apply to contracts
for the provision of spaces for the storage of goods (see above 7.2), for which the option may be exercised.

For financial services consisting in payment and receipt operations, including negotiation, with the exception
of debt recovery; the service provider may, under the conditions set by or on behalf of the Minister of
Finance, opt to tax it.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

In Belgium there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by foreign entities to taxable persons and legal entities with a Belgian VAT number
established in Belgium. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Articles 44 and
196 EU VAT Directive. In addition there is also the reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and electricity
supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in Belgium.

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism - non-resident suppliers (Art. 194 Directive 2006/112/EC)

In the case of a non-resident supplier, the reverse charge applies if the recipient of the goods and/or services is:
+ a taxable person resident or established in Belgium filing periodic VAT returns, or
+ anon-established person who has appointed a fiscal representative.

The reverse charge does not apply if the customer is a non-resident company with a Belgian VAT identification
number through a direct registration. The reverse charge applies to all supplies of goods and services which are
subject to VAT in Belgium. On the invoice, reference should be made to the application of the reverse charge.
A foreign supplier must use the following text on the inyoice: “reverse charge” (in Dutch: “Btw verlegd”; in
French: “Autoliquidation”) and can additionally mention “Article 51, 2, 5° of the Belgian VAT Law”.

8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism - specific domestic rules (Art. 199 Directive 2006/112/EC)

On the following supplies and/or services domestic reverse charge rules apply:

* supply of construction work, including repair, cleaning, maintenance, alteration and demolition services,
and the supply of staff in relation to immovable property by a taxable person established in Belgium for

70 | Belgium

--- Page 71 ---
a taxable person resident or established in Belgium who files regular VAT returns, or a non-established
person registered for VAT in Belgium who files regular VAT returns;

+ supply of investment gold to a taxable person resident or established in Belgium, or a non-established
person registered for VAT in Belgium who files regular VAT returns; and

+ supply of CO2 emission rights to a taxable person resident or established in Belgium, who files regular
VAT returns.

The supplier should refer to the application of the reverse charge on his invoices. A specific text should be
included in order to free the supplier of its liability to pay the VAT in case the customer would not be filing
regular VAT returns in Belgium.

8.2 di nt and several liability

‘There is a joint and several liability for VAT grouping, The VAT liability exists for debts of each member of
the VAT group for the entire period during which the entity in question was a member of that VAT group.

Incase of non-established taxable persons registered for VAT with the appointment of a VAT representative,
the representative is jointly and severally liable for VAT, fines and interests due by the principal

Every taxable person shall be jointly and severally liable for payment of the tax if, at the time when he carried
outa transaction, he knew or ought to have known that the payment of the tax was not or will not be made. in
the chain of transactions, with the intention of evading the tax.

Aco-contracting party shall be jointly and severally liable for payment of the tax if the invoice or document
as such, the issue of which is required, has not been issued or contains an incorrect indication as to its name,
the address or VAT identification number of the parties to the transaction, the nature or quantity of the goods
or services supplied or the price or accessories thereof. This co-contractor who proves the identity of his
supplier and proves that he has paid him the price and the corresponding tax or part thereof is to that extent
exempted from joint and several liability.

In the case of VAT warehousing, the warehousekeeper, the person responsible for transporting the goods from
the warehouse and, where appropriate, his principal, shall be jointly and severally liable.

Any taxable person who, through the use of an electronic interface such as a marketplace, platform, portal
or similar means, facilitates supplies of goods taking place in Belgium shall be jointly and severally liable to
the State for payment of the tax if he is not acting in good faith or has been guilty of fault or negligence in
relation to the following supplies of goods:

+ supplies of goods of which the place of commencement and a arrival is in Belgium, supplies without
transport in Belgium, and intra-Community distance sales of goods carried out by taxable persons for
customers not having to tax their intracommunity acquisitions.

+ distance sales of goods imported from a third territory or a third country, in consignments with an intrinsic
value of more than EUR 150, where the supplier acts as consignee of the goods on importation.

‘The Belgian VAT law provides criteria for when a person is deemed to have been negligent or not to act in

good faith.

Managing directors may be held jointly and severally liable for the tax debts of the company they manage.
This liability arises when the company’s taxes remain unpaid and there has been a culpable breach of duty on
the part of the managers.

9) Recovery of input VAT

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

The input VAT is not recoverable in the case of:
+ supplies and intra-Community acquisition of manufactured tobacco and alcoholic beverages other than
those intended for resale or to be provided as part of a supply of services;

Belgiom | 71

--- Page 72 ---
+ accommodation, food and beverages for immediate consumption, unless the costs are incurred by:
+ employees delivering goods or providing services away from the business premises; or
+ — other taxable persons who in turn supply the same services for consideration;

* entertainment expenses;

+ travel agencies cannot deduct the VAT on goods and services supplied by other taxable persons to be used
for travel services which are directly for the travelers’ consumption; and

+ with respect to the supply, importation and intra-Community acquisition of motor vehicles intended for
passenger and/or goods transport by road, and with respect to goods and servives related to such vehicles,
deduction may under no circumstances exceed 50% of the tax paid. Some well-described vehicles do not
fall under the 50% rule (thus 100% deduction),

In case of mixed use of capital goods (¢.g. cars, real estate), the VAT deduction must not exceed the
professional use of the goods concerned.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT is immediately recoverable and refunded by the VAT authorities upon explicit request.

9.3 Adjustment periods

+ immovable property - 15 years starting on 1 January of the year of first use: in principle this applies
to VAT on the construction or purchase of a new building with VAT. Following a recent court case it
should also apply on VAT incurred at extensive renovation activities that extend the building its period of
economic use comparable to new buildings;

+ movable property - 5 years starting on | January of the year of purchase.

Belgium also has an adjustment period (5 years) for services. Mainly these are services where depreciation
applies, e.g. software.

In case of letting of immovable property for professional use under the optional VAT scheme, the adjustment
period is 25 years.

94 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

The refund application must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authorities of the
country in which the claimant is established, The link at which the application form can be filed is as follows:
www-financien. belgium.be under e-services.

Annual minimum limit for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year is
EUR 50. Interim minimum limit for claims that arc made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months is
EUR 400.

Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless it is a
remainder of the year. Maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year.

The deadline for making a claim is 30 September of the year following the year over which a claim is made.
This deadline is compulsory.

Supporting documents (e.g, the original invoices / copies of invoices) are generally not required. However, in
certain cases the tax authorities may require additional information. When the taxable amount exceeds EUR
1,000 (in case of fuel EUR 250), a copy of the invoice is required.

The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within four months of receipt of the claim. The

period in which the authorities must make a decision will be extended to six months or eight months when the
authorities request additional information.

72.| Belgium

--- Page 73 ---
Ifa refund is granted, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account number
provided to the tax authorities. Time limit for appeals is the end of the third year following the year of the
decision. If this appeal is unsuccessful, the claimant can initiate proceedings before the national court.

‘The rules for recovery of input VAT follow the general rules (see above).

95 VAT refund to non-EU taxable persons

In Belgium no reciprocity rules apply.

‘The refund application must be submitted electronically by a form 803 accessible through www.financien.

belgium.be under e-services.

For a first request an e-mail has to be provided to foreigners.team2@minfin. fed.be. This e-mail should

inchide:

+ a certificate of the status of taxpayer (less than 1 year old and translated into Dutch, French, English or
German);

+ a brief description of the activity;

+ the exact address of the company;

* the e-mail address to which the refund decision can be sent.

The annual minimum limit for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year
is EUR 50. The interim minimum limit for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least
3 months is EUR 400.

The minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year, unless it is a
remainder of the year. The maximum period for making a claim is | calendar year.

The deadline for making a claim is 30 September of the year following the year over which a claim is made.
This deadline is compulsory.

For non-EU claimants, the following documents must be included in pdf-format:

* —acertificate of tax status which can be obtained from the VAT authority of the country in which the
claimant is registered (or IRS Certificate for the U.S.);

+ latest balance sheet;

+ acopy of the articles of association;

+ the invoices, creditnotes, import documents must be submitted; and

+  ifnvoices refer to contracts these must also be included.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Invoice content

For VAT purposes a full invoice must contain the following information:

* date on which the invoice is issued;

+ invoice number (sequential);

+ VAT identification number, full name and address of the supplier or service provider;

+ VAT identification number, full name and address of the customer/recipient;

* name, address and VAT identification number of the fiscal representative (if applicable);

+ when making zero-rated intra-Community supplies of goods, the VAT identification number of the
customer in the EU Member State of arrival of the goods;

* when providing services and the customer has to pay the VAT due, the VAT identification number of the
customer under which he purchases the service;

Belgium | 73

--- Page 74 ---
+ the date of supply of goods or the period of supply of services as well as the date(s) on which any advance
payment falls due, if different from the date of invoice;

+ the quantity and a clear description of the goods or services supplied;

+ the taxable amount (as well as the unit price, if any);

+ the applied VAT rate;

+ the total VAT amount payable;

+ the total amount of VAT incurred must be expressed in the national currency of the EU Member State
that sets the invoicing rules; if the taxable transaction takes place in another EU Member State, it must be
stated that the VAT due and the VAT rate is from that EU Member State;

+ incase of self-billing the following reference is required: “factuur uitgereikt door afitemer” (French:
“qutofacturation”);

+ reference to the reverse charge (in Dutch: “Btw verlegd” / in French: “Autoliquidation”) if VAT is
imposed on the recipient; e.g. B2B service: “Btw verlegd” -Article 44 EU VAT Directive / Article 21, 2
Belgian VAT Law -Article 196 EU VAT Direclive/Article 51, 2, 1° Belgian VAT Law”;

+ incase of a supply of a new means of transport, the information necessary to determine whether a means
of transport is new;

+ incase ofa supply of other than new means of transport, information details about the vehicle;

+ the reference to the special scheme, if any special scheme is applied, e.g., the reference “Bijzondere
regeling reisbureaus” (in French: “Régime particulier - Agences de voyages”) in case the travel agents’
margin scheme is applied; or the reference “Bijzondere regeling gebruikte goederen” (in French: “Régime
particulier - Biens d’occasion”) in case that particular margin scheme is applied; and

+ the reason for the application of an exemption, zero rate, or no mentioning of VAT. For instance:

+ intra-Community supply: “Exempt from VAT - intra-Community supply - Article 39bis Belgian VAT
Law”;

+ export supply: “Exempt from VAT-Export -Article 39 Belgian VAT Law”; and

+ the reference to the invoice issued earlier if several invoices regarding the same transaction are issued,

10.1.2 Time limits

An invoice needs to be issued ultimately on the 15" day of the month following the month in which the
supply or service was rendered, or a prepayment has been received. For continuous services which do not
give rise to settlements or payments during a period longer than one year, the invoice shall be issued no later
than the 15" day of the month following the expiry of each calendar year,

The VAT is generally due at the moment the invoice is issued. If the invoice is issued too late or not at all, the
VAT is due the 15" day of the month following the month in which the supply or service was rendered.

10.1.3 Currency

‘The total amount of VAT incurred roust be expressed in the national currency of the EU Member State that
sets the invoicing rules. When Belgium is the EU Member State that sets the invoicing rules, the currency is
the Euro.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

When the factors used to determine the taxable amount on importation are expressed in a currency other
than that of the EU Member State in which assessment takes place, the exchange rate shall be determined in
accordance with the EU provisions governing the calculation of the value for customs purposes.

When the factors used to determine the taxable amount of a transaction other than the importation of goods
are expressed in a currency of a third country or an EU Member State not belonging to the Eurozone, the
exchange rate applicable shal] be the latest exchange rate published by the European Central Bank at the time
the tax becomes chargeable. For currencies not published by the European Central Bank, the exchange rate
applicable shall be the latest exchange rate published by the Belgian National Bank.

When contracting parties have settled an exchange rate in the contract, that exchange rate is applicable and
not the exchange rate set by the Banks.

74 | Belgium

--- Page 75 ---
10.1.5  Self-billing
Under Belgian VAT legislation, seif-billing is allowed in case there is a prior agreement between the two
parties and each invoice is subject to a procedure of acceptance. The prior agreement is preferably a written
agreement for reasons of proof. The procedure of acceptance can be implicit (e.g. payment of the invoice).

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

It is possible to issue a simplified invoice if:

+ the amount of the invoice is not higher than Euro 100;

+ commercial or administrative practice makes it difficult to issue a full invoice; and
+ the invoice issued is a document or message which changes the initial invoice.

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date at which the invoice is issued:

* invoice number (sequential);

+ VAT identification number under which the supply or service is performed;

+ full name and address of the supplier or service provider;

+ VAT identification number of the customer, or when there is no VAT identification number the full name
and address of the customer/recipient;

+ description of the goods or services supplied;

+ per VAT rate the taxable amount and the VAT amount; and

+ specific, unambiguous reference to the initial invoice and the details that are being amended (on a credit
note, debit note or other document treated as an invoice).

Itis not possible to issue a simplified invoice for:
+ supplies with installation;

+ distance sales;

+ intra-Community supplies;

+ supplies for which reverse charge applies.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices need to be kept available for 10 years. Invoices relating to immovable property need to be kept for
a period of 15 or 25 years. Bookkeeping records with respect to the special scheme for telecommunications,
broadcasting or electronic services must be kept for a period of 10 years.

Invoices can be stored in any form, i.e. received paper invoices can be stored in paper form or in an electronic
format; electronic invoices can be stored in electronic form or can be stored in printed form.

The authenticity of origin, integrity of content and readability must be guaranteed during the storage period.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

There are no technical conditions for electronic invoicing. The electronic invoice can be sent in any format,
including PDE, on condition of authenticity (as regards to the issuer of the invoice) integrity (the content
of the invoice must be unchanged), and legibility. As a formal condition, the customer must accept that the
supplier issues electronic invoices. This acceptance can be implicit.

As of 2026 Belgium intends to introduce compulsory electronic invoices in a structured format for domestic
transactions by taxpayers established in Belgium.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

The standard period for a VAT return is one month. When certain conditions are met, a quarterly VAT return is
possible. In Belgium, no annual VAT return is possible.

The same rule applies for both domestic and foreign businesses.

Belgium | 75

--- Page 76 ---
10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

‘The VAT due must be received on the bank account of the VAT authorities on the 20* of the month following
the VAT return period. As of 2025, taxpayers filing quarterly retums will have until the 25 of the month
following a quarter. The same deadline applies for the VAT return itself, A VAT return mast be submitted
electronically.

Further explanation can be found under:
Attps://tinyurl.com/Sfrsbum6
10.2.3 European sales listing

The European sales listing must be submitted on a monthly basis if the intra-Community supply of goods
exceeds EUR 50,000 in the current quarter or in any of the 4 preceding quarters. The European sales listing
also contains the services for the period involved.

The European sales listing must be submitted electronically.

There is no threshold applicable regarding the value of the transactions. Further explanation can be found
under: Aitps:/tinyurl.com/Sfrsbum6

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

There is also a national recapitulative statement in Belgium, namely, a yearly list of customers who have
received supplies of goods and services for more than EUR 250 on a yearly basis.

This list must be submitted electronically. Further explanation can be found under:
Attps:/tinyurl.com/Sfrsbum6

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period in Belgium is limited to 31 December of the third year following the year of
accountability for the VAT due. This means that at the end of 2024, the last open year for assessments is the
year 2021. In case a VAT return was not filed in due time this period can be extended with one year,

In the case of additional information’s becoming available, the tax assessment period is seven years under
certain conditions, [n case of fraud, the tax assessment period is ten years.

103 Penalties

103.1 Default penalties for late registration
The tax authorities will issue a penalty of EUR 100 per month overdue with a maximum of EUR 500.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

If the VAT return is not filed on time, the penalty is EUR 100 per return and per month overdue with a
maximum of EUR 500 After a period of 5 months the VAT return will be considered as a case of non-filing.
In case of non-filing the penalty is EUR 500 for the first offence, EUR 1,250 for a second offence, EUR 2,500
for a third offence and EUR 5,000 for subsequent offences.

If the European sales listing is not filed on time, the VAT authorities can issue a penalty depending on the
seriousness of the infringement. The penalty can vary from EUR 25 to EUR 3,000 per listing.
10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

If the VAT is not paid on time, the VAT authorities apply an interest for late payment of 0.67% per month
(current rate in 2024). Moreover, a penalty of 5% of the VAT due, is issues in case of late payment but timely
filing of the VAT return. In case of late payment and late filing of a VAT return the penalty is 10% of the VAT
due,

76 | Belgium

--- Page 77 ---
After a case of late filing the tax authorities can, after three months following a declaration period, issue a
“replacement VAT return” for the highest amount of VAT payable in a return of the previous 1 months with
a minimum of EUR 2,100. After receipt of notification of a “replacement VAT return” the taxable person can
still file its missing VAT return. If not, the penalty is 15% of the VAT due.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly
If the VAT is not paid up to the correct amount, the VAT authorities can also issue a penalty.

If the VAT is paid voluntarily and before an inspection has been announced, the penalties are waived.
Penalties for infringements assessed during a VAT audit vary from 5 to 20% of the VAT due depending on the
infringement.

10.3.5 Offense penalties

In the case of fraud, the penalty is 200% of the VAT due. The Belgian tax authorities may also decide to
prosecute for a criminal offense.

10.4 Intrastat

A separate monthly Intrastat return must be submitted to the National Bank of Belgium (Nationale Bank van
Belgié (NBB)/Banque Nationale de Belgique (BNB))

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
‘The Intrastat threshold for the arrivals of goods is EUR 1,500,000 and for the dispatches is EUR 1,000,000.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The information which must be reported for both the arrival and the dispatches statistics is (sometimes less in
case of a standard Intrastat return):

+ EU Member State of origin/destination;

+ nature of the transaction;

+ the VAT identification number of the costumer in the case of dispatches;
+ 8-digit Combined Nomenclature (CN) code of the goods;

+ net weight of the goods:

+ code of region of origin/destination;

+ value of the goods in EUR;

+ mode of transport; and

+ Incoterm (delivery terms)

if the threshold for distance sales has not been exceeded nor the supplier has opted to tax in the EU Member
State of the customer, the transaction must not be reported in Intrastat. If the threshold for distance sales has
been exceeded or the supplier has opted to tax in the EU Member State of the customer, the transaction must
be reported in Intrastat (when exceeding the Intrastat thresholds).

When Intrastat reports are not filed, reminders are sent by the authorities. If these reminders are ignored, a
penaity (EUR 100-10,000) or a criminal sanction can be imposed
10.5 Additional compliance requirements

So far Belgium has not implemented special rules such as real time reporting, split payment or any other
special method of reporting and payment of the VAT.

Bulgaria

‘The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Bulgaria through the VAT Act “Zakon za Danak varhu
Dobawenata Stoinost” and the VAT Regulations. VAT is called “Danak varhu Dobawenata Stoinost’ or ‘DDS’.

1. Taxable persons

11 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

The threshold for mandatory VAT registration is BGN 100,000 (approximately EUR 51,130) and as of 1
January 2025 - BGN 166,000 (approximately EUR 84,875) taxable turnover for the last 12 months (including
zero-rated supplies and exempt financial and insurance services).

In 2020, an anti-abuse rule was introduced, namely when one person carries out the same activity on the
same premises immediately after another person, and these persons are related parties or act in an abusive
coordinated manner, then the turnover of the first person is included in the calculations of the turnover of the
second person.

Any taxable person can apply for voluntary VAT registration, regardless of the amount of turnover.
An application form must be submitted to the VAT authorities. The contact details of the VAT authorities are:

National Revenue Ag ney
Territorial Directorate Sofia, blvd. 52
Dondukov Street

1000 Sofia, Bulgaria

Ten.: (+359 2) 0700 18 700

Bulgarian VAT legislation does not provide for a simplification of the VAT registration.

As a tule, registration should take no more than 14 days from the day when the VAT authorities receive the
necessary documents.

The application form can be downloaded at:
www,portal.nap.bg

12 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for taxable persons

Foreign taxable persons must apply for VAT registration 7 days before their first taxable supply with a
place of supply in Bulgaria (regardless of the taxable turnover), insofar as the reverse charge mechanism is
not applicable. Foreign taxable persons can also register for VAT voluntarily. The registration procedure is
generally the same as for resident taxpayers (see also paragraph 1.1).

When registering, the following information must be provided to the tax authorities by the foreign taxable
person:

+ application form;

+ information about the taxable turnover in Bulgaria;

+ cettificate of incorporation of the taxable person; and

+ cettificate(s) of tax and VAT registration of the taxable person in the country of residence

+ notarised and apostilled Power of Attomey (if documents are submitted by an authorised person).

‘The application form can be downloaded at:
www.portal.nap.bg

Bulgaria | 79

--- Page 80 ---
Bulgaria has not implemented the SME scheme for foreign taxpayers yet at the time of editing this chapter
(13 March 2025), However, the bill is expected to be accepted any time soon. The threshold will probably be
fixed at BGN 166,000. The total tumover of the foreign EU taxable person must also remain below the EU-
wide threshold of Euro 100,000.

1.3 VAT grouping
There is no VAT grouping in Bulgaria,

14 Fiscal representative

‘Taxable persons from EU Member States can register directly for VAT in Bulgaria and do not need to appoint
a fiscal representative.

Taxable persons from non-EU countries with which the EU has not signed an agreement on mutual assistance
in tax matters (like the scope of Directive 2010/24 and Regulation 904/2010) are obliged to appoint a fiscal
representative when applying for VAT registration in Bulgaria. The fiscal representative must meet certain
legal conditions. The fiscal representative must be a Bulgarian resident company or an individual who is not
in liquidation or bankruptcy proceedings and has no outstanding tax or social security liabilities.

The fiscal representative shall be jointly and severally liable with the foreign taxable person for the tax
liabilities of the foreign enterprise.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

The transfer of economic activity as a going concern within the meaning of the Bulgarian Commercial Act
is outside the scope of Bulgarian VAT. In-kind contributions to the share capital of the company, as well as
mergers, are also outside the scope of VAT.

A transaction will qualify as outside the scope of VAT if it meets the formal conditions under the Commerce
Act. For example, the transfer of a going concern must be made in a contract with notarized signatures. There
are special legal procedures and requirements for in-kind contributions and mergers.

2.2 Supplies free of charge
Bulgaria generally applies the principles laid down in Articles 16 and 26 of the EU VAT Directive.

Free of charge supply is defined as a supply without remuneration or the value of the supply is significantly
exceeding the remuneration received.

The free supply of goods or services of ‘small value’ for marketing purposes or as samples is not taxed in
Bulgaria if the market price per item is less than BGN 30 (EUR 15,34), excluding VAT, and if their supply is
not part of a series of supplies for which the recipient is the same person,

2.3. ‘Importation

Import of goods from third countries (non- EU member states) is subject to 20% VAT paid to the Bulgarian
Customs with a possibility for VAT credit deduction if the imported goods are related to the taxable supplies
of the taxable person. In case of import of goods which are subject to reduced VAT rate in Bulgaria, this
reduced VAT rate would apply.

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

VAT on import can be deferred or zero rated if the import of goods is followed by a subsequent intra-
Community supply destined for another EU Member State. The importer must be registered for VAT purposes

80 | Bulgaria

--- Page 81 ---
in Bulgaria, and it should be evident from the transport documents that the goods are destined for another EU
Member State.

The importer must also provide the VAT number from the EU Member State of the customer to which
the goods will be transported after being imported in Bulgaria, There is no requirement to appoint a fiscal
representative or to post a bank guarantee.

2.3.2. Postponed accounting on importation

Payment of the import VAT can be deferred if the goods are under a customs suspension regime (e.g. customs
warehousing, temporary admission, inward processing, etc.).

Taxable persons who are allowed to use the VAT incentive for large investment projects can account for VAT
on importation in their monthly VAT returns.

‘Taxable persons may also account for VAT on importation in their monthly VAT returns provided certain
conditions are in place:

+ the imported goods are included in a special list;
* the customs value of each of the imported goods is equal or above BGN 50,000 (approx. EUR 25,564);
* the taxable person is VAT registered for more than 6 months prior to the import; and

* the taxable person does not have any outstanding tax liabilities.

Ea Place of Supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

In Bulgaria, call-off stock rules are in place and can be applied as part of the implementation of the 2020
quick fixes. The use of this scheme has specific additional reporting requirements with regards to the
movement of the goods.

3.2 Installation supply

Supplies of goods with installation are treated as supplied where the installation takes place. There is no
formal definition of ‘supply with installation or assembly’, but generally such are supplies of goods where
the installation or assembly are ancillary to these supplies and are carried out by or for account of the supplier.

The reverse charge applies if the customer is registered for VAT purposes in Bulgaria and the supplier is not
established in Bulgaria, A supplier of goods with installation must apply for a Bulgarian VAT registration,
regardless of the taxable turnover, if the customer is not registered for VAT purposes in Bulgaria,

3.3 Distance sales

Once the threshold of EUR 10,000 of all European sales of TBE services and distance sales of goods is
exceeded, the supplier may either register directly in Bulgaria or in their country of establishment via OSS to
account for Bulgarian VAT for their distance sales to Bulgaria.

A taxable person of another EU Member State may opt for voluntary registration in Bulgaria for its distance
sales before the threshold is exceeded.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

According to the Bulgarian VAT Act, the middleman (B) cannot be registered for VAT purposes in the EU
Member State of dispatch (A) and/or of arrival (C).

However, according to the Regulations for the application of the VAT Act, the triangulation simplification can
apply even if the middleman is registered in the Member State of dispatch (Member State party A) or arrival

Bulgaria | 81

--- Page 82 ---
(Member State of party C), if he has carried out the respective intra-Community acquisition under another
VAT identification number than that of the country of dispatch and artival

3.5 Acquisition threshold

‘The threshold for VAT registration for intra-Community acquisition of goods by non-taxable persons,
including those engaged in exempt activities and the flat rate is BGN 20,000 (approximately EUR 10,225).
Where a foreign taxable person no longer meets all the conditions for applying the call-off stock arrangement
and as a consequence performs an intra-Community acquisition in Bulgaria, must file a registration
application 7 days prior to the taxable event regardless of the value of the goods.

3.6 Effective Use & Enjoyment

No effective use and enjoyment rules have been implemented in Bulgaria.

4. Chargeable event & Chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In principle VAT becomes chargeable at the time the taxable event occurs, meaning when the right to dispose
of the goods as owner is transferred or when the service is performed.

4.2 Advance payments

Should the compensation be received partially or in full in advance of the supply, then VAT becomes
chargeable at the moment of the receipt of the payment, proportionately to the received amount.

43 Continuous supplies

In situations of continuous supplies where a payment has been agreed for different periods, then VAT for the
relevant period becomes chargeable at the moment the payment becomes due. In case of a continuous supply
where a payment has been agreed to happen after more than a year, then the VAT shall become chargeable at
the end of each calendar year.

Where the supply is agreed to be performed in different stages, then the completion of a separate stage is
treated as a separate supply and VAT becomes chargeable at the moment of completion of the relevant stage.

44 Cash accounting

Taxable persons may opt to apply for the cash accounting regime, insofar they meet the following conditions:

+ tumover for the last 12 months not exceeding 500,000 EUR (approximately BGN 978,000);
+ no assessment act for due taxes issued under a specific regime; and
+ no outstanding tax or social security liabilities.

‘The taxable person needs to file a request for applying this regime and the tax authorities need to issue an
explicit permit.

The regime entails that VAT becomes chargeable when a total or partial payment is received, proportionately
to the received amount. However, cash accounting may not be applied to the following supplies:

* — import of goods;

+ intra-Community acquisitions and intra-Community supplies;
+ supplies to persons who are not registered for VAT purposes;
+ exempt supplies;

+ supplies with place of supply outside Bulgaria;

+ zero-rated supplies;

+ supplies subject to the reverse-charge mechanism;

82 | Bulgaria

--- Page 83 ---
+ supplies under a lease agreement;
+ supplies of goods and services, where the remuneration is not paid by bank transfer; and
+ supplies between related parties.

5. Taxable amount

5 Bad Debt Relief

Bulgaria introduced the legal framework for bad-debt in the VAT Act relief from 2023, following CJEU
practice (in particular C-242/18).

In order to claim bad debt relief, the following conditions should be met:

+ the supplier has issued an inyoice for the supply and has accounted for and paid the relevant output VAT
on the supply;

+ the recipient and the supplier are not related parties;

+ there was no transfer of the receivable for consideration;

+ the supplier can prove that he has taken action to collect the debt; and

+ the supplier has notified in writing the recipient, that it considers the respective receivable as a bad debt
and has evidence that the notification has been sent to the address of management of the recipient.

The taxable amount and the VAT charged are decreased through issuing a credit note, or a protocol.

The type of document depends on the status of the recipient, as well as on whether the goods/services
received have been used by the recipient for carrying out taxable supplies with the right to VAT credit,

5.2 Cash Discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
20% VAT EUR__200
Total to be paid EUR 1,200

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In case of a cash discount, the supplier must issue a credit note for
the discounted amount.

5.3 Open Market Value

The open market value shoutd be applied to determine the taxable amount for VAT purposes in supplies
between related parties, where:

+ the consideration is below market value, and the recipient cannot fully deduct VAT, or

+ the consideration is above market value, and the supplier cannot fully deduct VAT.

5.4 Returnable Packaging

Under Bulgarian law, the taxable base is increased by the value of customary or packing materials or
containers unless they are returnable or if the recipient is not a taxable person. If the packing materials or
containers are returned by the customer, the taxable amount is reduced with the value of returned materials or
containers.

Bulgaria | 83

--- Page 84 ---
6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rate

The standard rate in Bulgaria is 20%.

6.2 Reduced rate

The reduced rate of 9% applies to:
+ accommodation in hotels and other places of accommodation, including sheltered housing;
+ supply of books (including e-books), newspapers, magazines; and

+ baby foods and certain baby products,

Te Exemptions

TL Exemptions with the right of deduction

7.1.1 Intra~Community supplies

To prove a zero rate in the case of intra~Community supplies, the supplier must have a copy of the sales

invoice with the EU VAT number of the customer plus documents proving the transportation to another EU

Member State, if the transport is made by or for the account of the supplier. Where the customer is organizing

the transport, the supplier should either possess the sales invoice, plus one of the two:

+ transport document proving the arrival of the goods in the other EU Member State, or

+ written confirmation by the customer that the goods have been received in the other EU Member State
which should include date and place of arrival, type of goods and their quantity, type and registration
number of the transport vehicle and other.

‘The presumption under Art. 45a of the Regulation 282/201 which was introduced as part of the Quick Fixes,

may be applied by the taxable person.

7.1.2 Export supplies
To prove the zero rate for an export supply, the supplier must have evidence that the goods have left the EU,
including a customs declaration, invoice and transport documents.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse
Bulgaria has not introduced VAT warehouses.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

The storage of excise goods under the deferred payment regime of excise duty is carried out by licensed
warehouse keepers who have obtained a license to operate a tax warehouse for storage.

Excise duties are imposed on alcohol and alcoholic beverages, tobacco products, energy products, and
electricity. Goods are subject to excise duties:

+ when produced within the territory of the country;

+ when introduced into the territory of the country from another member state; or

+ when imported into the territory of the country.

‘The obligation to pay excise duty arises when the respective goods are released for consumption. Goods
intended for export are not subject to excise duty. Additionally, excise duty paid on ethyl alcohol is subject
to reimbursement if the alcohol is used in the production of food products and medicines for which no excise
duty is payable.

7.1.3.3. Bonded warehouse

Itis possible to put the goods in a bonded warehouse. If the goods coming from a third country are directly
placed under a bonded warehouse regime, no customs duties and VAT become due. VAT is due when the

84 | Bulgaria

--- Page 85 ---
goods are released for free circulation. The importer becomes liable for the VAT. The supply of goods within
the bonded warehouse is subject to Bulgarian VAT but exempt with credit (‘zero rate’).

7.2 Exemptions without the right to deduction

Certain hospital and medical services, ambulance services, certain educational and cultural services are VAT
exempt.

Financial and insurance services, management of collective investment funds, postal services, some real
estate supplies, services by religious and charitable organizations are also VAT exempt.

7.3 Option for taxation

‘The option to tax may apply to the following exempt supplies:

+ _ sale of ‘old buildings’. Old buildings are considered those for which 5 years have passed since the date of
issuance of the permit for their use;

+ transfer of title in land or other rights related to land (i.e, right to construct or right to use) or rental of
Jand; and

+ rental of immovable property to individuals for private use;

The supplier may choose to charge 20% VAT on the above-mentioned exempt supplies.

8 Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

8.1.1 General reverse charge ~ non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)

- Supply of goods

If a non-established supplier performs a domestic supply of goods, the reverse charge does not apply. The
non-established supplier will need to register for VAT purposes in Bulgaria and charge Bulgarian VAT. Ifa
non-established supplier performs an installation/assembly supply, the reverse charge applies if the customer
is registered for VAT purposes in Bulgaria.

- Supply of services

If the non-established supplier performs a domestic supply of services to a taxable person established in
Bulgaria, the reverse charge applies. The reverse charge also applies if the customer is a non-taxable legal
person insofar the non-taxable legal person has a Bulgarian VAT identification number. The reverse charge
even applies if a non-established supplier supplies services to a non-established customer (taxable person). In
that case the non-established customer will need to register for VAT purposes in Bulgaria.

The reverse charge does not apply if the non-established supplier is VAT registered in Bulgaria.

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 Directive)

Bulgarian reverse charge rules apply to supplies of scrap, specific supplies of investment gold, seeds, wheat
products and greenhouse gas emission allowances when the recipient is VAT registered in Bulgaria (which
includes foreign persons not established in Bulgaria but possessing a Bulgarian VAT ID).

8.2 Joint and several liability

Under the Bulgarian VAT Act, any registered person who is the recipient in a taxable supply is liable for the
value-added tax (VAT) due and unremitted by another person, provided that the recipient has exercised the
right to deduct credit for input tax related directly or indirectly to the due and unremitted tax.

This liability is enforced when it is proven that the recipient knew or was obligated to know that the tax would
not be remitted. This determination is made by the auditing authority following the procedure established in the
Bulgarian Tax and Social-Insurance Procedure Code. The recipient is presumed to have been obligated to know
if the tax due was not effectively paid as net tax for a tax period by any previous suppliers of the same goods or
services, regardless of whether they are in the same, modified, or processed form, and if the taxable supply is
simulated, circumvents the law, or is significantly priced differently from the market price.

Bulgaria | 85

--- Page 86 ---
Liability for the unremitted tax does not depend on whether the recipient obtained a specific benefit trom the
non-remittance. Preceding suppliers who owe the unremitted tax also incur liability under the same terms.
The liability is enforced first on the direct recipient of the supply where the tax was not remitted. If collection
fails, the liability may be enforced on any subsequent recipients in the order of supplies. These rules apply
similarly to preceding suppliers as well.

9. Recovery of input VAT

The VAT accumulated prior to VAT registration for assets available at the VAT registration date is recoverable
in specific circumstances.

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

As a tule, input VAT related to the business activity is recoverable except in the case of:
+ VAT exempt supplies;
+ free of charge supplies or non-business activities;

+ representation and entertainment; and

+ purchase and import of cars, as well as other related expenses such as fuel, repair and spare parts.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT for a given month results in VAT recoverable, which is offset against VAT payable over the
next 2 months. The remaining VAT recoverable is refunded by the VAT authorities within 30 days after filing
of the VAT return for the second month following the month when input VAT was incurred.

Excess input VAT can be refunded within | month (after submitting the VAT retum) when the taxable person
performs ‘zero rated’ supplies on a regular basis.

9.3 Capital goods - Adjustment periods

Adjustment periods are as follows:

+ movable property- 5 years following the year of first use

+ immovable property - 20 years following the year of first use; and
+ — services if they are accounted for as fixed assets 5 years.

94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

The refund application must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authorities of the
country in which the claimant is established.

Annual minimum limit for claims for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year is BGN 100 (around
EUR 50). Minimum limit for claims made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months is BGN 800
(approximately EUR 400). Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in 1 calendar
year, unless it is a remainder of the year, Maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year. The deadline
for submitting the VAT refund claim is 30 September of the year following the year over which a claim is made.
The tax authorities may require additional information and supporting documents, ¢.g. invoices.

The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within 4 months of receipt of the claim. The
period in which the authorities must make a decision will be extended to 6 months or to 8 months when the
authorities request additional information.

Ifa refund is granted, it will be processed within 10 business days and paid to the bank account provided to
the tax authorities, The deadline for appeals is 14 days following the decision. If this appeal is unsuccessful,
the claimant can initiate proceedings before the national court within 14 days after a receipt of the decision.

9.5 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

Bulgaria requires reciprocity when refunding the VAT to non-EU taxable persons.
A non-EU taxable person may claim a refund if in the period which relates to the refund claim, if the
following conditions are met:

86 | Bulgaria

--- Page 87 ---
+ he does not have its seat and management address, nor a fixed establishment, permanent address or
permanent residency in Bulgaria;

+ he has not carried out supplies with a place of supply in Bulgaria, except supplies of goods and services
regarding which the recipient is liable for the VAT due (where the reverse charge mechanism applies) or
transport services and services, related to international transport; and

+ his country of residence is included in the list of countries which grant a VAT refund to Bulgarian
businesses, approved by the Minister of Finance and the Minister of Foreign Affairs. Countries which
currently fall in the list are: Canada, Israel, Iceland, Moldova, Northern Macedonia, Norway, Serbia,
South Korea, Switzerland, Ukraine and the United Kingdom.

No refund will be given for VAT incurred on supplies without right to VAT credit under the Bulgarian VAT

legislation, for example:

* goods and/or services related to exempt business activity;

+ goods or services intended for supplies effected for no consideration or for activities other than the
economic activity of the taxable person;

» goods or services intended for entertainment and representative purposes; and

* — goods/services in relation to the purchase or import of cars with up to 5 passenger seats (excluding the
driver seat).

The refund application form must be completed in the Bulgarian language.

Non-BU resident claimants are required to appoint a VAT refund agent. The right to act as a VAT refund agent
is granted to traders that meet certain conditions and who are registered as agents with the Bulgarian tax
authorities.

Aclaim must be submitted not later than 30 June of the year following the calendar year in which the right to
VAT refund has occurred.

The minimum limits for claims are:
+ Percalendar year-B GN 50 (approximately EUR 25); and
* Per period of at least 3 months within one calendar year BGN 400 (approximately EUR 200).

The following documents should be submitted to the tax authorities:

+ an application form:

+ a Certificate of status - an original certificate from the foreign revenue authorities that the claimant has
carried out an economic activity during the calendar year for which the VAT refund is claimed. A certified
translation of the certificate into Bulgarian is required;

+ adeclaration by the claimant that the conditions for claiming VAT refund are fulfilled;

+ the original invoices/import documents; and

+ a power of attorney must be submitted together with the claim.

‘The revenue authorities may also require additional information regarding the supplies received by the
claimant, Refund payments must be made within 6 months after the date of submission of the refund claim to
the tax authorities.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

A VAT invoice must contain the following information:

+ — title of the document (invoice);

+ invoice numbering: 10-digit sequential numbers (containing only Arabic numerals);
+ date of issue;

+ supplier’s and recipient’s names and addresses;

+ supplier’s VAT identification number:

Bulgaria | 87

--- Page 88 ---
* customer's VAT identification number in Bulgaria or its BULSTAT number;

» a VAT identification number when the customer is registered in another EU Member State or other
identification number when such is required by the legislation of the state, where the recipient is
established;

+ quantity and type of the goods supplied or nature of the services;

+ date of taxable event or the date on which payment is received;

+ unit price (exclusive of VAT), taxable base and any discounts/rebates, if not included in the unit price;

+ VAT rate, or reference to the legal provision for applying a zero rate or to the grounds for not charging VAT;

* amount of VAT;

* total amount due, if different from the sum of the taxable base and the VAT charged;

+ the circumstances determining the goods as a new vehicle in case of an intra-Community supply of a new
vehicle;

+ incase of self-billing a corresponding reference “self-billing”/ “camocaxrypupane” is required, plus a
reference to art. 113, para. 11 of the VAT Act;

+ incase of simplified triangulation the middleman should mention on the invoice “Article 141 2006/112/
EC”;

+ the indication for the application of an exemption, zero rate or reverse charge. For instance:

+ intra~Community supply: “vatreobshtnostna dostavka”? (in Bulgarian “ssrpeoOmuocrua nocrsaKa”);

+ export supply: “Zero rated export supply” “iznos”/(”nsH00”);

+ B2B service: “Service not subject to Bulgarian VAT according to Article 44 VAT Directive; VAT due
by the recipient of the service according to Article 196 VAT Directive”, “Obratno nachisliavane”
(“oSparto Haunenapane”) and state the grounds for the reverse charge; and

+ an indication of the fact that a margin scheme has been applied as well as an indication of why the margin
scheme is applied, e.g:
+ margin scheme ~ second-hand goods - “rezgim na oblagane na marzha - stoki vtora upotreba’( in

Bulgarian "pexum 1a oGnarane wa mapa - CTOKH Bropa yrlotpe6a”);

* margin scheme - works of art - “rezhim na oblagane na marzha - proizvedenia na izkustvototo"/
(“perkum Ha oGuarane Ha Mapxa - NpOH3BeMeHHA Ha W3KyCTBOTO”):

+ margin scheme - collector’s items and antiques - “rezhim na oblagane na marzha - predmeti za
kolekeii | antikvarni predmeti”/ (” pexxuM Ha oGs1arane Ha Map>ka - MpeaMeTH 3a KONeKUAH H
anraKsapnn npeamera”); and

+ tour operator margin scheme -” oblagane marzha na turoperatora”/(“o6marane Ha Mapaa Ha
ryponeparopa)”.

10.1.1 Storage of invoices

Invoices need to be kept available for 10 years. Invoices may be stored electronically. In this case, (he taxable
person must provide access to the database to the tax authorities.

‘There are no specific requirements for storage, In the course of tax audits, the revenue authorities need to be
able to identify the information included in each document. PDF invoices can be stored electronically as well.
Invoices relating to electronic, broadcasting and telecom services also must be stored for 10 years.

10.1.2 Electronic invoicing

Electronic invoicing is allowed in Bulgaria if the authenticity of origin and the integrity of the content on the
invoices are guaranteed. There is no current obligation the invoices to be issued electronically in Bulgaria.

10.2 Compliance

10.2.1. VAT return period

VAT returns are filed monthly, together with VAT purchases and sales ledgers. As a rule, VAT retums are to be
submitted electronically.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

The VAT return must be filed and the VAT due must be paid by the 14" day following the month to which it
relates, If the due date falls on a public holiday, Saturday, or Sunday, then the due date is the next business
day.

88 | Bulgaria

--- Page 89 ---
10.2.3 European sales listing
Taxable persons carrying out intra~Community supplies of goods, services with a place of supply outside
Bulgaria in another EU Member State, supplies under the call-off stock regime or supplies under the
triangulation simplification must file a European sales listing (VIES) declaration.

The European sales listings, together with the VAT return and the VAT ledgers, must be filed electronically.
European sales listings are filed monthly. There is no threshold, neither for goods nor for services.

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement
There are no national recapitulative statements in Bulgaria.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period
‘The tax assessment period in Bulgaria is 5 years.

10.3 Penalties

Bulgarian tax authorities can impose different types of penalties for not complying with the VAT legislation -
fixed amounts or percentage of the VAT amount due.

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

In case of late registration, the penalty is between BGN 500 (approximately EUR 256) and BGN 5,000
(approximately EUR 2,556),

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

If the VAT or VIES return is not filed on time the penalty is between BGN 500 (approximately EUR 256) and
BGN 10,000 (approximately EUR 5,113).

‘The amount of the penalty would depend on whether the infraction occurs occasionally or regularly. If only
one return is not filed, usually the minimum penalty is imposed (BGN 500).

10.3.3 Default penalties for not charging VAT on time

A penalty of 100% (but not less than BGN 500 (approximately EUR 256)) of the VAT amount may be
imposed for not charging VAT (including for cases when the taxable person never registered for VAT) within
the statutory deadline. The penalty could be up to 200% (not less than BGN 5000 (approximately

EUR 2,556)) of the VAT amount in case of repeated offences.

If the taxpayer has charged the VAT due within 6 months from the end of the month when the tax became
chargeable then the penalty is 5% of the VAT amount, but not less than BGN 200 (approximately EUR 102)
or BGN 400 (approximately EUR 205) in case of repeated offenses.

If the VAT due was charged after the above-named 6 months period but not later than [8 months from the end
of the month when the VAT became chargeable, the penalty is 10% of the VAT amount, but not less than BGN
400 (approximately EUR 205) or BGN 800 (approximately EUR 409) for repeated offenses.

10.3.4 Other penalties
Penalty interest at 10 points above the basic interest rate announced by the Bulgarian National Bank may also
be assessed on the unpaid VAT amount, when there are outstanding VAT liabilities.

In case of reverse charge mechanism if the recipient of the supply did not self-charge VAT, a penalty of 5% of
the VAT may be imposed or 2% if the VAT is reported in the subsequent month.

10.4 Intrastat

The thresholds for Intrastat declarations for 2025 are:
+ for arrivals - BGN 1,700,000 (approximately EUR 869,196); and
* for dispatches - BGN 2,200,000 (approximately EUR 1,124,842).

The thresholds for declaring data on the statistical value of the goods are:

+ for arrivals - BGN 17,000,000 (approximately EUR 8,691,961); andfor dispatches - BGN 36,100,000
(approximately EUR 18,457,637)

‘The threshold for simplified declaration process of a single transactions with a low value is BGN 500

(approximately EUR 256).

The thresholds for dispatches and arrivals are announced annually.

Bulgaria | 89

--- Page 90 ---
10.4.1 Intrastat data to be reported

The information which must be reported for both arrivals and dispatches statistics is:

+ code of the goods (the 8-digit tariff code number);

+ EU Member State of dispatch/arrival;

* state of origin (for arrivals);

+ type of transaction (e.g. purchase/sale, replacement or return of goods, free-of-charge supply, goods for
processing);

+ statistical procedure;

+ terms of supply (e.g. the Incoterms delivery terms EXW, FOB, CIF);

+ type of transport;

+ nationality of the transport vehicle;

+ region of origin/region of consumption;

+ weight of the goods in kilograms;

* quantity in supplementary units;

+ invoice value in BGN; and

+. statistical value in BGN (if the declarer exceeds the threshold for declaring the statistical valuc).

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

There are no additional requirements.

Croatia

‘The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Croatia through the “Zakon 0 porezu na dodanu
vrijednost” (hereafter referred to as the “VAT Law”). The current version of the Croatian VAT Law is
published on the official web site of the Croatian Tax Authorities and can be found, in national language, at
the following link: https://inyurl.com/8 @ &

In Croatia, VAT is referred to as “porez na dodanu vrijednost” or “PDV”.

1. Taxable Persons

Lt Registration & SME scheme for domestic taxable persons

Threshold for entering the VAT system has been raised to EUR 60,000 with an obligation to register for VAT
on the first day following the day the threshold is breached. The threshold amount excludes the supply of
assets used by the taxable person. This threshold is applicable as of 1 January 2025. Small businesses may opt
for taxation voluntarily but must remain registered for at least 2 years.

The tax office responsible for the registration of the taxable person is determined according to the place where
the taxable person has established its business.

The following documents need to be presented to the competent tax office for registration for VAT purposes:

+ extract from the trade register;

* confirmation about the statistical classification of economic activities (NACE);

+ bank account information of the taxable person;

+ contract or declaration on the provider of accounting services (if accounting services are being
outsourced);

* example of imprint of the company’s stamp;

+ filled out P-PDV registration form; and

+ excerpt from rental agreement for the business premises or ownership.

The above mentioned documentation that is required for the registration for VAT purpos
by the legislation, but is required based on our experience. Please note that the Croatian Tax Authorities can
request additional documentation if they deem it necessary.

The forms can be downloaded at the following link:
https:/tinyurl.com/MB. mb 5

1.2 Registration & SME scheme for foreign taxable persons

A taxable person with its registered office, permanent address or usual residence in another Member State is
entitled to apply for the VAT exemption, if the following conditions are met:

+ the annual turnover in the European Union did not exceed EUR 100,000.00;
+ the value of supplies of goods and services carried out in the country did not exceed EUR 60,000.00.

Respective SME scheme is only applicable to small enterprises which are established in the EU. Taxable
persons from third countries are required to register for VAT in Croatia when they plan to perform a supply
that is taxable for VAT purposes in Croatia, irrelevant of the value of the transaction.

Foreign taxable persons that are required to be registered for VAT purposes in Croatia first need to acquire a
personal identification number (PIN or in Croatian language - OIB) which is used as a Croatian tax number.
The VAT number that they will subsequently receive, consists of that same PIN number with prefix HR (for
Croatia),

--- Page 92 ---
The registration application needs to be sent to the competent regional office. For the Zagreb area it is to be
sent to the following address:

Ministry of Finance, Tax Administration, Regional Office Zagreb,
Department for VAT refund to foreign taxable persons

Avenija Dubrovnik 32

10000 Zagreb

Croatia

Foreign laxable persons need to submit the following documents by post, personally or through their tax
representative to the competent tax office:

* an extract from the court or trade register (not older than 6 months) in original and translated into
Croatian language by an authorized court interpreter;

+ acertificate of registration for VAT purposes in the country of establishment (not older than 3 months) in
original and translated into Croatian language by an authorized court interpreter;

+ completed form for obtaining a personal identification number (OTB); and

* completed P-PDV registration form.

In the case where the foreign taxable person authorizes another person to acquire the Croatian tax and VAT
number in its name, a suitable power of attorney must be prepared and filed.

Taxable persons established outside the EU must appoint a fiscal representative, while taxable persons
established in other EU Member States may choose to appoint one (see hereafter under 1.4).

Once all documents are submitted, the tax authorities will issue the VAT number within 8 days starting from
the day of submission.
