13 VAT grouping

Croatian VAT legislation does not include any provisions on VAT grouping, which means that each taxable
person is considered as an independent taxable person for VAT purposes.

14 Fiscal representative

Taxable persons from EU Member States can, but do not need to, appoint a fiscal representative for VAT
registration purposes in Croatia, i.e. direct VAT registration is possible.

Taxable persons from non-EU countries are obliged to appoint a fiscal representative in Croatia when
applying for a VAT number unless the Republic of Croatia has concluded agreements on mutual assistance,
which in their scope resemble those stipulated by Council Directive 2010/24/EU of 16 March 2010,
concerning mutual assistance for the recovery of claims relating to taxes, duties and other measures (OJ L 84,
31.3.2010) and EU Council Regulation No 904/2010 of 7 October 2010 on administrative cooperation and
combating fraud in the field of value added tax (OJ L 268, 12.10.2010).

A fiscal representative is always required, except when the taxable person from a non-EU country has opted
for the special scheme for electronically supplied services. This rule is extended as of 1 July 2021 to all
supplies of services made by taxable persons from non-EU countries to non-taxable persons, as well as to
distance sale of goods.

The fiscal representative must be a taxable person established or with a permanent address or habitual
residence in Croatia. The tax authorities denies a request for fiscal representation or withdraws a previously
issued decision if:

92 | Croatia

--- Page 93 ---
+ a fiscal representative has committed tax-related offences; or
+ a fiscal representative has outstanding debts pertaining to taxes or other public levies.

‘The foreign taxable person can authorize only one fiscal representative at a time. The fiscal representative is
jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT, penalties and interest.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

The Croatian VAT Law implements Article 19 of the EU VAT Directive and reads as follows: “in the event
of a transfer, whether for consideration or not, or as a contribution to a company, of a totality of assets or part
thereof, it shall be considered that no supply of goods has taken place and that the person to whom the goods
are transferred (recipient) is to be treated as the successor of the transferor.”

Both the transferor and the transferee must be taxable persons and the transferee must continue with the
taxable economic activity in order for the transfer of goods to be deemed as “no supply”. If the transferee uses
the assets transferred to him for purposes other than those for which he has the right of deduction, he shall be
liable to charge and pay the VAT (deemed self-supply).

2.2 Supplies free of charge

Croatia applies the principles laid down in Articles 16 and 26 of the EU VAT Directive, With respect to
Article 16, it will not be applied to free provision of samples in reasonable amount to a buyer or future buyers
and awarding gifts of small value carried out by the taxable person as part of his economic activity, under the
condition that those gifts are given occasionally and not to the same persons. Gifts of small value are gifts
with a value under 22 EUR (exchiding VAT).

2.3 Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

It is possible to apply an exemption on the importation of goods into Croatia if it is followed by an intra-
Community supply. However, many conditions must be met in order to apply the exemption, such as a
VAT registration number of the importer or its fiscal representative, the VAT number of the customer in the
subsequent intra~Community supply, a security deposit, etc.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

In principle, the option of postponed accounting on importation following the authorization granted by the
Customs Authority is provided in the Croatian VAT Law. With this license, importers avoid payment of VAT
at the time of importation, Instead, with the deferment system the importer will have to pay the VAT due in its
Croatian VAT return, If the importer is entitled to a full deduction, the VAT can be reclaimed.

Postponed accounting scheme is available for taxable persons registered for VAT purposes, that have the right
to deduct input VAT in full and is available for both domestic and foreign taxable persons.

35 Place of supply
3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

Simplification rules for consignment and call-off stock in Croatia are provided for in the VAT Law.
Simplification regime for call-off stock arrangements are in line with EU VAT Directive.

Croatia | 93

--- Page 94 ---
3.2 Installation supplies

Croatian VAT Law provides that in case the supplied goods arc installed or assembled, with or without a trial
nun by the supplicr or on his behalf, the place of such installation supply is the place where the goods are
installed or assembled. There is no special definition of installation supplies in the VAT Law. If the foreign
supplier is a taxable person that is not established, and without a fixed establishment in Croatia and is not
registered for VAT purposes in Croatia, the VAT is payable by the customer if he is a taxable person or a
non-taxable legal person that is registered for VAT purposes and to which the goods or services are supplied
(reverse charge applies).

33 Distance sales threshold

As of July 2021 the distance selling threshold covers all EU sales of telecommunication, broadcasting and
electronically supplied services as well as distance sales of goods and has been set at EUR 10,000,

Once the threshold is exceeded an EU supplier may either register directly in Croatia or in their country of
establishment via OSS to account for the Croatian VAT due for their supplies made to Croatia. If during the
year the threshold of 10,000 EUR is exceeded, the taxpayer is obliged to submit the e-commerce form within
8 days from the day when he made the delivery that exceeded the threshold.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Simplified triangulation rules are applied in Croatia in accordance with the EU Directive. Taxable persons A,
Band C in the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU Member States (i.e. in Member
States 1, 2 and 3). The goods must be transported directly from A in Member State 1 to C in Member State 3.

The simplification also applies if the middleman (i.e. B in the ABC transaction) is registered for VAT in
Croatia and Croatia is the country of arrival of the goods (Country 3).

If the second acquirer (C in the ABC transaction) is registered for VAT in Croatia, the acquisition of goods
within the EU that was carried out by the middleman (B) in Croatia shall be exempt from VAT under the
following conditions:

+ the middleman is not established nor has a permanent address in Croatia, but is registered for VAT
purposes in another EU Member State;

+ the middleman provides a VAT identification number of another Member State than the Member State of
dispatch and arrival;

+ the acquisition of goods is made for the purpose of the subsequent supply of goods carried out by the
middleman in Croatia;

+ the acquired goods are directly dispatched or transported from another EU Member State in which the
middleman is not registered for VAT purposes to the recipient of the subsequent supply (C);

+ the recipient of the subsequent supply (C) is a taxable person or a non-taxable legal person registered for
VAT purposes in Croatia; and

+ Caccepts liability for payment of the VAT due.

Parties A and B must also report their supplies in the European sales listing. B must refer to the reverse charge
on his invoice.

Respective simplification can only be applied in a classic triangulation situation. If there are more than three
taxable persons involved, triangulation simplification is not applicable.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for acquisition of goods by taxable persons not entitled to deduct input tax and by non-taxable
legal persons is set at EUR 10,000.

94 | Croatia

--- Page 95 ---
3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Croatia has implemented effective use and enjoyment rules both based on Article 59a sub a and sub b of the
EU VAT Directive.

The place of supply of hiring out of movable property and vessels (with or without accommodation) to non-
taxable persons, not established in the EU, is shifted outside the EU if the effective use and enjoyment takes
place outside the EU. The hiring out of other means of transport is exempted from this rule.

Vice versa, the place of supply of the above-mentioned service to non-taxable persons, not established in the
EU, is in Croatia if the effective use and enjoyment takes place in Croatia.

4. Taxable event & chargeability

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer of the goods. The VAT due on services, in principle, becomes chargeable at the
time the services are supplied. The supply of goods, other than the hire of goods for a certain period or the
sale of goods on deferred terms, that involves successive issuance of invoices or successive payments, shall
be regarded as being completed on expiry of the periods to which such invoices or payments relate.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the goods are put at
the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied. The recipient of the advance payment is obliged to
issue an invoice.

4.3 Continuous supplies

Supplies of services for which VAT is payable by the customer pursuant to Article 196 of the EU VAT
Directive and which are supplied continuously over a period of more than one year and which do not give rise
to statements of account or payments during that period, shall be regarded as being completed on expiry of
each calendar year, until the supply of those services comes to an end,

4.4 Cash accounting

A taxable person who is established and has permanent or habitual residence in Croatia and whose supplies of
goods and services in the previous calendar year did not exceed EUR 2,000,000 (VAT excluded) may opt for
the cash accounting scheme for certain local supplies, i.e. may pay the VAT upon collection of receivables and
deduct input VAT when paying invoices to vendors.

5. Taxable amount
5.1 Bad debt relief

Croatian VAT legislation includes bad debt relief provisions.

The Croatian VAT legislation prescribes a detailed procedure for the adjustment of VAT if the VAT base
subsequently changes due to the non-collectability of a receivable (among other things).

In case of non-collectability of receivables, a VAT registered taxable person may adjust or reduce the amount

of VAT if it informs the taxable person to whom the goods or services were supplied on the adjustment of the
tax base and VAT liability.

Croatia | 95

--- Page 96 ---
This taxable person may apply the reduction of the taxable amount within six months from the day the right
to reduction arises, if actions with the care of a good businessman have been performed. The taxable person
shall have evidence that he took actions for the purpose of collecting the due claim, in particular that the

due claim is the subject of enforcement procedure or action is taken against the claim before a court or a
settlement with the debtor who is not an associated party has been reached, or to demonstrate by other means
that it requested collection of claims from his buyer.

Where the taxable person subsequently recovers partially or in whole the claim for the performed supply
after the reduction of the taxable amount, he is obliged to increase the taxable amount for the corresponding
amount.

‘The taxable person that adjusted the taxable amount in the manner described above is obliged to inform the
competent local office of the Tax Administration on the applied adjustment by the deadline for submitting
VAT return for the tax period in which the adjustment was performed.

5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
25% VAT BUR 250
Total to be paid EUR 1,250

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier must pay EUR 250 in its VAT return,
‘The customer can deduct EUR 250 in its VAT return. Therefore, the supplier has paid too much VAT and the
customer has deducted too much. The supplier can adjust its output VAT only upon informing the taxable
person to whom the goods or services were supplied on the adjustment of the tax base and VAT liability.

In addition, the tax base may be reduced due to different types of discount granted to the taxpayer who is not.
entitled to deduct input VAT in the case the supplier receives a statement that the recipient is not entitled to
deduct input VAT and has proof of refund of part of the sale price. Alternatively, this proof of refund is not
required in the case the payment for the supplies made was not effected by the time the tax base reduction is
performed.

At the end, the tax base may also be reduced due to different types of discount granted to the final consumer
in the case the supplier has a proof of a refund of part of the sale price. Alternatively, this proof of refund is
not required in the case the payment for the supplies made was not effected by the time the tax base reduction
is performed.

5.3 Open Market Value

In Croatia, the option for Member States to use the ‘open market value’ as the taxable amount for specific
transactions is implemented in case of supply of goods or services for persons who have family or other close
personal ties to the taxable person and in the event of financial and legal ties, including employer-employee
relationships as well as members of their families, relationships based on membership, management or
ownership, and only if:

a) The fee is lower than the market value and the recipient of the supply does not have the right to full input
tax deduction;

'b) The fee is lower than the market value, and the supplier does not have the right to full input tax deduction

and the supplies are VAT exempt in accordance with the provisions of Article 39 paragraph 1 and Article

40 paragraphs | and 2 of the Law; and/or

The fee is higher than the market value, and the supplier does not have the right to full input tax

deduction.

©)

96 | Croatia

--- Page 97 ---
Market value shall mean the total amount which the buyer or supplier should pay to the goods or service
supplier on the home territory at the moment of goods supply or supply of services under the principle of non-
disruption of market competition. If comparable supply of goods or services cannot be determined, the market
value shall refer to:

+ For goods, the amount which is not lower than the purchase price of those or similar goods or if that price
is unknown, the total amount of determined costs at the moment of supply;

+ For services, the amount which is not lower than the total determined costs of supply of services borne by
the taxable person.

5.4 Returnable packaging

Regarding returnable packaging, package return fee has the status of a ‘transitory’ item and VAT is not
charged to it. Under the relevant rules, the taxable amount shall not include the cost of return packaging for
which special records are kept.

6. VAT rates and exemptions

6.1 Standard rate
The standard rate in Croatia is 25%.

6.2 Reduced rate

Croatia applies two reduced rates, namely 5% and 13%.
The reduced rate of 5% is applicable to supplies of the following goods and services:

+ all types of bread;

+ all types of milk placed on the market under the same name in the liquid state, fresh, pasteurized,
homogenized, condensed (other than sour milk, yoghurt, kefir, chocolate milk and other milk products),
substitute for mother’s milk;

* books with scholarly, scientific, artistic, cultural and educational contents; textbooks for education, for
elementary, secondary and tertiary education, in all material forms;

+ medicines which have the approval of the competent authority for medicines and medical products;

+ medical equipment, aids and other appliances normally intended to alleviate or treat disability, for the
exclusive personal use of the disabled, as prescribed by the Ordinance on orthopaedic and other aids of
the Croatian Health Insurance Institute:

* movie tickets;

+ newspapers issued by a newspaper publisher holding a statute of a media, with daily circulation printed
on paper, with the exception of those that, in their entirety or mainly, contain advertisements or serve for
advertising purposes;

+ scientific periodicals;

* baby food and processed cereal-based food for infants and young children;

* edible oils and fats of plant and animal origin, butter and margarine;

+ supply of live animals: bovine animals, swine, sheep, goats, horses, asses, farmyard poultry, farmed rabbit
and hares;

+ supply of fresh or chilled meat and edible offal from: bovine animals, swine, sheep, goats, horses, asses,
farmyard poultry, farmed rabbit and hares;

+ supply of fresh or chilled sausages or similar meat products, meat offal or blood products;

+ supply of fresh fish;

+ supply of fresh or chilled fish, molluscs or other aquatic invertebrates;

+ supply of fresh or chilled crustaceans: lobster, crawfish, shrimp, prawn;

+ supply of fresh or chilled vegetables, roots and tubers, including dried leguminous vegetables;

+ supply of fresh and dried fruits and nuts;

+ supply of fresh poultry eggs in shell;

Croatia | 97

--- Page 98 ---
+ plants and seeds;
+ fertilizers and pesticides and other agrochemical products; and
+ products predominantly used as animal feed, other than petfood.

The reduced rate of 13% is applicable to supplies of the following goods and services:

* accommodation (including breakfast), half board or full board accommodation services provided in hotels
and similar establishments including holiday accommodation, renting of space in holiday camps or in
places set aside for camping as well as provision of accommodation in nautical tourism vessels;

+ newspapers and magazines issued by a newspaper publisher holding a statute of a media as well for those
who are not obliged to hold a statue of media according to the special regulations, with an exception
of those referred to in the previous Paragraph, and those that, in their entirety or mainly, contain
advertisements or serve for advertising purposes;

+ children’s car seats and babies’ napkins;

+ menstrual supplies;

+ supply of water, other than water marketed in bottles or other packaging, in terms of public water supply
and public drainage under special regulation;

+ supply of electricity to other supplier or end user, including considerations related to that supply;

+ supply of natural gas and supply of district heating services, including consideration related to that
supply;

+ fuel wood, pellets, briquettes and chipped wood;

+ public service of collecting mixed municipal waste, biodegradable municipal waste and separate
collection of waste under special regulation;

+ funerary uns and coffins;

+ services and related copyright of writers, composers and performing artists and bearers of phonogramic
rights who are members of relevant organisations for collective exercising of rights and supplying those
services under special regulations in the field of copyright and related rights with prior authorisation of
government authority competent for intellectual property; and

+ preparation and serving food and desserts in the hospitality facilities and outside the hospitality facilities
under special regulation.

6.3 Zero rates

From October 2022, the supply and installation of solar panels on private residences, living quarters and
public and other buildings used for activities of public interest, and supply and installation of solar panels in
the vicinity of such residences, quarters and buildings are subject to a VAT rate of 0 %.

Ta Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with right to deduction

A zero rate applies to intra-Community supplies and exports. It also applies to the following:

+ services connected to international transport of passengers (except in case of road and railway transport);

+ supply of goods and services under diplomatic and consular arrangements;

+ supply, chartering and repair of qualified vessels and aircraft;

* provisioning and performance of services to meet the direct needs of vessels and aircraft in the
international transport;

+ supply of gold to central banks; and

+ supply of goods in tax warehouses and services performed on such goods.

Intra-Community supplies are evidenced by transportation documentation (i.e. CMR). In addition, when the
customer is arranging transport, a statement containing the elements prescribed by the VAT Law is required to
be obtained from the customer (invoice number / date, delivery note, truck plate numbers). In order to apply a
zero rate for the exportation of goods, valid customs documentation must be obtained.

98 | Croatia

--- Page 99 ---
741.4 Intra-Community supplies

In order to make the applicability of the zero rate in case of intra-Commmunity supplies plausible, the supplier
must have a combination of the following documents; signed CMR (Consignment Note), the bill of lading,
invoice for air freight, or an invoice from the goods carrier, insurance policy in respect of the dispatch or
transport of goods, bank documents confirming payment for the shipment or transport of goods, official
documents issued by a public authority, etc.

In the case the transport is organized by the buyer, it is presumed that an intra-Community supply has been
made if the seller, next to the combination of the above documents, is in possession of a written statement
from the buyer confirming that the goods have been sent or transported by the buyer or by a third party acting
on behalf of the buyer, and indicating the Member State of destination of the goods. Buyer’s written statement
specifies: the date of issue; name or address and address of the buyer, quantity and type of goods, date and
place of arrival of goods, in the case of delivery of means of transport: identification number of means of
transport and the identification of the person receiving the goods to the buyer.

7.1.2 Export supplies

‘The taxpayer proves export has occurred with a customs declaration. When an electronic declaration is used
as proof that export customs clearance has been performed and that it has left the customs territory of the
European Union, an electronic message 1E599 from the automated export system (ECS system) is considered
to be sufficient proof. On the other hand when a paper declaration is used (in the form of a Single Customs
Declaration or in the form of a commercial document) the proof shall be the original copy of that document
certified (record number, signature of the official and stamp) by the competent customs office where the
goods left Croatia.

Croatian VAT legislation allows that taxpayers can also apply export VAT exemptions for the chain
transaction where the taxable person supplies goods that are already customs cleared for export to another
taxable person and these goods will be shipped directly to a buyer outside the EU.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

In Croatia it is possible to put only goods specified in the VAT Law (the same goods which are mentioned in
Annex V of the EU VAT Directive) in a VAT warehouse, The term VAT warehouse (or tax warehouse) means:

+ one or more related fenced premises or closed spaces forming a visibly marked and physically separated
unit such as a building, place in a building, silo or container,

+ in case of goods subject to excise duties: a place defined as an excise warehouse according to the Excise
Duty Law or for goods subject to the special tax on coffee and non-alcoholic beverages, a place defined as
a tax warehouse according to the special tax on coffee and non-alcoholic beverages.

Only a person established or with a habitual abode or residence in Croatia, registered for VAT purposes in
Croatia and holding a permit can keep a VAT warehouse. He is obliged to keep a detailed record on goods
stored as prescribed in the Ordinance on VAT. If the authorized warehouse keeper does not own the goods
stored in the VAT warehouse, the owner of the goods can also keep records on the goods. Goods such

as certain vegetables, nuts, coffee and tea, cereals, grains oils and fats, sugar as well as certain chemical
substances (aluminium, zinc, tin) can be stored im a VAT warehouse. The goods stored in a VAT warehouse
can be subject to common activities such as maintenance, the improvement of the appearance or marketable
quality or preparation for distribution or sale. Activities performed should not result in a change of type of
goods as defined in CN codes in Annex 2 in the VAT Law, except in the case of goods which are subject to
excise duty or coffee and non-alcoholic beverages.

The supply of the Annex 2 goods, as well as services performed on those goods, remains exempt from VAT
for as long as the goods and services are subject to VAT warehouse arrangements. Upon releasing the goods
from the warehouse, VAT must be reverse charged unless the transaction is exempt. The VAT is payable by

the person releasing the goods from the VAT warehouse.

Croatia | 99

--- Page 100 ---
7.1.32. Excise warehouse

The receipt, production, storage or other actions, as well as the dispatch of excise products under excise-
duty suspension arrangements may be performed only in the excise warehouse having the excise warehouse
keeping authorisation issued by the competent customs office to an authorised warehouse keeper. The special
authorisation shall be issued to an authorised warehouse keeper who shall be responsible for fulfilling

all obligations arising from such an authorisation for an authorised warehouse keeper and for an excise
warehouse.

7.1.3.3 Bonded (customs) warehouse

ILis possible to pul goods in a bonded (customs) warehouse in Croatia. The supply of goods which, in
accordance with customs legislation, are intended to be placed into free zones or free warehouses, or are
intended to be placed under customs warehousing arrangements or inward processing arrangements, are
exempt from VAT. The supply of goods and services performed on the goods remains exempt from VAT for as
long as they are covered by these arrangements and not released into free circulation.

Services connected with exempted supplies of bonded goods are also exempt from VAT.

VAT is payable by the taxable person releasing the goods from the warehousing regime into free circulation.

7.2 Exemptions without right to deduction
The following supplies are exempt trom VAT without a right to recover input VAT:

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest
+ education services;

* health and welfare services;

+ supplies by museums, libraries, theatres, orchestras and other cultural services; and
+ supplies by charitable organizations;

7.2.2 Other exemptions

+ financial and insurance services;

+ betting and gambling;

+ rent of residential property;

+ postal services supplied by the Croatian National Post;

+ public radio and television broadcasting;

+ supply of buildings or parts thereof, and of the land on which they stand, other than the supply before the
first occupation or use, that is, the supply where not more than 2 years have passed from the date of the
first occupation or use to the date of the next supply. For the purposes of this law, a building shall mean
any structure fixed to or in the ground;

+ — supply of land other than building land; and

+ private use of personal motor vehicles, yachts, airplanes, etc. when no input VAT was claimed.

7.23 Option for taxation

A taxable person shall have the right to opt for taxation of the transactions regarding the supply of agricultural
land, and supply of used buildings, provided that the customer is a taxable person who has a full right of input
VAT deduction. The right of option in respect to taxation and the right of deduction may be exercised at the
time of supply. In case the VAT option is exercised, VAT is applied via a reverse charge mechanism.

In addition, the taxable person has the right to opt for taxation when granting loans with respect to the
delivered goods or rendered services and under the condition that the financing fee (i.e. interest) is stated
separately on the invoice. In the case the taxable person decides to opt for taxation, he shall be obliged to
inform the Tax Administration in writing before the commencement of such an activity.

8. Liability

81 Reverse charges

In Croatia there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B

100 | Croatia

--- Page 101 ---
services provided by foreign entities to taxable persons and legal entities with a Croatian VAT ID number
established in Croatia. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Articles 44 and
196 EU VAT Directive. There is also the reverse charge for the supply of natural gas through the natural gas
distribution system and electricity through the heating networks, which is supplied by non-resident suppliers
to “taxable dealers” in Croatia (Article 195 EU VAT Directive).

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism - non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)

According to Article 75, Paragraph 2, where a taxable supply of goods or services is carried out by a taxable
person who is not established and has no fixed establishment in Croatia, VAT must be paid by the taxable
person or the non-taxable legal person with a VAT identification number to whom the goods or services are
supplied, The reverse charge applies if the non-established supplier who is not registered for VAT purposes in
Croatia, performs a domestic supply of goods to a taxable person or a non-taxable legal person, established
in Croatia, or even to a non-established taxable person if this person is VAT registered in Croatia. The reverse
charge is also applicable if the non-established supplier supplies services for which the place of supply is in
Croatia to a recipient who is VAT registered in Croatia. The application of the reverse charge is mandatory.

It applies to all supplies of goods and services which are subject to VAT in Croatia. On the invoice, reference
must be made to the application of the reverse charge (prijenos porezne obveze).

The reverse charge even applies if the non-established supplier is registered for VAT in Croatia.
8.1.2 Reverse charge mechanism - specific domestic rules (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

A taxable person registered for VAT purposes in the Republic of Croatia is liable for payment of VAT when he
is the recipient of the following supplies:

+ construction work, including services pertaining to construction, maintenance, reconstruction or removal
of a building, including repair and cleaning services. The same also applies to the supply of staff engaged
in construction-related services;

+ the supply of used material, used material which cannot be re-used in the same state, scrap, industrial and
non-industrial waste, recyclable waste, partially processed waste and goods and services, stipulated in the
Ordinance by the Minister of Finance;

+ the supply of immovable property under Article 40(1)(j\(k) VAT Law, where the supplier has opted for
taxation;

+ the supply of immovable property sold by a judgment debtor in a compulsory sale procedure;

+ the transfer of allowances to emit greenhouse gases in accordance with regulations stipulating the system
for greenhouse gas emission allowances trading; and

+ the supply of concrete steel and iron, as well as products made from concrete steel and iron
(reinforcement structures).

8.2 Joint and several liability

Management board members are in specific cases responsible as tax guarantors for the inability to pay tax
liabilities. As a tax guarantor, respective individuals are responsible for the tax debt if the taxable person (i.e. a
Company) has not paid it within the deadline, and the tax authority called the tax guarantor to pay the tax debt.
However, this does not apply if management board member himself/herself evaded tax or if he/she participated
in tax evasion, in which case the respective individual will have joint and several liability for the assessed tax
liability (i.e. CTA can collect tax debt from both the management board member and the Company).

Besides the above general provisions on the tax liability for management board members, there are specific
rules for specific VAT offences (i.e. participation in fraudulent activities and failure to pay the supplier's
invoice in due time).

8.2.1 Failure to pay the supplier’s invoice in due time

The taxable person to whom the supply of goods or services was made in Croatia, has joint and several
liability for the payment of VAT when he did not pay at least the amount of VAT charged on the invoice of the
supplier.

9. Recovery of input VAT

oA Non-recoverable input VAT

Input VAT is not recoverable in the following cases:

+ on the purchase and lease of pleasure boats, aircrafts, personal cars and other means of personal transport,
including the purchase of accessories for such goods, as well as services supplied in relation thereto,
except when used for the business activity of transporting passengers and goods and a rental activity
thereof, as well as for drivers training, transport of deceased and similar business purposes. For the
purchase or leasing of personal vehicles and other goods and services related to its use, a right to deduct
50% of the input VAT exists on the total purchase value of the vehicle; and

+ on the purchase of goods and services for the needs of entertainment whereby enterlainment shall be
deemed to be expenditure on hosting business contacts, giving gifts to business contacts, payment of
business contacts’ holiday expenses, sport and amusement or entertainment, car, boats, aircrafi, holiday
homes and the like.

As of | January 2025, the deduction of input tax based on a decision of the Tax Administration has been
enabled. If the amount of VAT assessed by the taxpayer or the amount which should have been assessed on
the supplies made, increases based on a decision of the Tax Administration (i.e. following tax audit), then the
taxpayer may issue a corrected invoice with correctly assessed VAT. The recipient of the supply may, for the
resulting difference, exercise the right to deduct input tax in the taxation period in which he or she received
that invoice.

9.2 Excess input VAT

A taxable person can choose among 3 options on how to use its (excess) input VAT:

+ to request a refund where the excess input VAT is refunded within 30 days from the date of submission of
the VAT return or within 90 days from the day an auditing procedure has been started;

+ to transfer it to the subsequent tax period(s) and use it for covering the VAT liability in those tax periods
or to use it to cover liabilities for other taxes; or

+ tocede the right to a refund.

93 Capital goods - adjustment periods

+ Immovable property - 10 years

+ Movable property - 5 years

The annual adjustment is made only with respect to 1/5, or 1/10 of the VAT charged on the capital goods.

‘The input VAT adjustment is not required where the input VAT to be adjusted is less than EUR 135 per capital
good.

There is no adjustment applicable for services.

94 VAT refund to EU taxable persons

Non-resident EU taxable persons can submit VAT refund requests to Croatia via the clectronic portal of their
locai tax authorities, i.e. the VAT Refund system, in accordance with the Council Directive 2008/9/EC.

‘The refund period may not be more than one calendar year or less than 3 consecutive calendar months.
Refund applications may, however, relate to a period of less than 3 months if the period consists of the
remainder of a calendar year.

If the refund application relates to a refund period of less than one calendar year, but not less than 3 months,
the amount of VAT for which a refund is applied for may not be less than EUR 400. If the refund application

102 | Croatia

--- Page 103 ---
relates to a refund period of a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year, the amount of VAT may not
be less than EUR 50.

To obtain a refund for VAT, a taxable person must file a refund application via the electronic system at the
latest by 30 September of the calendar year following the refund period. It is not possible to submit the claim
after the deadline.

Croatian tax authorities may require the applicant to submit by electronic means a copy of the invoice or
importation document with the refund application if there is a reasonable doubt regarding the validity of a
particular refund application. The requested information must be provided within one month of the date on
which the person receives the information request.

When the tax authorities request additional information, it issues a decision on the approval or refusal of the
refund application within 2 months of receiving the requested information or, if it has not received a reply
to its request, within 2 months of the expiry of the deadline (which is one month). The tax authorities issue a
decision on the refund for VAT within 4 months after the date of receipt of the refund application.

‘The time limit for making refunds is 10 working days from the day of decision.

In respect to the appellate procedure, renewal of the procedure, statute of limitations, charging and collecting
of VAT and conducting misdemeanour proceedings, the provisions of the Croatian General Tax Act are
applicable. For example, statute of limitations is set to 6 years (single period) from the statute of limitations
commencement. If some additional information or documentation is requested by the tax authorities, the time
limit for delivery is 8 days, The time limit for appeals is 30 days following the decision.

The VAT will not be refunded if not used for the business purposes of the foreign company. During
the process of refuund, the tax authorities often require further proof such as contracts, order forms and
explanations.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content
The invoice needs to contain the following details:

+ the invoice number and the date of issue;

* the name and surname (title), address, personal identification number or VAT identification number of the
taxable person supplying the goods or services (supplier);

* the name and surname (title), address, personal identification number or VAT identification number of the
taxable person to whom the goods or services were supplied (customer);

* the quantity or usual trade name of the goods supplied or the nature and extent of the services rendered;

+ the date on which the supply of goods or services was made or the date on which the advance payment
was made, in an advance payment invoice, if this date can be determined and if it differs from the date of
issue of invoice;

* the unit price exclusive of VAT or the consideration for goods or services supplied, categorized by VAT rates;

+ discounts or rebates if they are not included in the unit price;

* the VAT rate;

+ the VAT amount payable, categorized by VAT rates, except where a special scheme is applied under which
such detail is not required; and

« the total amount of the consideration and the VAT amount.

A taxable person needs to indicate his personal identification number on the invoice. For transactions within
the EU, the VAT identification number needs to be indicated on the invoice.

A taxable person carrying out supplies of goods or services subject to exemptions shall make a reference on

the invoice to the provisions of the VAT Law stipulating the exemption, to the provisions of simplification for
triangulation, or he shall refer to the corresponding provisions of the EU VAT Directive. In those cases, only the
total consideration amount will be indicated on the invoice, and not the information referred to above (VAT rate,
VAT amount payable categorized by VAT rates, total amount of the consideration and the VAT amount).

Where a taxable person applies the special margin scheme for travel agencies, there must be the mention on
the invoice “Special margin scheme - travel agencies / posebni postupak oporezivanja — putnitke agencije”.

104 | Croatia

--- Page 105 ---
Where a taxable person applies the special VAT cash accounting scheme, the following remark must be
included on the invoice “calculation on basis of the cash accounting scheme / obraéun prema naplacenim
naknadama”.

Where the taxable person applies the special margin scheme for second-hand goods, there must be the
mention on the invoice “Special margin scheme - second-hand goods/posebni postupak oporezivanja marze
rabljena dobra”, “Special margin scheme - works of art / posebni postupak oporezivanja marze — umjetnitka
djela”, “Special margin scheme - collectors’ items or antiques / posebni postupak oporezivanja marie
kolekcionarski ili antikni predmeti”.

When the customer receiving a supply issues the invoice instead of the supplier, the text, “Self-billing /
samoizdavanje ratuna” must be mentioned on the invoice.

When the receiver of goods or services is liable for the payment of the VAT, the text, “transfer of obligation to
pay VAT” or “reverse charge / prijenos porezne obveze” must be mentioned on the supplier’s invoice.

Ifthe taxable person appoints a fiscal representative, the invoice must contain the name and surname (title),
address, personal identification number or VAT identification number of the tax representative.

In the case of a supply of a new means of transport within the EU, the invoice shall also contain several
details about the new means of transport.

10.1.2 Time limits

In case of intra-Community supplies of goods and services, an invoice must be issued no later than on the 15"
day of the month following in which the taxable event occurred for supplies of goods made in accordance
with Article 41 of the VAT Law and for supplies of services for which the customer shall be liable for the
payment of the VAT pursuant to Article 196 of the EU VAT Directive. For domestic supplies, there are no
time limits for issuing invoices. If the changeable event precedes the issuance of the invoice, it must be
included in the tax return in the period in which the taxable event took place.

10.1.3 Currency
As of | January 2023, Croatia introduced EURO, Due to the introduction of the EURO, the relevant

adjustments were made to the legislation, with the amounts in HRK being converted into EURO using the
fixed exchange rate | EUR = 7,5345 HRK.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate
When the factors used to determine the taxable amount of a transaction other than the importation of goods

are expressed in a foreign currency, the exchange rate of the Croatian National Bank on the day the VAT
becomes chargeable must be applied for the conversion into EUR.

A taxable person may apply the exchange rate published by the European Central Bank on the day the VAT
becomes chargeable, Conversion between currencies other than EUR must be made by combining the EUR
exchange rates of each currency. Notification to the tax authorities on the application of the ECB latest
exchange rate is not necessary.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Under Croatian VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed in cases where there is an agreement between the two
parties (for instance, a contract or written agreement).

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

Taxable persons may issue a simplified invoice for supplies of goods or services carried out where the amount
of the invoice is not higher than EUR 100. Such an invoice must contain the following details

* an invoice number and the date of issue,

* name and surname (title), address, personal identification number or VAT identification number of the
taxable person supplying the goods or services (supplier) and an indication of the place where the supply
of the goods or services was carried out (number of points of sale, business premises, shop, and similar);

Croatia | 105

--- Page 106 ---
* name and surname (title), personal identification number or VAT identification number of a taxable person
to whom the goods were supplied or services rendered (the customer);

+ the quantity or usual trade name of the goods supplied or the nature and extent of the services rendered;

* the amount of the consideration including VAT, categorized by VAT rates;

+ the amount of the VAT charged, categorized by VAT rates;

+ where a document or message treated as invoice has been issued, reference to that initial invoice and the
specific details which are being amended.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Taxable persons must store issued and received invoices, documentation on invoice corrections, evidence on
exporis and imports, documents on the basis of which VAT exemptions were used, ledgers and accounting
documentation as well as all other documents used for determination and payment of VAT. These documents
must be retained for the time periods prescribed by the General ‘fax Law, i.e. 10 years, unless other special
regulations prescribe longer periods, such as the Accounting Law which prescribes a minimum of 11 years.
The VAT Law refers to the above-mentioned special regulations and prescribes only time limits for invoices
relating to immovable property which must be stored for a period of 10 years.

Invoices can be stored in any form. The authenticity of origin, integrity of content and readability must be
guaranteed during the storage period. When taxable persons store invoices by electronic means, the tax
authorities must have the right lo access those invoices, and to download and use them by electronic means,
insofar as the tax authorities need them for auditing purposes. The taxable person can decide to store the
copies of invoices outside the territory of the Republic of Croatia under the condition that they stay within
the territory of the EU, If the taxable person decides to store the copies of invoices outside the territory of
the Republic of Croatia, he shall notify the competent local office of the Tax Authoritics thereof and make
the invoices available to the Tax Authorities and other supervision authorities without undue delay upon their
request.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

‘The taxable person can choose any form of electronic invoicing as long as the authenticity of origin, integrity
of content and readability are guaranteed. The aforementioned may be achieved through the electronic data
interchange (EDI), advanced electronic signature and any business control method which creates a reliable
audit trail between an invoice and a supply of goods or services. The use of an electronic invoice must be
accepted by the recipient.

10.2 VAT compl

nce

10.2.1. VAT return period

The standard VAT return petiod (tax period) for VAT purposes in Croatia is a calendar month, If the value of
supplied goods and services, including VAT was less than EUR 110,000 in the previous year, the tax period
is a quarter, except when the taxable person performs intra-Community supplies of goods and services for
which it is obliged to file European sales listings. If performing intra-Community supplies of goods and
services, the taxable person must file monthly VAT returns for the months in which such supplies take place
or for which adjustments for previous supplies must be made, Such taxable persons may also opt for the
monthly submission of returns regardless of the value of transactions. Under certain circumstances, local
taxable persons may file VAT returns on a quarterly basis. Foreign taxable persons, however, must always file
monthly VAT returns regardless of the value of the transactions performed.

10.2.2 Filing of a VAT return and payment
The VAT return should be filed by the 20" day following the month/quarter to which it relates, There are no

differences between resident and non-resident taxable persons in this regard except that non-resident taxable
persons must file the VAT return monthly.

‘A taxable person must pay and declare the VAT before the last day of the month following the end of the tax period.
A taxable person who is established and has permanent or habitual residence in Croatia and whose supplies of
goods and services in the previous calendar year did not exceed EUR 2,000,000 (VAT excluded) may opt for the
cash accounting scheme for certain local supplies, i.e. may pay the VAT upon collection of receivables.

--- Page 107 ---
10.2.3 Ledger of incoming invoices

As of beginning 2019, VAT registered taxpayers are obliged to file a monthly overview of incoming invoices
(ledger of incoming invoices). Filing is carried out electronically, within the deadlines for the filing of the
VAT return,

10.2.4 European sales listing

The deadlines for submission of the European sales listing for goods and services coincide with the filing
periods of the VAT returns. There is no threshold applicable.

10.2.6 National recapitulative statement

‘There are national recapitulative statements in Croatia in case of the supplies mentioned in paragraphs 8.1.2.
(reverse charge mechanism based on article 199 EU VAT Directive).

10.2.7 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period in Croatia is 3 years unless interrupted by certain actions performed by the tax
authorities, This means that in the year 2024, the last open year for VAT assessments is the year 2021.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

There are no specific penalties for failing to register for VAT or for late registration. However, penalties
described below might be applicable in some of these cases.

10.3.2 Default penaities for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

Any taxable person shalt be fined with amounts varying from EUR 260 to EUR 66,360 for a misdemeanour
of failing to submit at all, by the stipulated deadline, or failing to complete all required information on the
VAT return and the European sales listing. The fine varying from EUR 130 to EUR 6,630 will also be levied
to the responsible person of the legal entity.

10.3.3 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

Any taxable person will be fined with amounts varying from EUR 260 to EUR 66,360 for a misdemeanour
of not assessing and not reporting VAT due within the stipulated deadline. The fine varying from EUR 130 to
EUR 6,630 will also be levied to the responsible person of the legal entity. Penalty interest at the rate of 7.5%
on outstanding liability will also be charged. In instances of a voluntary disclosure, the tax authorities may
lower the penalty amount. If VAT is not paid correctly, negligently or with intent and if such misdemeanours
become repetitious (tax evasion), they may result in criminal law consequences.

10.3.4 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

Any taxable person will be fined with amounts varying from EUR 260 to EUR 66,360 for a misdemeanour
of failing to pay, or not paying within the stipulated deadline, the calculated and declared amount of VAT
into the stipulated payment account. The fine varying from EUR 130 to EUR 6,630 will also be levied to the
responsible person of the legal entity. Penalty interest at the current rate of 7.5 % on outstanding liability will
also be charged.

Croatia | 107

--- Page 108 ---
10.4 Intrastat

A taxable person performing intra-Community supplies and acquisitions is obliged to file Intrastat returns.
Responsible authorities are the State Department for Statistics together with the Customs Authorities. The
Intrastat returns must be filed only when certain thresholds are exceeded, whereby the taxable person is liable
to self-report to the Intrastat system. ‘Ihe obligation is also applicable for distance sellers that have not opted
for the OSS.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold
The Intrastat threshold for arrivals for 2025 amounts to EUR 450,000 and for dispatches it is EUR 300,000.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported
The information which needs to be reported for both the arrival and the dispatch statistics is:

+ the 8 digit commodity code of the goods according to Combined Nomenclature;
+ — description of goods;

* the EU Member State of dispatch or destination;

+ transportation conditions (Incoterms, place of supply);

+ the nature of the transaction (¢.g. retum of goods, reparation, etc.);

+ means of transport (e.g, by road or by train);

+ the nature of the transaction (e.g. purchase, sale, retum of goods, reparation etc.);
+ the country of origin (e.g. where the goods are produced, made or processed);

+ the net weight of the goods in kilograms;

+ the quantity in measure units;

* the invoice value of the goods (in HRK);

+ the statistical value (in HRK); and

+ VAT ID of the customer (i.e. for the dispatches).

Cyprus

In Cyprus, the European Union (EU) VAT Directive (“VAT Directive”) has been implemented through the “O
xepi Dépov Tpootiépevng Aciag Néjtoc, Nopos 95(1)/2000 émag tpoxonoriOnke”. This will be referred to
hereinafter as the VAT Law. VAT is referred to in Cyprus as “pos xpootiOépevyc aktac” or “OTLA”.
Throughout the chapter, reference is made to some of the more significant Interpretive Circulars and
Implementing Orders issued by the Cyprus Tax Department, All circulars are in Greek and are referenced by
the number and date of issue. All circulars can be found using the following links:

1. Taxable persons

A taxable person (vzoxeipievo oto @épo mpécwno) is defined as any person or entity that is registered, or

is required to be registered, under the VAT Law. It follows that a taxable person must be carrying out a
business. A business is defined as ‘an economic activity carried out independently and in any place, whatever
the purpose or the results of that activity’. It can be observed that the definition of ‘business’ follows the
definition of a ‘taxable person’ as laid out in Article 9 of the EU VAT Directive. The additional requirement in
the Cyprus VAT Law that the taxable person be registered or required to be registered is perhaps unnecessary.
Its existence does not, however, adversely affect the right of a foreign taxable person to claim back input VAT
from the Cyprus Tax Department if such a right exists, even though that foreign taxable person may not meet
the definition of a ‘taxable person’ under the Cyprus VAT Law.

11 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Registration for VAT purposes is obligatory in the following circumstances listed below. The format of the
VAT registration number depends on whether a group registration exists and when the registration occurred as
per below:

+ VAT numbers issued before 1 January 2024

o Companies: CY10123456X,
o VAT Groups: CY99123456X
o Individuals: CY00123456X,
o Non established entities: CY90123456X
0 Joint ventures CY51234567X

* VAT numbers issued after | January 2024 for all taxable persons: CY60123456X

There is no separate recapitulative statements (“VIES") registration. You only need to apply for activation of
the relevant portal to allow you to submit your VIES returns.

1.1.1 Historic and future tests

A Cyprus establishment of a taxable person must register for VAT if the value of its taxable supplies
(including zero rate) exceeds the registration threshold of EUR 15,600 when applying the historic test or the
future test (explained below).

Where the taxable supplies are below the registration threshold, the taxable person will not be obliged to
charge VAT, and cannot deduct any VAT incurred on the acquisition of goods or services.

The historic test states that registration is required when at the end of each month the taxable supplies for the
last 12 months exceeded the registration threshold. In this case, the registration must be made within 30 days
from the end of the month in which the threshold is passed and registration will be effective on the next day
following this 30-day period, as shown in Example 1 below.

will be exceeded within the next 30 days. In this case, the application for registration must be made within
30 days from the relevant day and registration will be effective as of that same day, as shown in Example 2
below.

1.2 Provision of intra-Community B2B goods and services

Where any person, based in Cyprus but not registered for VAT in Cyprus, makes intra-Community supplies

of goods and/or services, to another person that is VAT registered (business-to-business) in a different EU
‘Member State, then that person is obliged to register for VAT in Cyprus, regardless of the value of the supply.
‘The reason is that the supply must be declared on the VAT Information Exchange System (VIES) (see Chapter
10.2.3). In order to register for VIES, VAT registration is a prerequisite.

1.1.3 Registration for intra-Community supplies

‘There is no separate registration for intra-Community supplies i.e. to use the prefix ‘CY’. This is done
automatically following registration with the Tax Department.

1.1.4 Voluntary registration

Itis possible to register for VAT voluntarily in Cyprus before the relevant threshold is exceeded. In this case,
the person would have to prove to the Tax Department that he is carrying out taxable supplies allowing the
applicant to deduct input VAT. The Cyprus Tax Department is becoming increasingly strict with the type of
evidence they are willing to accept in order to allow voluntary VAT registration. Moreover, they normally
require proof that income will be realized, which makes it difficult for a business in the set-up phase to
register for VAT if no sales contracts have been completed.

1.1.5 — Registration Forms

Registration as a result of the historic or future test is effected with Form TD1101. This form can also be used
for voluntary registration. The applications form for VAT registration can be found online at this web address:
» — hittps://tinyurl.com/3npbuaps

Before submission of the VAT registration form the taxable person needs to obtain a TAX Identification
number. This process is now electronic through the TaxForALL (“TFA’) portal.

Form 1101 is only available in Greek and a hard copy of the application is to be submitted at any of the
District Offices of the Tax Department. The registration form will need to be accompanied by the following
supporting documentation:

+ company certificates (incorporation, directors, secretary, registered office and shareholders);

* memorandum;

«articles of association;

* copy of identity cards or passport of signatory; and

+ evidence demonstrating that the value of taxable supplies have exceeded the registration threshold.

110 | Cyprus

--- Page 111 ---
In the case of voluntary registration, evidence that the taxable person is carrying out taxable supplies is
required (such as a copy of agreement, invoices, details of contracting parties etc).

The VAT registration is activated within 10 business days, on average. The VAT certificate is available only
through the TFA portal.

1.2 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

Small businesses from other EU Member States who apply the scheme for small businesses in the country of
establishment:

+ with an annual turnover in the EU of less than Euro 100,000; and

+ an annual turnover of less than Euro 15,600 in Cyprus

+ may also choose to apply the exemption in Cyprus as of 1 January 2025.

Foreign taxable persons that cannot apply the exemption for small businesses, must register if they
perform taxable transactions for VAT purposes in Cyprus, and the obligation to pay the VAT falls on them.
Furthermore, foreign taxable persons may register for VAT in Cyprus if they can prove that they will be
undertaking taxable supplies in Cyprus. They are also able to register for the purposes of undertaking chain
transactions where they will undertake either an intra-Community supply of goods, or an intra-Community
acquisition of goods, in Cyprus. Taxable persons established in another EU Member State can either effect
a direct registration or appoint a fiscal representative (see Section 1.4 below). Taxable persons established
outside of the EU will normally be required to appoint a fiscal representative, unless a mutual reciprocity
agreement exists between that country and Cyprus, in which case they can effect a direct registration.
Currently such reciprocity agreements exist only with the UK, Israel and Norway. However, even if no
mutual reciprocity agreement exists, it may be possible to effect a direct registration with the deposit of a
bank guarantee. The Cyprus Tax Department has issued Interpretive Circular EE243, dated 01.09.2020,
giving information regarding the registration obligations of a non-established taxable person.

13 VAT grouping
VAT Grouping is possible in Cyprus. Group registration is optional.

+ Financial links exist where one entity controls, either directly or indirectly, the other, or if all entities to be
included in the group are controlled by a third person;

+ Organisational links exist where group members are under common management;

+ Economic links exist where the main economic activities of members are alike, or complementary, or
mutually combined, or mutually beneficial.

Although the VAT Law states that only taxable legal persons incorporated in Cyprus may be part of a VAT
Group, the Tax Department issued on 12.01.2018 Interpretive Circular EE221 which clarifies that other
establishments (such as fixed establishments) carrying out economic activity in Cyprus may form part of a
VAT Group. “Pure” holding companies or companies exclusively involved in exempt supplies with no right
of deduction of input VAT are not allowed to join VAT Groups, contrary to the judgment of the European
Court of Justice in Commission vs Ireland (C-85/11 [2013]). Members of the group are jointly and severally
liable for any VAT, interest and penalties that may be imposed. The supplies of goods or services between the
members of a VAT group are ignored for VAT purposes.

Where two or more companies effect a VAT group registration, their existing VAT numbers are cancelled,
and the VAT group obtains a new common VAT number under the representative member’s name. The
representative member is responsible for filing a single VAT return on behalf of the group and paying any
VAT due. This will create a problem for validation purposes given that only the name of the representative
member appears on the EC VIES VAT number validation website. It will thus not be possible to validate
through the website the VAT registration number of the other members of the VAT group, given that their
names do not appear on the validation confirmation. However, it is possible to show documentation that these
companies form part of the VAT group.

Cyprus | 111

--- Page 112 ---
1.4 Fiscal representative

In Cyprus, a fiscal representative is referred to as a VAT representative. The right to appoint a VAT
representative is available to taxable persons or persons carrying out taxable supplies, not established in
Cyprus, and which do not have a fixed establishment in Cyprus.

A physical person residing, or a legal person incorporated, in another EU Member State does not have to
appoint a VAT representative in Cyprus. Similarly, no obligation to appoint a VAT representative arises for
persons in a country with which Cyprus has entered into a mutual reciprocity agreement that covers tax
registrations (currently such agreements exist only with Israel and Norway). In addition, and even though
no mutual reciprocity agreement exists with the UK, the Cyprus Department of Taxation also accepts direct
registration under the Brexit arrangements for UK taxable persons. It is possible for such taxable persons to
register directly with the Cyprus Tax Department.

Anon-EU company is normally obliged to appoint a VAT representative, however the Commissioner of
Taxation may instead register the company directly and accept a bank guarantee.

Appointing a VAT representative confers no tax advantages when compared to registering with the Tax
Department directly.

A VAT representative is appointed by virtue of Article 37 of the VAT Law, as well as of Regulation 10 of Reg.
(K) K.A.11.314/01 and the Decree based on Regulation 10,

The form for appointing a VAT representative is Form VAT 104, which can be accessed through the following
link: https://tinyurl.com/3w9uj5bz

When a VAT representative is appointed, the Commissioner of Taxation must be notified within 30 days. The
name of the VAT representative together with the name of the taxable person being represented must appear
in the VAT Register. The VAT representative is jointly and severally liable to the ‘fax Department for any
transactions undertaken by the company that may give rise to a VAT liability, although the law limits his/ her
liability to cases of fraud, misconduct or negligence by the VAT representative.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

Cyprus has implemented the option afforded in Articles 19 and 29 of the VAT Directive to consider the
transfer of a business as a going concern to be outside the scope of VAT. There are certain conditions that
must be met:

+ all, or an autonomous part, of a business (assets, stocks, goodwill) is transferred;

+ the transferor is a taxable person;

+ the transferee is or becomes a taxable person; and

the transferee continues, without a substantial discontinuation, to carry out a business of the same nature.

In this case, the transferor must not charge any VAT and the transferee is not allowed to deduct any VAT
as input tax, even if the transferor has charged VAT by mistake. If, as a result of the transfer, the transferor
terminates his taxable supplies, he must notify the Tax Department within 60 days by submitting an
“Application for Cancellation of Registration” (Form VAT 204), The form can be accessed through the
following link: htfps:/tinyurl.com/4bswnad9

Furthermore, if the transferee becomes liable to be registered, then he must notify the Tax Department within
30 days by submitting Forms TD2001 and TD1101. See Section 1.1.5 for a link to the forms. The transferee
is by default liable to register if the transferor was registered just before the transfer. If both the transferor and
the transferee wish, they may submit an “Application for the Transfer of VAT Rights and Obligations” (Form
VAT 103), upon which, if approved, the transferor must hand over his accounting books and records to the

112 | Cyprus

--- Page 113 ---
transferee and the transferee will take up all previous rights and obligations of the transferor. The form can be
accessed through the following link: hitps://inyurl.com/2ny9bcha

2.2 Supplies free of charge
Cyprus generally applies the principles taid down in Articles 16 and 26 of the EU VAT Directive.

In relation to Article 16, the free supply of ‘gifts of small value’ is not taxed in Cyprus if the purchase price
per item is less than EUR 17,09 (excluding VAT).

For the free supply of ‘samples’, the Cyprus law still makes reference to provisions that have already been
judged as incorrect by the CJEU (for example see EMI (C-581/08)). Specifically, as per the VAT Law,
samples can be provided for free without there being a deemed supply of goods, however, this will not apply
in all cases. The law specifically states that where:

(a) the same person gives a number of samples to another person (cither all on one occasion or on several
occasions), and

(b) these samples are identical or do not differ substantially from each other:

then a deemed supply of goods will apply to all but the first sample given.

There is also a specific provision regarding deemed supplies relating to immovable property. Based on this
provision in the VAT Law, where a related person is believed to have used a property belonging to a company,
the Tax Department often assesses a deemed supply of a good, resulting in conflict especially over the
interpretation of evidence, such as electricity consumption.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

‘The Cyprus VAT Law applies the exemption for the intra-Community supply which follows an importation.

For this exemption to be applied the following conditions must be satisfied:

+ the intra-Community supply is carried out by a taxable person and is subject to the zero rate as per Article
25 of the Cyprus VAT Law;

* the imported goods are the ones which will be the subject of the intra-Community supply; and

+ the Commissioner is satisfied that the taxable person intends to move the goods to another EU Member
State, and the taxable person is importing the goods for the onward intra-Community supply.

It is at the discretion of the Commissioner when granting this exemption to request a bank guarantee for an
amount which will not exceed the VAT amount that would have been due on the importation of the goods.
The exemption applies when the goods are moved to the other EU Member State within 1 month from
their arrival in Cyprus (or such other period as may be agreed with the Commissioner). In practise, such a
guarantee is rarely requested.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Inport VAT must be (pre)paid upon the importation for any goods from places outside of the EU, regardless
of whether it is undertaken:

+ bya taxable or a private person;

+ for business or private purposes; or

+ with or without consideration.

VAT chargeable on importation is paid to the customs authorities as if it were a customs duty; normal rules
then apply for the right of deduction for taxable persons.

There are certain exceptions to the payment of VAT on importation. The main exceptions are the following:

+ for gas and electrical energy imported through a specific distribution system. For these kinds of
importations self-billing will apply;

+ for importation of non-commercial aircraft to be used for business purposes by a taxable person (a bank
guarantee may be requested by the Commissioner). Self-billing will also apply;

Cyprus | 113

--- Page 114 ---
+ in certain cases, deferral of 30 days of the VAT payable may be granted by the Commissioner, upon
request by the taxable person and written approval, and upon receipt by the Commissioner of a bank
guarantee for the amount of VAT due. This exception emanates not from the VAT Laws but from the
Customs legislation (Customs Regulation K.A.11. 329/2005, under Article 40 of the Customs Code Law
94(1)/2004);

+ for personal belongings:

+ following migration to Cyprus:
+ onretum to Cypmus following studies; or
+ inherited through procedures of a will;

+ for certain goods reccived by charitable organizations;

+ for commercial samples; and

+ for wedding gifts on return to Cyprus following an overseas wedding.

3. Place of supply

3.1. Consignment and call-off stock

Consignment stocks (axo0épace. xov BptoKovtat vxd my evivn tov axootodMEa) are stocks transferred to
Cyprus by a supplier established in another EU Member State, which remain under his control, and from
which supplies will be made by him or on his behalf, within Cyprus.

‘There is no simplification for consignment stock under the VAT Law, Where a non-established supplier
transfers consignment stock to a Cyprus customer, i.e. to be placed in the consignee’s care, the supplier will
have to register in Cyprus in order to undertake an acquisition of own stock. The ensuing sale of goods to the
customer under the consignment stock agreement will constitute a domestic sale of goods for the supplier,
subject to VAT at the applicable VAT rate,

Where both the supplier and the customer are based in Cyprus, und goods are held on a consignment basis,
the goods are deemed to be supplied when the goods are taken out of the stock under consignment and the
right to dispose of the goods as owner is transferred to the customer. An automatic supply is deemed to
take place if 12 months have passed from the date that the goods were transferred under consignment to the
consignee.

Call-off stocks (amo9épara asgadetas) are stocks used for and paid by one customer, for his own needs.
This applies where the customer will use the stock for his own purposes or will use the stock to effect further
supplies to his own customers.

From | January 2020, the quick-fix for call-off stock applies in Cyprus. In this case, the transfer of the
goods by the non-established supplier to the Cyprus-established customer is no longer a deemed supply of
goods (transfer of own goods), The transfer will be recorded in the VIES of the supplier as a call-off stock
movement, When the actual call-off occurs by the customer, the supplier will then have made the intra-
Community supply, and the customer the intra-Community acquisition, in the normal way.

Both the supplier and the customer will have to maintain a register detailing any goods transferred under the
call-off stock simplification.

Where the conditions are not met for the call-off stock simplification, or a period of 12 months passes from
the arrival of the goods in Cyprus without the customer calling-off the stock, then to the extent of the stock
not called off by the customer, the supplier will have to VAT register in Cyprus for the intra-Community
acquisition as a result of the movement of his own goods to Cyprus.

Where the supplier and the customer are based in Cyprus, and goods are held on a call-off basis, the goods
are deemed to be supplied when the goods are taken out of the stock under call-off and the legal title is
transferred to the customer. An automatic supply is deemed to take place if 12 months have passed from the
date that the goods were transferred to the customer.

114 | Cyprus

--- Page 115 ---
The Cyprus Tax Department has issued Informative Leaflet 17A dated January 2020 on the treatment of call-
off stock. No simplification applies in case of imports. If the importer/consignee is registered, he may recover
VAT charged on importation as input tax.

3.2 Installation supplies

Article 36 of the EU VAT Directive is not implemented word-for-word in the Cypriot legislation. There is one
reference to Article 36 in the Cypriot VAT Law, making reference to an intra-Community supply of goods
with installation / assembly.

Cyprus applies an extension of the reverse charge to the supply of goods with installation, as long as the
supplier is VAT registered in another EU Member State, and is neither established nor VAT registered in
Cyprus, and the customer is a taxable person, registered for VAT in Cyprus. In such a case, the customer
will apply the reverse charge mechanism. The invoice from the supplier should make reference to ‘Reverse
Charge’ and the supplier should notify the Cyprus Tax Department in writing.

Construction work is considered to be a supply of a service where a building is erected and the supply
is without the supply of the land on which the building stands (e.g. the land is owned by the customer).
Domestic reverse charge may apply to construction works (sce Section 8.1.2.1 below).

33 Distance sales threshold
The distance selling threshold covers all European sales of TBE services and distance sales of goods and has
been set at EUR 10,000.

Once the threshold is exceeded, the supplier may either register directly in Cyprus or in their country of
establishment via OSS to account for Cyprus VAT for their distance sales to Cyprus.

A taxable person of another EU Member State may opt for voluntary registration in Cyprus for its distance
sales before the threshold is exceeded. The Cyprus Tax Department must be notified at least 30 days before
initiating distance sales with evidence of notification of the authorities in the home EU Member State

The option is applicable for the subsequent 2 calendar years from the date of exercising the option.

The Tax Department has created a new microsite dedicated to e-commerce and OSS schemes. It can be
accessed through the following link: hrtps://tinyurl.com/3e5ke78w

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

The Tax Department has issued the following circulars, which are relevant: EE 88 (04.06.2004) and EE 88A.
(27.08.2004), The simplified triangulation rules cannot be applied if the middleman (B) is either registered
or established in the country of dispatch or that of arrival of the goods. More specifically, under the current
wording of the Cypriot VAT Law, B cannot have a VAT registration number in the country of dispatch,
although this is contrary to the judgment in Hans Biihler, (ECJ C-580/16).

3.5 Acquisition threshold

Where a Cyprus established person is not liable to be registered for Cyprus VAT purposes (for example
non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons exclusively engaged in exempt activities (such as universities and
flat-rate farmers)), that person becomes liable to register when he acquires goods from VAT registered persons
in other EU Member States which exceed the threshold of EUR 10,251.61 within a calendar year and those
goods are shipped to Cypnus, from another EU Member State. An obligation to register also arises if there are
reasonable grounds to believe that the value of intra~-Community acquisitions that person will be making in
the following 30 days alone, will surpass the registration threshold.

The Cypriot Tax Department has issued Information Leaflet 3A (in Greek) for further guidance with regards
to an obligation for VAT registration due to intra-Community acquisitions.

Cyprus | 115

--- Page 116 ---
3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

Use and enjoyment provisions have been implemenied in the VAT Law.
The general use and enjoyment rule as per the Cyprus legislation provides that:

+ Art. 59a(a); where the supply of relevant services would otherwise be regarded as made in Cyprus, but
the effective use and enjoyment of those services is outside the Community, the supply is deemed to be
outside of the Community, and

+ Art. 59a(b): where the supply of relevant services would otherwise be regarded as made outside the
Community, but the effective use and enjoyment of the services is in Cyprus, the place of supply is
deemed to be Cyprus.

The special provisions for effective use and enjoyment as per (a) and (b) above, apply to:
hiring of means of transport, both short-term and long-term, B2C and B2B;

+ _ short-term hiring of means of transport, B2B and B2C;

+ hiring of movable goods, other than means of transport, B2B and B2C;

+ telecommunications, radio and television broadcasting, B2B and B2C;

+ transport of goods services, other than intra-Community transport of goods, B2B; and

+ electronically supplied services, B2B.

The special provisions for effective use and enjoyment as per (b) above, only apply to: electronically supplied
services to non-taxable persons (B2C).

provisions:
