4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes, in principle, chargeable at the time the goods
or services are supplied. This is known as the ‘basic’ tax point. For goods, this is the date when they are sent
or received by the customer, or the date the goods are placed at the customer's disposal. For services, this is
the date the service is supplied.

116 | Cyprus

--- Page 117 ---
If an invoice is issued or payment is received before the ‘basic’ tax point, then an ‘actual’ tax point is created
and this is then considered when VAT becomes chargeable. In addition, where an invoice is issued within 14
days after the “basic” tax point, then the date of the invoice constitutes an ‘actual’ tax point, thus shifting the
chargeability of the VAT to the date of the invoice.

The VAT on the purchase of a service, which falls under the main rules of Articles 44 and 196 of the EU VAT
Directive, is nevertheless due at the time the service is supplied.

42 Advance payments

As explained above, VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer
or the service is completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received, before the
goods are put at the disposal of the acquirer or the services are supplied. To the extent that any payment is
received in advance, an ‘actual’ tax point arises

4.3 Continuous supplies

Continuous supplies of goods over a period of more than I calendar month which are dispatched or transported
from the Cyprus to another EU Member State and which are supplied or transferred VAT exempt, shall be
regarded as being completed on expiry of each calendar month until such time as the supply comes to an end

Services ongoing over a period of more than | year, that do not give rise to statements of account or payments
in that period and for which the customer is liable to account for the VAT on the basis of article 196 EU VAT
Directive, are regarded as being supplied on the expiry of each full calendar year up to the time the provision
of the services ceases.

44 Cash accounting

‘Taxable persons have the right to opt to apply a cash accounting scheme for the payment of the VAT, if the

following conditions apply:

+ the value of their transactions during the last 12 months does not exceed EUR 25,000;

+ they have submitted all the VAT returns within the statutory deadlines, and settled any amounts shown as
payable therein;

+ they have settled any VAT imposed through assessments;

+ they have not been convicted of any VAT offence.

The scheme cannot be used for certain transactions.

Where the scheme applies, output VAT is chargeable when the payment is received, and input VAT is
chargeable when the payment is made, The words ‘cash accounting scheme’ must be stated on the invoice.
The taxable person must inform the Commissioner in writing when applying the scheme.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

In Cyprus, bad-debt relief is available to taxable persons, provided that they can substantiate to the tax
authorities that the amount not yet paid by the customer became a bad debt within the relevant period, as
prescribed by the law.

When claiming bad debt relief, the following conditions must be met:

+ the taxable person has supplied goods or services and has accounted for and paid the relevant output VAT
on that transaction;

+ the amount has been written-off in the income statement of the accounting books of the taxable person, as
a bad debt;

+ aperiod of 12 months (beginning by reference to the chargeable event) must have elapsed;

Cyprus | 117

--- Page 118 ---
+ the claim for bad debt relief must be made within 4 years from the later of:
+ the date when the supply was due and payable to the supplier; or
+ the dale the chargeable event had taken place;

+ the taxable person must demonstrate that he has taken all the necessary measures to collect the amount
due; and

+ ifthe customer is a taxable person, the supplier must notify the customer in writing of its making the
claim within a 7 day period from the date the claim is made. A copy of the notification must be kept in
the records of the supplier.

The claim is via the quarterly VAT return, shown as an increase in the input VAT claimed for that period.
The Cyprus Tax Department has issued Information Leaflet 14 dated January 2002 on the subject of bad
debt relief, as well as Interpretive Circular 234 dated 13 June 2019. The Leaflet can be accessed through the
following link: https://tinyurl.com/2teya2bs

Interpretive Circular 234 can be accessed through the following link: hitps://tinyurl.com/Sjeweb78

52 Cash discounts

The Cyprus VAT legislation provides that in cases where the supplier offers a discount for prompt settlement,
the taxable amount shall be the amount after the discount is applied, regardless of whether the payment is
made within the said time limits or not.

Example:

Supply of goods for €1000 pl us VAT with a 2% cash discount if due amount paid within 14 days

Net Value: € 1,000

Discount @ 2% (€ 20)

Net amount after discount: € 980

VAT @ 19% on €980 € 186.20

Gross amount € 1,186.20 [€1,000 + €186.20]

Regardless of whether payment is made within the 14 days and the discount is granted, the taxable basis and
relevant VAT amount will always be those amounts after the discount is applied. Should the payment be made
within the 14 days, a credit note for the correction of the € 20 discount value will be issued and which will not
impact the VAT.

5.3 Open Market Value

With regard to the options afforded in Article 80 of the EU VAT Directive to implement the open market
value in transactions between related parties, Cyprus has only implemented the provision in Article 80(1)
(a). This allows the Cyprus Tax Department to implement the open market value only in the case where the
consideration is lower than the open market value, and the recipient of the supply does not have a full right
of deduction. The Cyprus Tax Department can do this only within a three-year period from the date of the
supply. The provision is found in paragraph 1 of the Fourth Schedule of the VAT Law. Therein, there is also
the definition of what constitutes a related party for the purposes of that provision

54 Returnable packaging

Cyprus has not implemented any of the options afforded in Article 92 of the EU VAT Directive with regards
to the cost of returnable packing materials. There are thus no relevant special provisions in place.

118 | Cyprus

--- Page 119 ---

6.2 Reduced rate
Cyprus applies two reduced rates as noted below.

6.2.1 Reduced rate of 5%

‘The reduced rate of 5% can be found in the Fifth Schedule of the VAT Law and applies mainly to:

«various types of fertilisers and pesticides;

+ the supply of food and beverages when sold as take-away or with delivery, except soft-drinks and
alcoholic beverages;

+ construction, erection or supply of housing used as a permanent residence in Cyprus before first
occupation. There are criteria regarding the size and value of the property as well as the person who may
apply for the reduced rate. The Tax Department has issued the following interpretive circulars on this
topic: EE 155 (07.10.2011), EE 158 (14.1).2011), EE 167 (12.06.2012), EE 168 (10.07.2012), EE 210
(06.02.2017), EE 212 (22.03.2017) and EE 11/2023 (28.11.2023);

+ renovation, extension and repairing of private dwellings, excluding materials which account for more than
50% of the value of the services supplied. The Tax Department has issued the following circulars on this
topic: EE 118 (18.10.2007), EE 119 (19.11.2007), EE 132 (23.01.2009), EE 192 (14.06.2015) and EE 199
(29.12.2015);

+ admission to theatrical performances, circuses, cinemas, cultural and sporting events;

+ use of sporting facilities. The Tax Department has issued EE 231 (21.02.2019) on this topic;

+ performances by performing artists, services of composers, artists and writers;

+ supply of gas in cylinders. The Tax Department has issued the following circular on this topic: EE 196
(21.10.2015);

+ children’s car seats when adapted for use in land bound vehicles for persons up to 12 years of age;

+ works of art wholly executed by the artist.

The reduced rate of 9% can be found in the Twelfih Schedule of the VAT Law and applies mainly to:

6.3.2 Super reduced rate

From 21 July 2023, Cyprus applicd for the first time a super-reduced rate of 3%. The proposal was to have

it in place for a limited time but the expiry date has been extended several times. Some of the goods and

services captured by the 3% were previously taxed at the 5% rate. The list of goods and services taxed under

the 3% rate can be found in the Fifteenth Schedule of the VAT Law and include:

+ books, magazines and newspapers, both in hard copy or digitally provided;

+ specific medical equipment;

+ street cleaning, waste collection and waste treatment services but not if supplied by local authorities or
government departments;

* wastewater disposal and treatment and emptying of septic and industrial tanks; and

+ admission to the first performance of theatrical, musical, dance or classical productions.

7. Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with the right of deduction

The VAT exemption with credit (referred to as a ‘zero rate’ in Cyprus) applies to the following supplies of

goods and services:

+ intra~Community supply of goods (B2B);

* services relating to imports of goods;

* — exports of goods;

+ services in the framework of inward processing on behalf of principals resident outside the EU;

+ certain supplies of, services and ancillary services relating to, sea vessels and aircraft, with the exception
of vessels for inshore fishing with foodstuffs;

+ — supply of gold to the Central Bank of Cyprus;

+ supply of goods to non-profit organizations which subsequently export the goods for humanitarian,
educational or charitable reasons;

+ transport of goods to and from Madeira and the Azores;

* services of intermediaries relating to transactions carried out outside the EU;

* goods under customs control or VAT warehousing arrangements;

* services to goods under customs control;

+ supply of a travel package under TOMS which is enjoyed outside of the EU; and

+ any supplies of goods for the provisioning of commercial sea vessels and warships.

7.1.1 Intracommunity supplies

Given that Cyprus is an island, proving a zero-rated supply (export or intra-CCommunity supply) is normally
not a problematic issue. The following combination of documents would suffice for proving the transaction:
+ original VAT invoic
* transport documentation (bill of lading / CMR);

* purchase order;

71.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Cyprus implemented Article 57 of the EU VAT Directive, with regard to VAT warehousing regime, through
Articles 13A-13E of the VAT Law.

Goods that can be placed in a VAT warehouse include:

* — products listed in Annex V of the EU VAT Directive;

+ all imported goods for which the relevant VAT has been paid or suspended; and

+ goods that are subject to special excise duties for which the relevant duty has been paid or suspended.

A taxable person not established in Cyprus can also place goods in a VAT warehouse. Where required, a
taxable person can register or appoint a VAT representative (see Section 1.4) to store goods in, or remove
goods from, the warehouse. If certain conditions are met, a supply of goods that are intended to be placed
within a VAT warehouse is not subject to VAT and thus no VAT registration is required. The customer must
provide the supplier with a certificate that he intends to place the goods in a VAT warehouse. This must be
done no later than the time of acquisition or supply, and the certificates should be kept in the records of the
taxable person.

The VAT warehouse keeper must record all receipts of goods into the warehouse and maintain a detailed
record of all stock kept in the warehouse. There are detailed requirements relating to the movement of the
stock. The services of the warehouse keeper relating to safeguarding goods, as well as any maintenance or
repackaging services on those goods, are zero rated.

VAT becomes chargeable at the moment when the goods are released from the VAT warehouse regime.
Certain releases are not subject to VAT, such as, exports or intra-Community supplies, or the temporary
removal for the purpose of repair, processing or treatment. In such instances, prior authorisation is required

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse
See explanation of tax warehouse in Section 7.1.3.3 below.
7.1.3.3 Bonded warehouse

Itis possible to store goods coming from outside the EU in a bonded warehouse before import occurs.

All goods coming from a third country of third territory can be directly placed under a bonded warehouse
regime, with suspension of customs duties and VAT, When the goods are released from the warehouse for free
circulation, duties and VAT become due. The importer who declares the goods into free circulation becomes
responsible for the payment of the VAT.

In addition, the intra-Community acquisition of excise goods (alcohol, tobacco, energy products) can be
placed in a tax warehouse with suspension of excise duties and VAT.

The release of goods from a bonded or tax warehouse regime does not constitute an importation if the goods
are subsequently placed under another customs regime.

Cypmis | 121

--- Page 122 ---
The sale of goods while still in bond is exempt from VAT, Evidence that the sales took place in the bonded
warehouse should be kept in files (i.e. list of the transactions from the warehouse keeper, customs documents,
the agreements, the invoices, etc.) Certain services related to bonded goods are also exempt from VAT.

7.2. Exemptions without the right to deduction

Exemptions without the right of deduction are supplies which are generally in the public interest (mirroring
Article 132 of the VAT Directive), financial services (mirroring mainly Article 135 of the VAT Directive) and
supplies and lease of immovable property. However, a right of deduction exists for most of financial services
where the recipient of the service is established outside of the EU, in accordance with Article 169(c) of the
BU VAT Directive.

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Exemptions without the right of deduction are mainly provided for the following supplies (the list is not

exhaustive but covers the main supplies):

+ services of the Postal Service;

+ certain hospital services, including medical, paramedical and services of registered doctors which are not
of a cosmetic nature;

+ ambulance services;

+ supply of human organs, blood and human milk;

+ services relating to the protection of children;

+ education offered by registered public or private schools, universities, music schools, ballet schools or
examination centres, including private tuition;

* — services by non-profit organizations to their members relating to cultural, religious, union, philosophical
or charitable purposes, provided this does not result in any distortion of competition;

+ cultural services (e.g. libraries, museums, theatres) by non-profit organizations provided this does not
result in any distortion of competition; and

+ the transactions of the Cyprus Broadcasting Corporation except for those of a business nature.

7.22 Other exemptions

Other exemptions include services of insurance, financial and management of mutual funds, With regards

to the latter, the Tax Department has issued the following interpretive circulars: FE 14 (27.11.1996), EE 72
(19.03.2002), EE 92 (12.08.2004), EE 124 (22.08.2008), BE 143 (19.11.2010), EE 197 (25.10.2015) and EE
203 (16.05.2016).

7.2.3 Letting of immovable property and the option for non-taxation (opt-out)

Cyprus applies Article 135(1)(I) of the EU VAT Directive, regarding exempting the leasing and letting of
immovable property,

However, Cyprus VAT legislation has made use of the exception provided in Article 135(2) of the EU VAT
Directive in offering an option to tax the leasing and letting of immovable property. All such leases are
subject to VAT by default where certain conditions are met, and affords the landlord with an option not to tax
(ice. to opt-out) which binds the landlord for the remainder of his/her ownership. The conditions include that
the property is for commercial use and that the tenant does not have exempt VAT supplies, without a right of
deduction, that constitute more than 10% of the gross turnover of the business. Old leasing agreements that
roll over for new periods may not come within the scope of the new law, unless substantial amendments to
their terms are made.

‘Therefore, the rental of immovable property would be exempt in the following cases:

+ The property at hand is a residential property to be used for residence purposes of the tenant;

+ The rental of immovable property, other than residential, when the tenant engages in exempt activities
without the right to deduction in excess of 10% of its gross turnover;

+ The rental of immovable property, other than residential, where the owner has opted for exemption of the
said property.

122 | Cyprus

--- Page 123 ---
7.2.4 Immovable property

Article 12(1)(b) of the EU VAT Directive is implemented for the sale of building land, which encompasses

all land sold by a taxable person, except agricultural land and land that is in planning zones that do not permit
development (such as environmentally or archeologically protected zones).

From 11 November 2022, the definition of what constitutes “new property’ was amended, without any
transitional arrangements, removing the previous notion of ‘first use’. Instead property is subject to VAT for
any supply made within a 5-year period from the date that the property was first available for use. The only
exception is where the property was actually used for a period of at least 24 months, by a person that is not
related to the owner. ‘Actual use’ is further defined as the use on a systematic basis.

As of I January 2019, long-term leases are considered to be a supply of goods, and subject to VAT if they are
sold before first occupancy (i.e. only applies to new developments sold under long-term leases). A long-term
lease is defined as a lease running for at least 15 years, and which has been deposited with the Department
of Land Registry. The Tax Department has issued interpretive circular EE 229 on 27 December 2018 on this
topic.

73 Option for taxation

Cyprus has not made use of any of the options afforded in Article 137 of the EU VAT Directive.

8. Liability

8.1 Reverse charges

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism — non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)
Article 194 of the VAT Directive allows EU Member States to use the reverse charge mechanism for

transactions where the place of supply is in the respective EU Member State and the supplier is not
established.

Normal rules would create an obligation for the non-resident supplier to register for VAT in Cyprus and apply
local VAT on the said transaction. The extension of the reverse charge means that the obligation to discharge
the VAT is transferred to the local recipient and thus the obligation for VAT registration is lifted from the non-
resident supplier.

Cyprus applies the reverse charge as follows:

(1) to all services not covered by the general rule, In this case, the supplier should not be established in
Cyprus, and the recipient should be a taxable person, identified for VAT purposes in Cyprus;

(2) to the supply of goods with installation (see Section 3.2 above). In this case, the supplier should neither be
established nor identified for VAT purposes in Cyprus, including not having appointed a VAT representative,
and the recipient should be a taxable person, registered for VAT in Cyprus.

icle 199 Directive)

For the cases outlined below, where the supplier is not established in Cyprus but is required to register as a
result of the legislation, then any related Cyprus input VAT can be reclaimed through this local VAT return.

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (A\

8.1.2.1 Reverse charge on local construction services

The reverse charge applies to services, or services with a supply of goods, supplied for the construction,
alteration, demolition, repair or maintenance of a building or of any civil engineering project.

For these rules to apply, the supplier can be any person, whether taxable person or not, and the customer is a
taxable person, and receives the services for the purpose of his economic activity.

Cyprus | 123

--- Page 124 ---
The Tax Department has issued extensive guidance over which services, or services with goods, are included
within the meaning of Article 11B. This guidance is included in the following Interpretive Circulars: EE 164
(21.03.2012), 165 (06.04.2012), 166 (14.05.2012), 169 (16.07.2012), 174 (30.01.2013) and 184 (12.03.2014).

8.1.2.2 Reverse charge on supplies of used metals and scrap

When a taxable person supplies scrap metals or waste to another taxable person, which is allocated a specific
economic transaction code (issued by the Customs Office), and the supply is a domestic one, then the
customer must apply the reverse charge.

The Tax Department has issued an interpretive circular 179 (29.10.2013) explaining the reverse charge
application on scrap metals and other waste.

8.1.2.3 Reverse charge on the supply of immovable property and land in case of loan default

This provision was enacted in order to facilitate the payment of VAT in cases where (he taxpayer is no longer
in a sustainable financial position, with a bank having to take possession of the property which was held as

a guarantee against the loans provided. In such cases, the bank would purchase the property from the person
but would take possession as part of the loan default proceedings. This would still qualify as a taxable supply
of goods from the defaulting owner to the bank but clearly the defaulting owner would not be in a position to
pay any VAT. For this purpose, the obligation to discharge the VAT is transferred to the recipient (being the
bank), through this provision.

Specifically, the reverse charge mechanism applies to transactions whereby ownership of new buildings or
land subject to VAT, is transferred from the borrower to the lender as part of a loan restructuring or under
forced transfer conditions. The provisions enacted go beyond what is permissible by the VAT Directive which
through Article 199a(1)(g) only permits the use of the reverse charge on the supply of immovable property
sold by a judgment debtor in a compulsory sale procedure.

The conditions for this rule to apply are:

+ The taxable person supplies goods to the recipient;

+ The goods qualify as new buildings, building land intended for erection of one or more fixed structures;

+ The recipient at the time of the supply is a taxable person acquiring the goods as part of its taxable
activities.

When the above conditions are met, the recipient of the goods is obliged to apply the reverse charge
mechanism and discharge the VAT due on the transaction as if it was the recipient’s own supply. Reverse
charge is not an option but an obligation that determines which party discharges the VAT for a specific
transaction.

This provision will remain in force for a limited period of time, currently being until 31 December 2024, but
may be extended.

8.1.2.4 Reverse charge on certain domestic supplies of IT and telecommunications equipment
‘The reverse charge applies to any supply of goods of: (i) mobile telephones, being devices made or adapted
for use in connection with a licensed network and operated on specific frequencies, whether or not they have
any other use; (ii) integrated circuit devices such as microprocessors and central processing units in a state
prior to integration into end user products; (iii) game consoles, tablet PCs and laptops. There is no threshold
for this reverse charge mechanism to appy.

For this provision to apply, the supplier can be any person, whether taxable person or not, and the customer is
a taxable person, and receives the goods for the purpose of his economic activity.

The Tax Department has issued Implementing Order | (29.09.2020) providing detailed explanations on the
application of Article 11E.

8.1.2.5 Reverse charge on certain domestic supplies of non or semi processed precious metals

A domestic reverse charge applies to B2B supplies of unprocessed or semi processed precious metals, in
accordance with Article 199a(1)G).

124 | Cyprus

--- Page 125 ---
For this provision to apply, both the supplier and the customer must be taxable persons, and the customer
receives the goods for the purpose of his economic activity.

‘The customer must maintain the relevant registry required by the laws governing trade and signage of
precious metals for a period of 6 years.

8.2 Joint and several liability

Under Cyprus law, officers of a company, namely directors and secretary, are jointly and severally liable for
the unpaid taxes of the businesses they manage. Joint and several liability also exists for businesses that form
part of'a VAT group for the VAT debts of each member of the VAT group for the entire period that the relevant
entity was part of that VAT group.

Except for the above, Cyprus has not applied Article 205 of the EU VAT Directive to hold any other persons
jointly and severally liable for payment of the VAT, other than the person liable for payment under the VAT
Law.

ux Recovery of input VAT

VAT expenditure is only deductible up to the proportion of the asset's use for business purposes. Cyprus
applies this principle to immovable property, movable goods, as well as to intangible assets.

The Tax Department is allowed, as per the VAT Law, to suspend the examination of any request for a VAT
refund, where the taxable person has not submitted all declarations that are required for direct tax legislation.
‘Any examination will recommence when such obligations have been met.

To this effect, it has issued Implementing Order 2, dated 05.10.2020.

Input VAT deduction is also available for recovery for the period before registration:
+ 3 years for goods (unless the goods were consumed before the date of registration);
+ 6 months for services.

‘The Tax Department has issued Interpretive Circular EE 114 (09.05.2007) on this topic.

a1 Non-recoyerable input VAT

Input VAT is not recoverable in the following cases

* — entertainment expenses except where these are solely for employees;

= purchase of an ordinary passenger (saloon) car unless used for qualifying purposes ie. resale, passenger
transport for reward (e.g. taxi, bus), rental, or for driver instruction;
+ however, input VAT on running and maintenance expenses of passenger cars is allowable, to the

extent that the car is used for business purposes;

+ accommodation and subsistence expenses for entertainment purposes other than for employees and
business associates;

+ remuneration-in-kind, as well as on fringe benefits for staff (provision of food and drink, sporting and
recreational facilities, salaries, transport for personal use, etc.); and

= corporate gifts exceeding EUR 17,09 in value per gift (excluding VAT).

If the entire non-recoverable input VAT is less than the deminimis rule, then it is entirely recoverable, The de-
minimis rule is where the total non-recoverable input VAT is less than €171 per month on average and does
not exceed 50% of all input VAT for the period, calculated on an annual basis.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT shows up as a credit balance on the TFA portal. In order to obtain a refund, the taxable
person must submit a refund request for each quarter separately through the TRA portal. A taxable person
has a 6-year period from the end of the VAT quarter in which excess input VAT arose, to make a request for
payment of the due VAT refund.

Cyprus | 125

--- Page 126 ---
A VAT refund is provided in cases where:

+ the taxable person is not likely lo cover excess input VAT with output VAT; or

+ excess input VAT relates to zero rated supplies or supplics made outside of Cyprus (with right to
deduction) or acquisition of fixed assets used in the business.

Where excess input VAT has existed for more than 8 months, this should be refunded immediately upon
submission of a request for refund via TFA.

Refunds are returned with interest based on the Unified Public Interest Rate for Overdue Payments (see
Section 10.3.3 below), The law provides that interest starts on the fourth month following the date of
application for the refund, and only if the Tax Department is not to blame for any delay. The 4-month period
is extended to 8 months if the Tax Department instigates an investigation.

9.3. Adjustment periods

Movable goods and immovable property are subject to the capital goods scheme. Intangible assets such as
copyrights, patents, licenses, trademarks and similar rights, including goodwill, are also subject to the capital
goods scheme provided that:

+ they are used for more than one accounting period; and

+ their value exceeds € 17,086.

Adjustment periods are:
+ Syears for movable goods and intangible assets; and
+ 10 years for immovable property.

‘The Tax Department has issued the following circulars on this topic: EE 107 (02.05.2006)andEE 170(01.11.2012).
Cyprus does not generally have an adjustment period for services.

The refund application must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax authorities of the

found using the following link: httpy.//inyurl.com/45r97ujn

Annual minimum limit for claims that are made for | calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year is
EUR 50. Interim minimum limit for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3 months is
EUR 400.

Minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in | calendar year, unless it is a
remainder of the year. Maximum period for making a claim is | calendar year.

Deadline for making a claim is 30 September of the year following the year over which the claim is made. It
is possible to submit a claim within 5 calendar years after the end of the calendar year in which the VAT was
incurred, but if the claim is filed after the deadline of 30 September then no appeal can be filed.

Supporting documents (e.g, the original invoices / copies of invoices) are generally not required, although the
Commissioner of Taxation has the right to request original documentation in examining a refund request.

The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within 4 months of receipt of the claim. The

period in which the authorities must make a decision can be extended to 6 months or 8 months when the
authorities request additional information.

95 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

‘The Tax Department has issued in English, Information Leaftet 8, dated December 2008, on the topic of VAT
refunds to EU and non-EU taxable persons.

It is possible for taxable persons outside of the EU to reclaim input VAT on the purchase of goods and

services in Cyprus, or VAT that was imposed on imports in Cyprus, provided that the taxable person:

+ does not have a residence in any EU Member State;

+ isnot registered, liable or eligible to be registered for VAT in Cyprus;

+ has no place of business or other residence in Cyprus; and

+ does not make any supplies in Cyprus, other than those subject to reverse charge by the recipient,
including international transportation services of goods.

Cyprus will only allow input VAT to be reclaimed if there are reciprocity arrangements in place i.e. that
country applies similar concessions to Cyprus traders, Currently, reciprocity arrangements are in place only
with Switzerland, Israel and Norway. In addition, under the Brexit arrangements, the Department of Taxation
also accepts input VAT to be reclaimed by taxable persons established in the UK.

‘The non-EU taxable person must have proof of the VAT that it paid, either correctly completed VAT invoices
from the supplier, or import documents Included with the refund application, originals of all invoices and/or
import documents must be provided. Copies or photocopies are not acceptable.

Claims for refund must be submitted no later than 6 months after the end of the ‘prescribed year’ in whieh the
input VAT was incurred. Prescribed year means the 12-month period beginning with the 1" July and ending
the 30" June of the following calendar year. As such, claims must be made by 31 December. In no case can a
claim be made for VAT outside of the prescribed year dates.

Claims can be made by the taxable persons themselves or through an agent / VAT representative. The Cyprus
Tax Department may require the nomination of a VAT representative.

Form VAT 109 must be used to request the claim. The form can be accessed through the following link:
https://tinyurl.com/mtc4vews. Documentary evidence should be attached to the form. For the first claim,

the taxable person should inchide a certificate from the official authority of the taxable person’s country of
establishment, showing it is registered for business purposes in that country. Such certificates are valid for 12
months from their date of issue. Once a certificate expires, a new one will have to be furnished with further
claims,

Once a claim has been submitted, it will be examined, and if approved, a credit transfer to a specified account
will be made. If the claim is refused, the Tax Department will communicate this in writing, along with the
reasons for the refusal.

The minimum claim is EUR 205 if it covers a period of less than 1 prescribed year. However, this minimum
claim is reduced to EUR 25 in the following situations:

+ where that period represents the final part of the prescribed year; or

+ where the claim covers the entire prescribed year.

Cyprus | 127

--- Page 128 ---
The following VAT cannot be reclaimed:

+ non-business supplies (but, if the supply covers business and non-business purposes, then it is possible to
reclaim VAT on the business part of the supply);

+ any supply or import of ordinary passenger cars;

* certain second hand goods, e.g. cars and antiquities for which the VAT margin scheme is used;

+ all business entertainment/hospitality expenses, but does not include the provision of entertainment to (a)
employees of the business, (b) if the business is a body corporate, to its directors or persons otherwise
engaged in the management, unless the provision of entertainment to such persons is incidental to its
provision to others;

+ any goods or services used or to be used to make a supply in Cyprus (in this case they should effect a
direct registration in Cyprus); and

* goods and services, such as hotel accommodation, that the business bought for resale and which are for
‘the direct benefit of travellers.

10. Administrative Obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice must contain:

* unique sequential identification number;

+ time of supply or of cash receipt;

* date of issue;

+ name, address and VAT registration number of supplier;

* name and address of customer;

+ where the supply involves B2B intra~Community supplies of goods or services, where the reverse charge
applies, the VAT registration number of the customer;

+ sufficient description of the identity of the goods or services;

+ for each description, the unit price or quantity, as well as the VAT rate, and the net payable in EUR;

+ the gross total amount payable, net of VAT;

+ the amount of any cash discount provided;

+ each VAT rate and the total VAT amount for each different rate, in EUR;

* the total VAT amount in BUR;

+ name, address and VAT identification number of VAT representative where applicable;

+ reference to self-billing or a reference: “avtotioz6ynon” where this is the case;

* reference to reverse charge or a reference: “avtiotpopn xpéwon” if the VAT is payable by the recipient;

+ reference to triangular transaction or a reference: “tpryavuch ovvadAayi” where the invoice is issued as
an intermediary in triangulation, although as per ECJ jurisprudence and the VAT Directive, “avtiotpogn
‘ypéoon” could also be used:

+ where a special scheme is used, reference to that scheme should be included. Specifically, where
applicable, reference should be made to:

+ cash accounting or KaPeotéis taperaKiis hoylotuciig;

10.1.2 Time limits
An invoice must be issued within 30 days of the time of the supply.

However, with regards to intra-Community supply of goods, as well as for all supplies of services for which
the VAT is due by the recipient, invoices must be issued by the 15" day of the month, following the month of
supply, as required by Article 222 of the VAT Directive.

--- Page 129 ---
10.1.3 Currency
Invoices can be issued in foreign currencies. However, the total amount of VAT must (also) be stated in EUR.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

Given that VAT declarations are to be completed in Euros, where the value of a supply is denominated in a
foreign currency, the taxable person must convert that value to Euros. Depending on the transaction, such a
conversion can only be made using the exchange spot rate as per the European Central Bank (“ECB”), or the
Customs legislation, which provides for using the exchange rate of the Department of Customs and Excise.
The table below summarises when to use which rate:

Type of transaction: Rate to be used:
+ Amounts reported in VIES returns Customs rates
+ Output VAT reported in VAT returns Customs rates
+ Services received from abroad which are subject to
the reverse charge provisions in Cyprus Customs rates
+ Intra-Community acquisitions ECB

Customs rates: https.//tinyurl.com/mwvdujuu

Currently the VAT Law contains two provisions which contradict each other:

+ Article 8(1), Part (I), Fourth Schedule, VAT Law — Provides that all supplies of goods and services are
translated using Customs & Excise Department rates;

+ Paragraph 4, Regulation 12, KAII 314/200! ~ Regulates invoice contents and provides that for purposes
of calculating the excise value, the translation is made using ECB published rates;

+ Paragraph 3(1), Part I, Fourth Schedule ~ Provides that intra-Community acquisition transactions are
translated using the ECB published rates.

10.1.5 Self-billing

Self-billing is permitted where there is an agreement between the supplier and the customer and a notification
has been provided to the Tax Department.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

Businesses are allowed to issue a simplified invoice only in the cases of sales by a retailer to a non-taxable
person for an invoice amount up to EUR 85 and assuming it does not relate to a transaction with a person of
another EU Member State.

Such a simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ name, address and VAT registration number of the retailer;

+ date of issue;

+ sufficient description of the identity of the goods or services;

+ total gross amount (VAT included); and

+ for every VAT rate applicable, the total gross amount (VAT included), and the relevant VAT rate.

Tf the customer requests a full invoice, the retailer is required to provide one.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices, and other relevant documents for VAT compliance, must be stored for the minimum period of 6
years, unless otherwise stated in the law (i.e. 10 years under OSS schemes).

Invoices must be stored in the same form as they were received or issued i.e. received paper invoices must be

stored in paper form and electronically received ones must be stored in electronic form. For electronic invoices,
the authenticity of origin, integrity of content and readability must be guaranteed during the storage period.

Cyprus | 129

--- Page 130 ---
Original paper invoices must be stored at the premises of the business. Electronic invoices can be stored
elsewhere, even overseas. However, in such cases, the tax authorities must have the right to access those
invoices, and to download them in a usable format, and within a reasonable period of time.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

The taxable person can choose any form for the electronic invoicing as long as the authenticity of origin,
integrity of content and readability are guaranteed, A customer is allowed to refuse an electronic invoice.

10.2 Compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period

A VAT return period covers 3 calendar months i.e. 4 quarterly periods per year. Before | January 2024 there
were 3 different stackers of VAT returns following a different quarterly pattern each. The allocated stacker
was dependant on the industry of the taxable person. However, for VAT registrations as of | January 2024 all
have to file VAT returns per quarter on a calendar-year basis.

Taxable persons may apply for monthly or yearly returns if they are in a position of continuous refunds or
limited transactions. Electronic filing is obligatory via TFA portal as of 23 March 2023 for all classes of
taxable persons.

10.2.2. Filing of VAT return and payment
Every taxable person is obliged to submit a tax declaration, being the VAT return Form VATA, and pay the
tax that may be due. As of 23 March 2023 electronic filing of VAT returns via the TFA portal is mandatory.
Please note that both VIES and VAT returns are submitted through TFA electronically although each requires
a separate registration/activation, Such activation may be done electronically at:
hutps:/Maxforall. mof gov.cy/CreateAccount

VAT must be reported, and any VAT that is due must be paid, within 40 days following the end of the
reporting period, If the due date falls on a weekend, or on a public holiday, then the due date for filing and
payment is moved to the next business day.

Payment can only be made:
+ at approved credit institutions (banks),

+ through a direct wire transfer to the account of the Tax Department with the Central Bank of Cyprus,
card payment directly through TFA portal.

‘The Tax Department no longer mails tax declarations in paper format. Instead reminders are sent via e-mail
to the registered e-mail address of each taxable person. The liability to submit tax declarations lies with the
taxable person regardless of whether a reminder is received or not. Thus, as the Tax Department is moving to
a fully digital environment, it is important to ensure that the e-mail address registered with them is valid and
any electronic correspondence is not blocked by server/e-mail filters as spam or junk

10.2.3. European sales listing
The European sales listing is referred to in Cyprus as the VAT Information and Exchange System or VIES.

In order to register for VIES, a VAT registration with the Tax Department is firstly required. Registration in
the VIES system occurs automatically when registering with the Tax Department.

A VIES declaration must be submitted by the 15" day of the month following the reporting month. If the due
date falls on a weekend or on a public holiday, it can be submitted on the next business day. It can only be
submitted electronically. There is no threshold for VIES, meaning that if a supplier established in Cyprus,
who is not VAT registered, supplies a service to a taxable person in another EU Member State, and the VAT
is due in that EU Member State, then the Cyprus supplier must register with the Cyprus Tax Department in
order to further register for VIES and declare the transaction therein.

130 | Cyprus

--- Page 131 ---
The Tax Department has issued in the Greek language Information Leaflet 17, dated July 2013, on VIES. The
Leaflet can be accessed through the following link:
hitps://tinyurl.com/ycxhvute

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement
Cyprus has no national recapitulative statements.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

‘The Tax Department does not have the right to issue a VAT assessment if 6 years have passed from the end
of the VAT period, or the date of importation or acquisition. In the case of fraud or purposeful omission, the
period of assessment can be extended to 12 years.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
The following table summarises the main penalties imposed under the VAT Law:

Non compliance: Penalty:
+ Late registration EUR 85 per month
+ Late deregistration BUR 85 one off

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time
The following table summarises the main penalties imposed under the VAT law:

Non compliance: Penalty:

+ Late submission of VAT retum EUR 100 per return
+ Late submission of VIES return — EUR 50 per return
+ Late submission of Intrastat return EUR 15 per return

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

The penalty for not paying VAT on time is 10% on the VAT payable amount and additional interest on this
amount applies. The rate of interest for unpaid taxes, contributions or charges payable to the Government

is interest based on the Unified Public Interest Rate for Overdue Payments which is fixed annually by the
Minister of Finance by virtue of section 4(1) of the Unified Public Interest Rate for Overdue Payments Law of
2006, Law 167(1)/06. The historic rates of interest for unpaid taxes are as follows:

+ 2006 and prior years: 9.00% p.a.

+ 2007-2009: 8.00% p.a.
+ 2010: 5.35% p.a.
+ 2011-2012: 5,00% p.a.
+ 2013: 4.15% p.a.
+ 2014: 4.50% p.a.
+ 2015: 4.00% p.a.
+ 2015-2016: 4.00% p.a.
+ 2017-2018: 3.50% p.a.
+ 2019: 2.00% p.a.
+ 2020-2022: 1.75% p.a.
+ 2023: 2.25%

+ 2024: 5%

+ 2025: 5.5%

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

The following penalties can be imposed:

+ additional VAT of 10% on the output VAT due but not accounted for;

+ annual interest (see Section 16.3 above) calculated on both the output VAT due and the 10% additional
tax; and

Cyprus {131

--- Page 132 ---
* criminal penalties of up to 300% of the output VAT if tax evasion is detected.

In case of fraudulent evasion of VAT, the penalty is up to 3 years imprisonment or a fine up to 3 times the
amount of VAT payable, or both. In case of receipt of goods on which VAT was evaded, the penalty is up to
12 months imprisonment or a fine of EUR 8,543 or both. If the VAT shown in an assessment issued by the
Commissioner of Taxation is not paid, the penalty is up to 12 months imprisonment or a fine of EUR 8,543
or both. Directors and officers, including secretary, of a legal entity are considered liable for any criminal
obligations of the company.

10.3.5 Other penalties
The following penalties can be imposed:

+ Late correction of mistake on VIES return EUR 15 per return
+ Failure to issue a lawful receipt 20% of the value of the supply
+ Failure to correctly apply reverse charge mechanism
(from 1 July 2021) EUR 200 per return, to a
maximum of EUR 4,000

10.4 Jntrastat

In Cyprus a separate monthly Intrastat return must be submitted to the Cyprus Tax Department by the 10" day
of the month, following the reporting month. It can only be submitted electronically.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

The 2024 reporting thresholds are provided below. Readers can refer to the Cyprus Tax Department's website
for updated information in English, using the following link: https-//tinyurl.com/mwhmex8a

For both arrivals and dispatches, there are two reporting thresholds to bear in mind. The exemption threshold
is the threshold under which no declaration is required. There is also the simplification threshold. If the

total value of the relevant commercial transactions since the 1* of January fall between the two thresholds,
then only limited information is required on the Intrastat form. If the value of the transactions exceeds the
simplification threshold, then all the information is required.

For arrivals, the exemption threshold is EUR 350,000 and the simplification threshold is EUR 2,700,000.
For dispatches, the exemption threshold is EUR 75,000 and the simplification threshold is EUR 5,800,000.
A taxable person may appoint an agent to file Intrastat returns.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported
‘The information which needs to be reported for both the arrivals and the dispatches is the following:

* the EU Member State of dispatch or destination;

+ VAT number of the taxable person making the declaration;

+ the reference period (i.e. the reporting month);

+ the nature of the movement of the goods (i.e. arrival or dispatch);

+ the HS Code (eight-digit harmonised commodity code according to Combined Nomenclature (CN));
* the invoice value of the goods;

+ the statistical value;

+ the quantity of the goods (i.e. net weight in kilograms);

+ other measure for certain goods (e.g. litres, m?, or number of pieces);

+ the country of origin (e.g. where the goods are produced or made or processed);

+ the nature of the transaction (e.g. purchase, sale, return of goods, reparation etc.); and

+ means of transport, for instance “by road” or “by sea”.

Distance sales transactions must be included in the Intrastat declaration.

There are no additional requirements.
