The Czech Republic

In the Czech Republic, the European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented through the Law 235/2004
Coll. “Zakon ze dne 1, dubna 2004 o dani z ptidané hodnoty” (‘the VAT Law’). VAT is referred to in the
Czech Republic as “dati z ptidané hodnoty” or “DPH”. The territorial scope is the entire Czech Republic,
which consists of three historical lands, Bohemia, Moravia and part of Silesia.

1. Taxable persons

11 Registration & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Small businesses do not have to register and enjoy a VAT relief. The annual turnover must remain below a
threshold. This threshold is CZK 2,536,500 (approximately EUR 100,000) for the preceding calendar year.
If the cumulated turnover of CZK 2,536,500 is exceeded in a calendar year, the taxable person becomes a
taxpayer from the next day.

The registration is started through filing the electronic registration form in the Czech language only.
hitps:/itinyurl.com/d mi. dic

The application form and the guidelines can be read in English via the following link:
hittps://tinyurl.com/aes

1.2 Registration & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

‘The local Czech VAT registration turnover threshold for non-established legal entities and non-resident
individuals is also CZK 2,536,500. If the threshold of CZK 2,000,000 (that was applicable for 2024) was not
exceeded in 2024, the exemption may be applied in 2025 until the new threshold of is exceeded. A taxable
person becomes a VAT payer the day after exceeding the new threshold. The taxable person has 10 working
days to register with the tax office after exceeding the new turnover threshold.

‘The foreign taxable person must decide whether it wishes to be registered for Czech VAT as a taxpayer or
just. ‘identified’ for Czech VAT. The latter is mostly applicable for intra-Community acquisition of goods.

‘The applicant must file an electronic application in Czech language and deliver a signed hard copy original
confirmation of the electronic filing of the registration in the Czech language at the following address to
register for Czech VAT:

Finanént tad pro Moravskoslezsky kraj
Na Jizdérné 3162/3

709 00 Ostrava PFivoz

The Czech Republic

When registering, the following information must be provided to the tax authorities in Czech language or ina

sworn translation of the hardcopy originals to the Czech language:

+ acompletely filled in confirmation PDF “e-tiskopis” of electronic filing of registration form “25 5104
MFin 5104 — vzor &. 10”;

: VAT registration certificate in the country, where the business is established “osvédéeni 0 registraci k
DPH éi obdobné dani v jiné zemi™

+ licence to perform economical activities “oprévnéni k provozovani éinnosti”; and

+ the extract from the commercial register, traders’ register or similar register “vYpis z obchodniho
rejsttiku”.

‘The Czech tax authorities usually assign the VAT number within 30 days after receiving all the required
documents.

‘The Czech Republic | 135

--- Page 136 ---
Taxable persons not established in the EU who are registered for Czech VAT must appoint a Czech
representative for accepting mail from the Czech VAT authorities (a ‘mail representative’, also see 1.4).
This representative must have an active Czech DATABOX, assigned by law. Severe penalty applies if the
representative is not assigned.

Czech VAT registered persons not established in the Czech Republic will be de-registered by the Czech VAT
authorities, when they do not perform taxable supplies or zero-rated supplies within a period of six months.
The de-registration process is initiated by sending a notification to the “dormant” non-established person. This
process requires active communication with the VAT authority, to prevent the de-registration, which would
result in forced repayment of previously deducted input Czech VAT on assets belonging to the “dormant”
non-established person located in the Czech Republic

13 VAT grouping
‘The Czech VAT legislation provides the possibility for VAT grouping. The supplies of the goods or services
within the VAT group are not subject to Czech VAT.

Where two or more persons established or having a fixed establishment in the Czech Republic are closely
bound to one another by financial, economic and organisational links, those persons are treated as a single
taxable person for VAT purposes at their own request or on the initiative of the tax authorities.

Financial links exist if more than 40% of the shares of the members are directly or indirectly held by the same
person(s). Organisational links exist if the members are under common management.

The VAT group gets a new common VAT number “CZ699nnnnnk”, The members of the VAT group cannot
use their separate VAT numbers, as they are cancelled when they join the group. The leading member of the
group must file VAT returns and other reports (¢.g., the European sales list) on behalf of the entire VAT group.
4 _ Fiscal representative

A fiscal representative concept does not exist in the Czech Republic, EU and non-BU taxable persons register
directly for VAT purposes.

Taxable persons established in non-EU countries are obliged to appoint an EU established representative in
customs proceedings when importing goods in the Czech Republic. Taxable persons established in non-EU
countries are also obliged to appoint a ‘mail representative’ with obligatory databox in the Czech Republic
when registering for Czech VAT.

2. Taxable transactions

2.1 Transfer of business

The non-taxation of the transfer of a going concern (applicable to both goods and services) has been
implemented in Czech VAT legislation.

2.2 Supplies free of charge

The principles laid down in Articles 16 and 26 of the EU VAT Directive are incorporated in the Czech VAT
Act.

In relation to the free supply of goods, the free supply of ‘gifts of small value’ is not taxed in the Czech
Republic if the purchase price per item is less than CZK 500 (EUR 20) excluding VAT.

‘The fiee supply of ‘trade samples’, is defined in the Czech VAT legislation without any financial limit if
provided in the furtherance of the business only.

136 | The Czech Republic

--- Page 137 ---
‘The supply of goods or services free of charge may be subject to VAT as a deemed taxable supply. Taxation of
a deemed supply of goods and services occurs in general if the acquisition of goods and services entitled the
purchaser to a full or partial input tax deduction. The tax authorities are focussing on the free consumption of
goods and services by employees and owners of a business, and the relevant provisions in the Czech VAT Act
are applied resulting in assessment of the output VAT.

2.3 Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

Itis possible to apply for an exemption of VAT on the importation of goods into the Czech Republic if it is
followed by an intra-Community supply. The importer of record must be registered for VAT in the Czech
Republic and the exemption code and VAT registration number of the importer and VAT registration number
of the customer in the other EU Member State must be shown on the SAD import document.

2.3.2. Postponed accounting on importation
Czech VAT registered taxable persons importing goods from non-EU countries may defer the payment of
import VAT to the regular VAT return. With this scheme, importers avoid payment of VAT at the time of
importation that has to be reclaimed at a later stage (in the VAT return). Instead, the importer will have to
account for the VAT due in its Czech VAT return, If the importer is entitled to a full deduction, the VAT due
can be reclaimed in the same periodical VAT return. On balance, there is no payment of VAT.

Taxable persons that are not established in the EU can use the import VAT deferment scheme if they are
registered for Czech VAT and appoint a representative in customs proceedings. This representative uses
the Czech VAT number assigned to the non-EU established taxable person and his Economic Operator
Registration and Identification (BORI) number.

3. Place of Supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

There is a simplification for call-off stock in the Czech Republic. It applies to intra-Community acquisitions.
It does not apply to imports.

Generally, a non-established taxable person bringing stock into the Czech Republic must be identified for
VAT in the Czech Republic. However, a simplification for call-off stock applies and the VAT registration of a
non-established taxable person could be avoided if the following conditions are met:

the goods are intended for a buyer who receives the goods from another EU Member State;

+ the buyer is registered for VAT in the Czech Republic;

+ the seller and the buyer have a written frame purchase agreement;

+ the ownership of the goods is transferred to the buyer when the goods are called off for the buyer's
production process or resale; and

+ the non-established taxable person is not registered or, otherwise, not required to register for VAT in the
Czech Republic.

There is an obligation of the buyer to recognise the intra-Community acquisition the moment the goods are
physically taken out (delivered) from the warehouse in the Czech Republic, This purchase by the Czech
customer is treated as an intra~-Community acquisition. From the Czech perspective, the above-mentioned sale
of goods by the non-established taxable person is, therefore, not treated as a Czech local supply.

There is no simplification for consignment stock. The domestic supply of goods to Czech VAT registered final

customers is subject to the domestic reverse charge mechanism, provided that the owner of the goods in stock
is not established in the Czech Republic and not registered for Czech VAT as a taxpayer.

The Czech Republic | 137

--- Page 138 ---
3.2 Installation supplies

Goods, supplied with installation or assembly, are treated as supplied where they are installed or assembled.
In the Czech Republic, there is no formal definition of the notion “supply with installation or assembly”. The
VAT Law only stipulates that the goods may be installed or assembled by the supplier or by a third person.

As concerns to pure installation services, the Czech VAT law refers directly to Council Implementing
Regulation (EU) No 282/2011, Article 31a, paragraph 2. letter m) and letter f) to define the relationship of
installation services to immovable property.

Construction work is seen as a supply of services if it falls within the nomenclature CZ-CPA codes from 41 to
43 valid as of | January 2015.

On both types of supplies the reverse charge may apply (see also paragraph 8.1.2).

33 Distance sales

‘The distance sales threshold for the Czech Republic is BUR 10,000 or the equivalent of this amount in Czech
Crowns (approximately CZK. 250,000).

Itis possible to opt voluntarily for charging VAT in the Czech Republic before the threshold is exceeded.
The taxable person must inform the tax authorities of the Member State of departure of the goods that he has
opted for payment of VAT in the Czech Republic before the threshold is exceeded.

A Czech taxable person can opt to tax supplies in the EU Member State of destination but must notify the tax
authorities in writing. The option is in both cases applicable for at least 2 years.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Under the Czech VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the
EU VAT Directive. Under the relevant rules, the simplified triangulation rules apply if
+ the Czech Republic is the country of arrival of the goods;
+ the middleman (B) is not VAT registered or identified for VAT in the Czech Republic;
» the middleman states on the tax document:
* the same tax identification number that it communicated to the seller; and
+ — the indication “the tax will be paid by the customer”.

Where the Czech Republic is the EU Member State of des ition of the goods, the wording of the Czech
legislation does not preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is VAT registered
in the country of dispatch, however, it does preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the
middleman (B) is VAT registered for VAT or identified for VAT in the Czech Republic. The Czech rules also
preclude application of the simplified triangulation to (part of) a supply chain consisting of more than three
parties.

35 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons, taxable persons engaged in exempt
activities and flat-rate farmers or SMEs from suppliers established in another EU Member State is CZK
326,000 (approximately EUR 13,000).

3.6 _ Effective use and enjoyment

The clause of “effective use and enjoyment’ included in Article 59a paragraph b of the EU VAT Directive, is
implemented in Article 9a of the Czech VAT Law. The use and enjoyment provision applies to B2B supplies
where the customer is a taxable person established outside the EU and he is VAT registered in the Czech
Republic. This applies to all services mentioned in Articles 44 and 56 of the EU VAT Directive. If the service
is used and enjoyed in the Czech Republic, the place of supply will be the Czech Republic and the supplier
must charge Czech VAT. The use and enjoyment rules also apply o B2C supplies but only for the rental of

138 | The Czech Republic

--- Page 139 ---
means of transport. If the service is actually used in the Czech Republic, the supplier of the service will have
to charge and pay Czech VAT. The use and enjoyment for the rental of means of transport works both ways; if
the service is actually used outside the EU no Czech VAT is due.

4, Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles

In the case of a supply of goods or services, the VAT becomes chargeable at the time the goods or services
are supplied. For goods, this is the date when they are put at the disposal of the purchaser. A domestic supply
of goods involving their transportation is deemed to be carried out at the date the goods are delivered to the
customer. A supply of service is deemed to be carried out at the date the service is finally executed or when
the invoice is issued, whichever is earlier.

For intra-Community acquisitions of goods, the VAT becomes chargeable on issue of the invoice, latest on the
15® day of the month following the acquisition if no invoice has been issued by that time,

If VAT was unduly charged, this VAT becomes chargeable on the date of issuing the invoice on which that
VAT was mentioned.

4.2 Advance payments

Where a payment is to be made on account before the goods or services are supplied, VAT becomes
chargeable on receipt of the payment in case the object of the supply is sufficiently specified. This applies also
to advance payment under the tour operator margin scheme (TOMS).

4.3 Continuous supplies

Services ongoing over a period of more than I year (12 months) are regarded as being supplied on the expiry
of each full calendar year up to the time the provision of the services ceases. An exception to this rule applies
to services of the attorneys and bankruptcy solicitors, if payment for their services is due by the government.

4.4 Cash accounting

Czech VAT legislation does not allow ‘cash accounting’, VAT is chargeable when the advance payment is
received, and/or VAT is chargeable when the supply is made or invoice raised, whichever is earlier. If an
invoice is issued, the recipient of the invoice can reclaim the VAT upon receipt of the invoice under the
normal conditions in the VAT return for the period in which he received this invoice. He doesn’t have to wait
until he paid the invoice.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

In the Czech Republic, bad debt relief is available to taxable persons, provided that the amount not yet paid
by the customer became a bad debt as a result of the customer being (declared) bankrupt.

Application to the tax authorities (a refund request) must be done at the latest 3 years from the original day of
taxable supply. The supplier can submit the refund claim in the VAT retum for the period of the declaration
of bankruptcy by the court. A corrective tax document must be sent to the debtor. The 3 year period is paused
during the bankruptcy proceedings.

The Czech Republic | 139

--- Page 140 ---
5.2 Cash discountsAssume that the supplier sends the following invoice issued in the end of d ne:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
21% VAT EUR 210
Total to be paid EUR 1,210

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount.”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days, at the beginning of July. In that case, the supplier must pay EUR
210 in its June VAT return. The customer can deduct EUR 210 in its June VAT return, Therefore, the supplier
paid too much VAT and the customer deducted too much. In late July, the supplier must send a credit note
for the 2%. When the customer confirms the receipt of this credit note in July, the supplier has the right to
decrease the output VAT and the customer must decrease the recovered input VAT in the July VAT return,

5.3 Open Market Value

In the cvent of a supply of goods or services takes place between related parties (with no full right to input
VAT deduction) for reasons outside business purposes or for use of staff, the ‘open market value’ must be
applied.

Open market value means the full amount that a customer at the same marketing stage at which the supply of
goods or services takes place, would have to pay, under conditions of fair competition, to a supplier at arm’s
length, in order to obtain the goods or services in question at that time.

In line with Article 80 of the EU VAT Directive, the Czech Republic has implemented the following

transactions for which ‘the open market value’ as the taxable amount must apply:

+ where the consideration is lower than the open market value and the recipient of the supply does not have
a full right of deduction; or

+ where the consideration is higher than the open market value and the supplier does not have a full right of
deduction.

‘The relevant provision in the Czech VAT Act contains the definition of what constitutes a related party for the
purposes of that provision.

54 Returnable Packaging

Under the Czech VAT rules, the supply of the retumable packaging is not subject to VAT, provided that the
payment for returnable packaging is guaranteed to be remitted at the return of the returnable packaging.

6. VAT rates

6.1 Standard rate
The standard rate in the Czech Republic is 21%.

6.2 Reduced rate

The reduced rate in the Czech Republic is 12% and applies for instance to:

+ live animals;

+ food except certain beverages;

* medication, if not exempt from VAT;

+ regular public transportation services on road and in cableways and ski lifts;

+ regular public transportation services on waterways;

* all accommodation services, including Airbnb and similar residential dwellings;
+ catering services including tap water and milk (except beverages);

+ sewage;

+ supply of drinkable water in tubes;

140 | The Czech Republic

--- Page 141 ---
+ stay in Turkish bath and salt caves;

* access to cultural and sports events;

* — renovation of old residential houses and social dwellings;
+ construction of private houses and social dwellings; and
+ funeral services for human bodies.

The full list is shown in the Annexes 2 and 3 to the VAT Act. The list changes regularly in relation to changes
of government of the Czech Republic.

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rate

Zero rate and applies to:

+ sale of paper books and non-advertisement magazines and newspaper; in CN headings 4901, 4903 to
4905, 4911 or 8523; and

+ _ supply of electronic books.

7. Exemptions

TA Exemptions with right to deduction

‘The Czech Republic has a zero rate for the following supplies and services:

+ exports and intra~Community supplies of goods:

+ goods supplied to travellers resident outside the EU under retail export schemes or through tax-free shops;
* services in the framework of inward processing on behalf of principals resident outside the EU;

* sea vessels and aircraft and related goods and services;

+ supplies made to customers with diplomatic immunity;

* supplies of monetary gold to central banks;

+ international passenger transport by land, seagoing vessels and airplane; and

+ provisioning of seagoing vessels, except for provisioning of vessels for inshore fishing with foodstuffs.

7.1.1 Intra~-Community supplies
‘To prove zero rating in case of intra~-Community supplies, certain documents are required,

‘The supplier must have transport documents proving the receipt of goods in the other EU Member State or
a combination of certain documents, such as a copy of the sales invoice, proof of payment (from abroad), a
purchase order or sworn statement of the customer (if available) that the goods were received by him. These
documents, or a sufficient combination thereof, must provide sufficient evidence that the goods in question
were transported to another EU Member State. The invoice must include a reference that a supply is exempt
(e.g. “VAT exempt intra-Community supply, Article 138 of 2006/112/EC directive”, VAT payable by the
customer) and the VAT number of both the supplier and the customer.

7.1.2 Export supplies

The export of goods is exempt from tax in respect of the supply of goods which are dispatched or transported
from the territory of the Czech Republic to a third country

(a) by the seller or a person authorised by the seller, or

(b by the buyer or a person authorised by the buyer, if the buyer has no registered office, place of residence
or establishment in the Czech Republic.

To apply a zero rate in case of exports, the exporter must issue the export invoice based on Article 33a of the
VAT Law and in addition to that required evidence in its files that the goods have left the EU based on the
Judgment of the Court of Justice in Case C-275/18 Vin’:

+ an export declaration with a confirmation that the goods have left the BU issued by the Customs Office.

Tn case the above document is missing, the following evidence are required:
+ transport documents (bills of lading/CMR) signed by the customer on receipt of the goods;
+ import documents stamped by the non-EU customs authorities;

‘The Czech Republic | 141

--- Page 142 ---
+ payment from outside the FU; and
+ inyoives from providers of transport and customs procedures.

7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse
The Czech Republic does not allow VAT warehouses.

7.1.3.2 Excise warehouse

On the intra-Community acquisition and on import of excise goods (alcohol, tobacco, fuels), these can be
placed in excise warehouse with suspension of excise duties. This does not apply to the VAT due on the
transactions,

7.1.3.3. Bonded warehouse

It is possible to place the goods in a bonded warehouse procedure in the Czech Republic. If the goods coming
from a third country are directly placed under a bonded warehouse procedure, no customs duties and no VAT
become due. When the goods are released from the warehouse for free circulation, duties and VAT become
due. An importer who releases the goods into free circulation becomes responsible for the declaration of the
import VAT.

‘The release of goods from a bonded warehouse regime does not constitute an importation if the goods are
subsequently placed under another customs procedure, other than ‘Import’.

The sale of goods while they are placed in a bonded warehouse regime is subject to Czech VAT. Evidence that
the sale took place in the bonded warehouse should be kept in the files (e., list of the transactions from the
warehouse keeper, the agreements, the invoices, etc.). The supplier makes a domestic Czech taxable supply
and is obliged to charge output VAT.

If the supplier is not registered for Czech VAT, and the purchaser is registered for Czech VAT, the domestic
reverse charge supply applies. The purchaser must self-asses the Czech VAT on purchase of the goods within
the bonded warehouse

7.2 Exemptions without the right to deduction

TA Exemptions in the public interest
Examples of exemptions in the public interest are:
+ hospital services;

+ medical care;

+ ambulance services;

* services rendered by dentists;

+ basic postal services and sale of postal stamps;
* social care, and

* education.

Some exemptions, however, are granted on the condition that the supplier is a non-profit institution and/or on
the condition that no distortion of competition will occur because of the exemption.

7.2.2 Other exemptions
Examples of other exemptions are:

+ financial and banking services;

* insurance services;

* certain games of chance and lotteries;

+ non-commercial activities of public radio and television broadcasting organizations;
+ letting and supply of immovable property;

142 | The Czech Republic

--- Page 143 ---
+ services by employers’ or employees’ organizations; and
+ political, religious, charitable organizations, etc.

The Czech Republic does not provide the possibility to opt for VAT on financial services.

Specifically excluded from the VAT exemption applicable on letting and supply of immovable property are
accommodation services. These services supplied by hotels and private individuals (Airbnb) fall within CZ
CPA 55 category of services and are always taxable.

73 Option for taxation

Regarding the supply and the letting of immovable property parties may opt to tax these transactions for VAT
purposes.

There are conditions for exercising the option to tax the letting of immovable property. The supplier may opt
for VAT and subject the rent to Czech VAT if the customer is registered for Czech VAT. The supplier must
make it explicit, for example, in the contracts, pricelists and on the invoices. Owners of real property cannot
opt for VAT in lease relationships between two VAT payers for leases of dwelling apartments, the building
of a family house in accordance with the legislation governing the land register; and land which includes the
construction of a family house, living space or the building referred to in with which the land is leased.

It is not possible to opt for taxation of financial services.

8. Liability

81 Reverse charges

In the Czech Republic there are several obligatory reverse charge rules in place besides the reverse charge for
general B2B services provided by non-resident suppliers to taxable persons and legal entities with a Czech
VAT ID number established in the Czech Republic, This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and
follows from Art, 44 and Art. 196 EU VAT Directive. There is also the reverse charge in the Czech Republic
for the supply of natural gas and electricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to ‘taxable dealers’,

8.1.1 General reverse charge - non-resident suppliers (Article 194 Directive)
A distinction should be made between the supply of services and the supply of goods.

Services
‘The reverse charge applies to all supplies of services by a non-established supplier if the customer is a taxable
person in the Czech Republic who is obliged to declare and pay the tax in the Czech Republic. This includes
non-established customers registered for VAT in the Czech Republic. The reverse charge also applies if

the customer is a non-taxable legal person established in the Czech Republic. The reverse charge does not
apply if the supplier is VAT registered in the Czech Republic in case of supply of goods with installation or
assembly.

Goods

The reverse charge applies to the supply of goods if the customer is a taxable person registered for VAT
purposes in the Czech Republic. This includes non-established customers registered for VAT in the Czech
Republic. The reverse charge does not apply if the customer is a non-taxable legal person established in the
Czech Republic. The reverse charge does not apply if the non-established supplier is VAT registered in the
Czech Republic.

On the invoice, reference should be made to the application of the reverse charge. A non-established supplier

could use the following text on the invoice: “VAT due by the recipient under Article 108 of the Czech VAT
Act”.

The Czech Republic | 143

--- Page 144 ---
8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

A domestic reverse charge applies to the following supplies of goods and/or services between two Czech VAT

registered taxpayers, based on government decree, if applicable:

+ the supply of construction work, if both supplier and customer are registered for Czech VAT;

+ the supply of old (used) materials, scrap, waste and specific connected services;

+ the supply of investment gold;

+ the supply of CO, Emission Rights; and

+ the supply of ‘hot goods’ specified by the governmental decree Natizenf vlddy ¢. 361/2014 Sb. as a
mechanism of prompt reaction to tax fraud concerning specified ‘hot’ commodities, such as sugar beets
and oily seeds, mobile phones and laptops.

A supplier must refer to the reverse charge. The following text must be mentioned on the invoice in the Czech
language “dai odvede zikaznik”.

8.2 di nt and several liability

Liability of the authorized recipient of excisable goods

An authorised recipient of excisable goods who has incurred an obligation to declare and pay excise duty in
connection with the receipt of selected products from another Member State shall be liable for the unpaid VAT
on the supply of these goods to a third party by a person who purchased them from another Member State.

Liability of the recipient of taxable supply
A Czech VAT registered taxable person purchasing goods or services from another Czech VAT registered
taxable person is jointly liable for the VAT if at the time of the taxable supply or provision of the payment
for such supply he knew or should have known and could have known that the VAT would not be paid by the
supplier to the VAT authority. When such supplier intentionally avoids payment of VAT at the time of supply,
the recipient is obliged to pay this VAT to the VAT authority.

Unreliable VAT registered person
Czech VAT registered taxable persons may be declared by the Czech VAT authority as an ‘unreliable VAT
registered taxable person’ if they lose the trust of the VAT authority. When the supplier is declared on the
public website as an unreliable VAT registered taxable person at the time of supply, the recipient is obliged to
pay the VAT to the VAT authority, in case it is not paid by the supplier (split payment).

9. Recovery of input VAT

OL Non-recoverable input VAT

The input VAT is not recoverable if incurred or due ov costs related to:

+ entertainment that is not tax deductible in the meaning of the Czech Income Tax Law;

+ _ business gifts where the value per item exceeds CZK 500 (approximately EUR 20); and

+ purchase of an ordinary passenger (saloon) car for the part of the price that exceeds CZK 2 million. VAT
is only recoverable up to CZK 420,000 if the taxable supply was performed more than two years ago, unless
the supply was subject to reverse charge.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Excess input VAT is immediately recoverable and refunded by the VAT authorities within 30 days after filing
the VAT return unless a VAT audit is performed.

9.3. Adjustment periods - Capital goods

+ immovable property - 10 years following the year of first use
+ movable property - 5 years following the year of first use

The Czech Republic does not have an adjustment period for services which are capitalised.

--- Page 145 ---
94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

The refund application for Czech VAT must be submitted electronically through the portal of the tax
authorities of the country in which the claimant is established.

The annual minimum limit (for claims that are made for a calendar year or the remainder of a calendar year)
is EUR 50. The interim minimum limit (for claims that are made for less than a calendar year but at least 3
months) is EUR 400.

‘The minimum period for making a claim is 3 consecutive calendar months in one calendar year unless it

is a remainder of the year. The maximum period for making a claim is 1 calendar year. The deadline for
submitting the claim is 30 September of the year following the year over which a claim is made. If the claim
is filed after the deadline of 30 September, then no appeal can be filed.

If the application is not accepted, no notification of such denial is sent to the claimant by the competent Czech
VAT authority, department Praha 1. The claimant must contact its home Member State VAT authority to
obtain a confirmation that the claim was accepted by the Czech VAT authority.

Example: In the decision of the denial of an appeal by the Appellate Tax authority from March 2023 it is
written:

w..dn both cases, it was automatically excluded because it contained an incorrect application period, and in
general, a validation report is always generated automatically by the system, which is sent to the country of
establishment of the applicant (taxpayer). In addition, the Appellate Body also states in general terms that
this validation report is not sent directly to the taxpayer, but to the tax administrator of the applicants country
of establishment. It is therefore within the jurisdiction of each Member State whether to notify its taxpayer of
the ineffectiveness of such a submission and of the possibilities of remedy..... ....On the contrary, the system is
set up in such a way that the Czech tax administrator informs about the receipt of the request for processing.
If the claimant did not receive this communication, he should have been interested why its submission was not
effective and, if necessary, to repeat the application for VAT refund within the deadline specified in the law
(i.e. in this case by 30 September 2021).

The constitutional court decided that the wording of the Czech VAT Act § 82b (2) “delivery of the decision
is valid as of the date, when the e-mail was sent to the claimant” is in breach of the constitutional right of the
claimant. More can be found in the court decision “PLUS 23/20 ze dne 18. 5. 2021”.

Supporting documents (¢.g., the original hardcopy invoices) are generally required to be sent to the Czech
VAT authorities by post. Often, in certain cases the tax authorities may require additional information.

The tax authorities must issue a decision on the refund claim within 4 months of receipt of the claim. The
period in which the authorities must decide will be extended to 6 months or 8 months where the authorities
request additional information. Instead of other methods of delivery, the tax administrator may deliver
documents in the matter of tax refund electronically via the public data network to the e-mail address
specified in the application for tax refund; this document is deemed to have been delivered on the tenth day
following the day on which the data message was sent.

Ifa refund is granted, it will be processed within 30 days and paid to the bank account number provided to the
tax authorities. The time limit for appeals is 30 days following the decision. If this appeal is unsuccessful, the

claimant can initiate proceedings before the Czech administrative court (within 2 months after a receipt of the
decision on appeal).

* refreshments and entertainment.

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice can be in an electronic or a paper form and must contain the following information:
+ full name and address of the supplier of goods or service provider:
+ VAT registration number under which the supply of goods or services is performed:
+ full name and address of the customer/recipient;
+ VAT registration number of the customer;
* invoice number (unique number);
+ quantity and a clear description of the goods or services supplied;
* date at which the invoice is issued;
+ date of the taxable point or the date on which the advance payment is received, if different from the date
of invoice;
* unit price and the discount, in case the discount is not included in the unit price;
+ taxable amount;
+ applied VAT rate;
+ VAT amount which must be shown in the Czech currency “CZK”; and
* — further information:
+ incase of self-billing the following reference is required “vystaveno zakaznikem”;
+ reference to a specific Article and “osvobozeno od dané”, if a supply is VAT exempt;
* reference to the reverse charge: “dai odvede zakaznik”, if VAT is imposed on the recipient on a
domestic reverse charge principle: and
+ reference to the special scheme if any special scheme is applied, e.g., the reference “zvlé8tni rezim
- cestovni sluzba” in case the travel agents’ margin scheme is applied; “zvléStni re2im -pouzité
zbozi” (second-hand goods), “zvlaStnf rezim -umélecka dila” (arts) and “zvla8tni rezim -sbératelské
piedméty a starozitnosti” (antiques).

10.1.2 Time limits
A VAT invoice must be issued within 15 days following the taxable point.

10.1.3 Currency

In case Czech VAT is charged, the amount of Czech VAT must be stated in Czech crowns (CZK). Alll financial
information on the invoice can be stated also in any other currency. The invoice can be payable in any
currency.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

When converting the amount of VAT into CZK, the amount in foreign currency must be converted into CZK
using the last official conversion rate of the Czech National Bank or the European Central Bank on the day of
the tax point.

https://inyurl.com/34f7matf

‘The Czech Republic | 147

--- Page 148 ---
10.1.5 Self-billing

Under Czech VAT legislation, self-billing is allowed in case there is a written agreement between the two
parties, In case an electronic agreement is concluded, it must he electronically signed with a valid electronic
signature of the supplier.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

+ identity of the supplier of the goods and/or services;

+ type of goods or services supplied; and

+ the VAT amount payable or the information needed to calculate it,

It is possible to issue a simplified invoice if the amount of the invoice is not higher than CZK 10,000.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices and other VAT documents must be stored for a period of 10 years. The place of storage must be
announced in advance to the tax authorities if this storage is outside the Czech Republic.

Invoices can be stored in any form, i.e., received paper invoices can be stored in electronic form and vice
versa.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

‘The taxable person can choose any form for the electronic invoicing if the authenticity of origin, integrity
of content and readability are guaranteed. A customer must explicitly agree with the acceptance of an EDI
invoice.

10.2 Compliance

10.2.1. VAT return period

‘The standard VAT period is a calendar month. A small business from its third year of registration may file
a quarterly return after notifying this to the tax authority, provided that its turnover did not exceed CZK.
15,000,000 (approximately EUR 600,000) in the preceding calendar year, There is no annual return. A VAT
registered taxpayer must submit an electronic VAT return for each periodic tax return 25 5401 MFin 5401
vzor &. 25.

https://tinyurl.com/mpzxtahy

Non-established taxpayers are not obliged to file VAT returns for periods of no taxable activity.

10.2.2 Filing of VAT return and payment

The VAT in Czech Crowns due must be received on the CZK bank account of the VAT authorities by the 25"
day of the month following the reporting period (i.e., the VAT due for October 2025 must be received by the
VAT authorities by 25 November 2025). The same applies for the electronic filing of the periodic VAT return.
The guidelines 25 5412/A MFin 5412/A - vzor &. 19 for filling of VAT return are available in English at the
following link.

https://tinyurl.com/3xwx792k

10.2.3 European sales listing

‘A European sales listing VAT Information Exchange System (VIES return) must be submitted on a monthly
basis if an intra-Community supply of goods is performed. The European sales listing for services can be
submitted on a quarterly basis, in case in that period no intra~-Community supply of goods is performed, and
the person has a quarterly tax period. Otherwise, the European sales listing for services must be filed monthly
ifan intra-Community supply of services is performed.

A taxable person making intra-Community supplies must submit an electronic European sales listing return
by the 25" day of the month following the reporting period

An explanatory note to the European sales listing is attached to the electronic form on the website of the
Financial Authority. The guidelines 25 5525 MFin 5525 - vzor &. 5 can be found here:

Anttps://tinyurl.com/46mknefw

Special purchases listing must be filed for the intra-Community acquisition of goods in the Czech Republic
(see 10.2.4).
10.2.4 National recapitulative statement and intra-Community acquisition reporting

The VAT listing in the Czech Republic must be submitted in case the domestic VAT is due or VAT recovery is
applied. This must be submitted by the 25 day of the month following the reporting period, no exemptions neither
later filing are tolerated. Both supplier as well as the customer who claims the input VAT must submit the domestic
VAT listing, This means that for one domestic supply two statements are filed, in which the data relating to one
invoice should match each other for any invoice higher that CZK 10,000. In case of intra-Community acquisition
of goods and services, the customer registered for Czech VAT as a taxpayer must report this acquisition.

Every VAT document for a supply amounting more that CZK 10,000 including VAT must be recorded on
the domestic VAT listing in the Czech Republic line by line. Supplies below this threshold are reported in a
summary box.

National recapitulative statement in the Czech Republic on the website of the Financial Authority in the

Antips://tinyurl.combwwaS3mrv
and the guidelines:

https://inyurl.com/2xapu9cx

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

The tax assessment period in the Czech Republic is 3 years (36 months). This means that, for example, in
June 2025, the last open tax period for assessments is May 2022.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration

The tax authorities will issue a penalty of 20% for all tax due as of the day of the breach of the registration
obligation, and interest of 8% per year plus (about 3.75%) Repo Rate of the Czech National Bank.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time

If the VAT return is not filed in time, the penalty is not more than CZK 300,000 (approximately EUR 12,000).
The penalty is calculated with a maximum of 5% of the VAT liability. Everyday arrears of 0.05% of the VAT
liability is accrued. The same applies for the VAT refund.

If the European sales listing is not filed in time, the VAT authorities can issue a fine of up to CZK 50,000
(approximately EUR 2,000).

If the national recapitulative statement is not filed in time, the VAT authorities will issue a fine from CZK
1,000 to CZK 50,000 (approximately EUR. 2,000).

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the VAT authorities

If the VAT is not paid in time, the VAT authorities must assess an interest on the VAT in arrears calculated as
8% per year plus Repo Rate of the Czech National Bank, Maximum interest is for 5 years of arrears, If the
VAT is paid within 5 days after the date it should have been paid, no penalty will be issued.

The Czech Republic | 149

--- Page 150 ---
10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly
If the VAT is not declared up to the correct amount, the VAT authorities will also impose a penalty. If the VAT
is paid based on a voluntarily disclosure, no penalty is imposed. In other cases, the penalty is 20% of the
increased VAT liability or decreased VAT refund,

10.3.5 Offence penalties

In case of gross negligence, a penalty up to CZK 500,000 can be levied. This applies for intentional
noncompliance with VAT registration obligation or omission to keep the VAT ledger.

10.4 Intrastat
In the Czech Republic, separate monthly Intrastat retums must be submitted to the Customs Office.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

The Intrastat threshold separately for both the arrivals and the dispatches is C7K 15,000,000 (approximately
EUR 600,000) in the calendar year.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

+ EU Member State of dispatch or destination;

+ reference period (i.e. the reporting month);

+ nature of the movement of the goods (i.e. arrival or dispatch);

+ HS code (8-digit harmonised commodity code according to the Combined Nomenclature (CN);
+ invoice value of the goods in CZK;

+ quantity of the goods (ie. net weight in kilograms);

* other measure for certain goods (e.g. litres, thousands of pieces, or number of pairs);

+ country of origin (e.g. where the goods are produced, made or processed);

+ nature of the transaction (e.g. purchase, sale, return of goods, relocation, etc.);

+ means of transport, (e.g. by road or by sea);

+ delivery conditions based on Incoterms 2020; and

+ VAT registration number of the recipient of the goods for delivery to another EU Member State.

Intrastat data for distance sales

It is obligatory to file an Intrastat for distance sales in case the distant sales are reported in the Czech VAT
return. In case the distant sales are taxable in the EU member state of the consumer, and are not subject to
Czech VAT, the value must be reported in a special box 24 of the VAT return, for all distant sales to all above
mentioned EU Member States on one amount.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

10.5.1 Special withholding scheme - split payment

Article 109a of the Czech VAT Act provides a special withholding (split payment) scheme which must be
considered for supplies carried out by ‘unreliable taxable persons’ in the Czech Republic in special cases.
Accordingly, the customer is allowed to withhold the VAT on the transaction and to pay it to the Czech tax
authorities in the name and in behalf of the supplier.

If the withholding scheme applies, the customer will transfer the net consideration to the supplier whereas
the VAT will be paid to the supplier’s responsible tax authority on a special account. If the customer does not
make a split payment he can be held liable by the tax authority for the VAT unpaid by the supplier.

13 VAT Grouping

VAT grouping is possible in Denmark, Generally, supplies of goods and services between the members of the
group are outside the scope of VAT. Further, supplies to or from one or more group members towards third
parties are treated as being supplied by the group, and not by the individual group member.

In order to form a VAT group, the members of the group must all be:

+ established in Denmark;

+ VATttaxable persons or; and

+ non-taxable persons, fully owned (by direct or indirect ownership of all shares etc.) by a company
included in the VAT group.

Foreign companies cannot be included in the VAT group unless they have a fixed establishment in Denmark
in the form of a branch.

VAT grouping is optional.

When forming a VAT group in Denmark, the members of the VAT group hold one common VAT number

for all future purposes. Thus, a single VAT retum is filed by the group consolidating the VAT accounts for

all group members. The common VAT number is also the relevant VAT number for all group members with
respect to EU sales and EU acquisitions. This will create a problem for validation purposes given that only
the name of the representative member appears on the EC VIES VAT number validation website. It will thus
not be possible to validate through the website the VAT registration number of the other members of the VAT
group, given that their names do not appear on the validation confirmation, However, it is possible to show
documentation that these companies form part of the VAT group.

‘The members of the VAT group are jointly liable for VAT.

14 Fiscal representative

For EU taxable persons and taxable persons established in the Faroe Islands, Greenland, Iceland, Norway, UK
and the Isle of Man, fiscal representation is optional and these taxable persons are accordingly not obliged

to appoint a fiscal representative. ‘The fiscal representative is not jointly liable for VAT in case BU taxable
persons or taxable persons from the Faroe Islands, Greenland, Iceland, Norway, UK and the Isle of Man opt
to appoint one.

However taxable persons established in the Faroe Islands, Greenland, Iceland, Norway, UK and the Isle of
Man must appoint a fiscal representative who is jointly liable for import.

Non-EU taxable persons, on the other hand, are liable to appoint a fiscal representative jointly liable for VAT
due to the Danish tax authorities when registering for VAT in Denmark. Normally a bank guarantee or other
type of security is required by the representative.

152 | Denmark

--- Page 153 ---
‘The fiscal representative is required to file a VAT retum for non-EU taxable persons as well as to keep
separate accounts for all non-EU taxable persons which the fiscal representative represents.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of business

In the event of a transfer of a going concer, either wholly or partially, no taxable supply of goods or services
is considered to have taken place. In order to apply this rule, the following conditions must be met:

+ the transferee is VAT registered or will become so at the time of the transfer;

+ all, or an autonomous part, of a business (assets, stocks, goodwill) is transferred;

+ the transferee continues, without a substantial discontinuation, to carry out a business of the same nature;
+ the transferor ceases the activity; and

+ the transferor notifies the Tax Agency of the transfer within eight days.

Although the transfer of a business that meets these legal conditions is not taxed with VAT, there are VAT
consequences. The taxable person who takes over the business of the transferring entity takes over all rights
and obligations, claims, liabilities and responsibilities for VAT such as the remainder of the adjustment
periods for (im)movable tangible property (see also paragraph 9.3).

2.2 Supplies free of charge

‘The free supply of ‘gifts of small value’ is not VAT-taxable in Denmark if the purchase price per item is less
than DKK 100 (approximately EUR 13,50) excluding VAT.

The free supply of ‘product samples’ are also not VAT-taxable in Denmark. The ‘product samples’ must
be provided for marketing purposes. ‘Product samples’ can, for example, be handed out as free product in
connection with:

* recruitment of new customers;

+ recording of orders;

* introduction of new products; and

+ sales campaigns for already marketed products

“Product samples’ can be ordinary copies of the product but will often be a special edition in a smaller format.
In contrast to ‘gifts of small value’, there is no limit of DKK 100 for “product samples’.

2.3. Importation

23.1 Importation followed by an intra-Community supply

‘The importation of goods which is followed by an intra-Community supply is VAT-exempt, if the supply to
the other EU Member State is VAT-exempt. The importer must be VAT registered in Denmark.

‘To avoid fraud, the importer must have informed the Danish Tax Agency of its VAT identification number
- or if not established in Denmark - the VAT identification number of the fiscal representative before the
importation. Also, the VAT identification number of the company to which the goods are delivered must be
communicated to the Danish Tax Agency.

Furthermore, the authorities might demand documentation verifying that the intra~Community supply to
another EU Member State actually has taken place.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation

Postponed accounting on importation is possible in Denmark by choosing the Danish tax authorities warranty
scheme or similar (i.e. bank guarantee). The importer is then not required to settle VAT at the time of
importation, Instead, reporting and possible payment of any VAT due on the importation will be settled via the
importer’s general, periodic Danish VAT return,

Denmark | 153

--- Page 154 ---
As VAT may be settled this way instead of settling VAT’ al the lime of importation, the importer may at the
same lime and in the same VAT return deduct VAT on the importation according to the importer’s general
Danish VAT deductibility. Thus, in cases of a full VAT deductibility on the importation, there will be no VAT
to settle on the importation and no loss of liquidity.

Any importer of goods from non-EU companies into Denmark must apply for an import licence. Non-EU
companies are required to have a fiscal representative. The deferment of VAT on importation is only possible
if the foreign company is VAT registered in Denmark.

In many situations, importers are obliged to register for excise duty purposes and pay the duty on a periodical
basis instead of at the time of importation. Some examples: alcoholic beverages, batteries, chocolate, tobacco,
coffee, nuts and others.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

The Danish rules concerning goods on consignment and call-off stock are in line with the EU VAT Directive.

Transfer of own goods to a consignment or call off stock in Denmark does not trigger VAT registration or

VAT at the time of transfer under the following conditions (among others):

+ the seller and the buyer are taxable persons and have entered into a consignment agreement;

+ the seller is not established in Denmark;

+ the buyer is VAT registered in Denmark, and buyer’s identity and VAT number is known by seller at the
time of dispatch; and

+ the seller registers the transfer of the goods in a register and indicates the buyer’s identity and VAT number.

When the conditions are met, the seller is considered to have made an exempt intra-Community supply at
the time the customer removes the goods from the stock. The customer must report an intra~-Communily
acquisition.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

‘The Danish VAT rules tefer to the supply of goods with installation or assembly, but do not provide specific,
formal definitions of the terms “installation” or “assembly”.

In practice, however, such supplies commonly refer to placing/installing, integration, the laying down of more
permanent elements (c.g. pipes), setting up something and, of course, assembling.

Ifa foreign supplier installs and/or assembles goods in Denmark, the reverse charge applies if the customer
is a taxable person established in Denmark or he is not established in Denmark, but he has a Danish VAT
umber.

The handing over of certain construction works are treated as a service.

33 Distance sales threshold

From 2021, the rules and thresholds for distance sales (both intra~-Community distance sales and distance
sales of imported goods) have been harmonized across the EU with no leeway for Member States. The
distance selling threshold for sales to customers in other EU Member States without a VAT registration
number (¢.g. private individuals) is now EUR 10,000 for intra-Commanity distance sales. Turnover
generated from the supply of ‘electronically supplied services’, telecommunication services and broadcasting
services are included in the EUR 10,000 threshold. A threshold of EUR 150 per consignment applies for the
application of the special scheme for distance sales of imported goods.

154 | Denmark

--- Page 155 ---
Once the threshold is exceeded the supplier may either register directly in Denmark or in their country of
establishment via One Stop Shop (OSS) to account for Danish VAT for their distance sales to Denmark.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

The simplified triangulation rules in Denmark have been implemented in line with the BU VAT Directive.

Taxable persons A, B and C in the ABC transaction must be registered for VAT in different EU countries (i.e.
in countries 1, 2 and 3). The transport must be organized by either A or B. The goods must be transported
directly from A in country | to C in country 3

The simplified triangulation rules can be applied if (among other conditions):

+ Denmark is the country of artival of the goods:

+ the taxable persons participating in the transaction have been identified for VAT purposes in 3 different
Member States;

+ the middleman (B) is not established in Denmark; and

+ the middleman (B) issues an invoice without VAT but referring to a simplified triangulation and the VAT
liability of the last acquirer (C).

Where Denmark is the EU Member State of arrival of the goods, the wording of the Danish legislation does
not preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middleman (B) is VAT registered in Denmark or
in the country of dispatch. It does preclude the application of the triangulation rules if the middteman (B) is
established in Denmark.

The simplification can also be applied in the case of 4 parties where Denmark is the country of arrival and
parties C and D are VAT registered in Denmark. If party A or B arranges the intra-Community transport from
Ato D, party B may apply the simplification. Of course, also the other conditions must be fulfilled. Party C
must charge Danish VAT to party D.

35 Acquisition threshold

Non-taxable legal persons and taxable persons not registered for VAT (e.g. due to having VAT-exempt
activities) who are established in Denmark, are not liable to register for VAT on intra-Community acquisitions
as long as the total value of these acquisitions, excluding VAT, does not exceed DKK 80,000 (approximately
EUR 10,00) in the current calendar year or in the previous calendar year. Taxable persons not established in
Denmark are always liable to register for VAT in Denmark on intra-Community acquisitions.

3.6 Effective use and enjoyment

The purchase of the following services by a recipient who is established in Denmark, is not subject to Danish
VAT, if they are primarily used and enjoyed outside the EU:

* transfer and assignments of copyrights etc.;

+ advertising services;

* — services of consultants etc., lawyers, data processing and information;

+ obligations to refrain form pursuing or exercising a business activity or a right;
+ banking, financial and insurance transactions, except hire of safes;

+ supply of staff;

+ hiring out of movable tangible property, except all means of transport;

* transport and transmission of gas, electricity, heat and cold;

+ telecommunication (only B2B); and

* broadcasting services (only B2B).

If the services mentioned above are provided by a Danish taxable person to a taxable person established

outside the EU or a non-taxable person resident outside of Denmark (only the first 8), they are subject to VAT
in Denmark, if the foreign buyer primarily used and enjoyed the service in Denmark.

Denmark | 155

--- Page 156 ---
4. Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

4.1 Principles
The tax liability for most goods arises at the time of delivery or passage of title to the goods. For services the
tax liability arises at the time of completion of the service.

In cases where an invoice is issued for a delivery before or soon after the end of the delivery, the invoicing
time is considered the delivery time.

In cases where payment takes place in whole or in part before the end of the delivery, or before an invoice is
issued, the time of payment is considered the time of delivery.

There is no time limit for issuing invoices. Generally, the invoice should be issued at the time of delivery or
soon thereafter, and at any case no later than what is standard for the industry in question.

For VAT-exempt intra-Community supplies, the invoice must be issued no later than by the 15th day of the
month following the month of supply.
4.2 Advance payments

If payments are made up front, invoices for such payments must be issued.

4.3 Continuous supplies

In the case of delivery of goods or services that result in several consecutive settlements or payments, the time
of delivery is considered to be at the end of each of the periods to which these settlements or payments relate.

For continuous supplies of services within the EU, where the recipient of the services is liable for the VAT,
the time of supply is considered to be every last day of the calendar year, when the supply of the services has
extended over a period of more than 1 year without intermediate settlement or payment for the services, and
as long as the supply of the services is not completed.

4.4 Cash accounting

Denmark did not implement a cash accounting scheme which is based on Article 66 of the EU VAT Directive.
5. Taxable amount

5A Bad debt relief

Relief for payment of sales VAT on supplies made to a debtor unable to pay for the supply (bad debt relief)
has been implemented in Denmark.

In general, the loss must be identified/proved, either by being included in a bankruptcy, a forced or voluntary
debt agreement, a foreclosure or something similar.

VAT on bad debts of less than DKK 3,000 (approximately EUR 400) may be relieved if the supplier has
effectively sought recovery of the outstanding amount from the debtor and generally provided that the
business relationship between the parties has been determined. For bad debts of more than DKK 3,000
(approximately EUR 400), certain conditions to the relief apply if not otherwise identified/proved according
to the above (i. either by being included in a bankruptcy, a forced or voluntary debt agreement, foreclosure
or similar).

The VAT is relieved through filing negative sales VAT on the VAT return once the loss is established or by a
reduction of the sales VAT.

156 | Denmark

--- Page 157 ---
In general, accounting records, etc. must clearly demonstrate how the outstanding claims are processed. If the
claim is subsequently paid in full or partially, the refunded VAT must be repaid to the Danish tax authorities.

5.2 Cash discounts

‘Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
25% VAT EUR_250
Total to be paid EUR 1,250

If payment is received within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount.”

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier must pay EUR 250 in its VAT return. The
customer can deduct EUR 250 in its VAT retum. Therefore, the supplier paid too much VAT, and the customer
deducted too much. The Danish supplier must issue a credit invoice for the 2%.

5.3 Open Market Value

With regards to the options afforded in Article 80 of the EU VAT Directive, Denmark has implemented the
open market value/cost price in transactions between related parties. This allows the Danish Tax Agency to
implement the open market value/cost price in the following three situations:

+ the consideration for a supply subject to VAT is lower than the cost price, and the recipient of the supply
does not have a full right of VAT deduction;

+ the consideration for a VAT-exempt supply is lower than the cost price and the supplier has a partial right
of VAT deduction;

+ the consideration for a or supply subject to VAT is higher than the normal value and the supplier has a
partial right of VAT deduction.

‘There is, asa starting point, no definition of the concept ‘transactions between related parties’, but there is a
presumption that parties are related within the meaning of the Danish VAT Act when:

> one of the parties has a financial interest in the other parties business or property;

+ the parties are closely connected through family or other close personal ties; and

+ the parties are closely linked through legal, management or membership ties.

54 Returnable packaging

Denmark has not implemented any of the options afforded in Article 92 of the EU VAT Directive with regards
to the cost of returnable packing materials, There are thus no relevant special provisions in place. Normally,
the packaging is included in the taxable amount. If returned, the supplier issues a credit note or the buyer
issues an invoice to the seller for the packaging.

6. VAT rates
61 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate in Denmark is 25%.

6.2 Reduced rate

Denmark does not apply reduced rates or differentiated VAT rates for certain products. The VAT rate of 25%
is thus fixed.

6.3 Super-reduced and zero rates

6.3.1 Zero rate

6.3.2 Super reduced rate

Denmark does not apply reduced rates or differentiated VAT rates for certain products. The VAT rate of 25%
is thus fixed,

wh Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with right to deduction (zero rate)

In general, the following supplies of goods and services are zero rated:

+ exports, intra-Community supplies and transfer of goods, including under certain conditions the supply of
goods liable to excise duties and goods supplied to travelers who are residents outside the EU;

* certain sea vessels and aircraft and a number of related goods and services;

+ supplies made to customers with (diplomatic) tax immunity;

* supplies made to NATO, international organizations ete.;

+ supplies of monetary gold to the National Bank of Denmark;

+ supplies of services directly linked to exports or imports under customs duty relicf;

* certain intermediate services linked to zero-rated supplies;

+ supply of newspapers;

+ goods supplied under a customs duty relief or VAT warehousing; and

+ new vehicles transported to another EU country.

7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies
The conditions for the exemption are as follows:

When a supplier performs an intra-Community supply from Denmark, the supplier must ensure that the
person acquiring the goods or services has a valid EU VAT registration (other than in Denmark). This is a
new condition which was implemented as a result of the Quick Fixes. Without the valid VAT identification
number, the supplier is not able to apply the exemption. The supplier must also hold proof that the goods have
been transported to another EU country.

Validation of the buyer’s VAT number is a material requirement in being able to deliver goods and services
without applying VAT to customers in other EU countries. All active EU VAT identification numbers can

be checked in the EU’s VIES database. The inquiry number and customer VAT number must be stored in

the debtor directory. The verification in VIES must be done regularly and at least once every quarter during
continuous trading ~ on occasional deliveries the customer’s VAT number must be validated in VIES prior to
each delivery. This must be an actual VAT number registered in the VIES system.

In addition to the validation of the VAT number itself, the data associated with the VAT number must be
compared with the company’s own registration information on the customer. If formalities are not in order,
the company risks — in addition to a fine — to be charged VAT on the supply unless:

+ the seller can prove to have acted in good faith; and

+ acorrection of the error is made to the relevant authorities.

If' customer claims to be registered for VAT — but this cannot be confirmed in VIES ~ you can request
confirmation from the customer's local tax authority. If you cannot get a VAT number verified and you are
also in doubt as to whether a buyer has a valid VAT number, VAT must be charged on the sale.

7.1.2 Export supplies

In the case of exports, the supplier must hold proof that the goods have actually been transported out of the
EU territory. The confirmed electronic customs declaration, freight or other commercial documents may be
used.

158 | Denmark

--- Page 159 ---
7.1.3 Warehousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Denmark has introduced VAT/Excise warehousing with respect to certain goods (Annex V of the EU VAT
Directive).

A taxable person not established in Denmark can also place goods in a VAT/Excise warehouse. Where
required, a taxable person can register or appoint a VAT representative (see paragraph 1.4) to store goods in,
or remove goods from, the warehouse. If certain conditions are met, the supply of goods that are intended
to be placed within a VAT warehouse is not subject to VAT and thus no VAT registration is required. The
customer must provide the supplier with a certificate that he intends to place the goods in a VAT warehouse.
This must be done no later than the time of acquisition or supply, and the certificate should be kept in the
records of the taxable person.

The VAT warehouse keeper must record all receipts of goods into the warehouse and maintain a detailed
record of all stock kept in the warehouse. ‘There are detailed requirements relating to the movement of the
stock. The services of the warehouse keeper relating to safeguarding goods, as well as any maintenance or
repackaging services on those goods, are zero rated.

VAT becomes chargeable at the time the goods are released from the VAT warehouse regime. Certain releases
are not subject to VAT, such as, exports or intra-Community supplies, or the temporary removal for the
purpose of repair, processing or treatment. In such instances, prior authorisation is required.

7.1.3.2. Excise warehouse
See paragraph 7.1.3.1 above.

7.1.3.3 Bonded (customs) warehouse

Goods imported from non-EU countries may be placed in a bonded (customs) warehouse in Denmark. A sale
within a bonded (customs) warehouse is VAT exempt. Once the goods are released from such customs duty
suspension and released into free circulation in Denmark, they must be declared for VAT purposes.

The release of goods from a bonded warehouse regime does not constitute an importation if the goods are
subsequently placed under another customs regime

Denmark also has a customs-free zone, the Copenhagen Freeport. A sale of goods and ancillary services
within the territory of the port are not subject to Danish VAT. However, VAT and customs duties must be paid
when goods are removed from the Copenhagen Freeport for sale within Denmark. Certain formalities apply if
goods are removed from the Copenhagen Freeport for sale and delivery outside of Denmark.

7.3 Option for taxation

For the lease and sublease of premises for business purposes (not for housing purposes) and for the supply of
immovable property it is possible to opt for taxation. Once opted, the option must stay in place for at least two
calendar years. It is not possible to opt for financial services.

8. Liability

81 Reverse charge

In Denmark there are several obligatory reverse charge rules besides the reverse charge for general B2B
services provided by foreign entities to taxable persons and legal entities with a Danish VAT number
established in Denmark. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and follows from Articles 44
and 196 EU VAT Directive, In addition, there is also the reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and
clectricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to “taxable dealers” in Denmark,

According to the Danish VAT rules, several other supplies are liable to reverse charges.

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism - non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)

Denmark did implement a reverse charge mechanism on the supply of goods with installation. The reverse

charge applies if the supplier is not established in Denmark and the customer of the installation supply is a

taxable person established in Denmark. The reverse charge also applies if the customer is not established in
Denmark but holds a Danish VAT number.

Denmark did not implement the reverse charge mechanism of Article 194 EU VAT Directive for the domestic
supply of goods.

Ifa non-established supplier performs a domestic supply of services to a taxable person or a non-taxable
legal entity, the reverse charge applies only for specific services: services relating to immovable property
(except bridge and tunnel tickets), restaurant and catering services on board of ships, airplanes and trains and
short-term lease of means of transport. The reverse charge applies if the customer of the services is a taxable
person, or a VAT registered non-taxable person resident or established in Denmark. The reverse charge even
applies if the customer is a foreign company holding a Danish VAT number.

The use of the reverse charge for Danish VAT is compulsory even if the foreign non-resident supplier holds a
Danish VAT number. It is not possible to opt for a Danish VAT registration in a situation where the customer
has to apply the reverse charge.

The supplier must refer on the invoice to the application of the reverse charge mechanism e.g. such as
“reverse Charge”. Furthermore, the customer’s VAT identification number must be stated on the invoice.
8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

Supplies of the following items are liable to domestic reverse charge rules:
+ CO, emission rights;

160 | Denmark

--- Page 161 ---
+ investment gold;

= scrap metal;

+ mobile phones;

+ integrated circuit devices; and

* game consoles, tablets and laptops.

Besides the supplies mentioned above, the following supplies are also liable to domestic reverse charge:
+ supply of electricity- and gas certificates to all VAT registered in Denmark (primarily companies).

‘The supplier must refer to the reverse charge if applied, e.g. by the text “reverse charge - customer settles the
VAT” or the like. In addition, reference to the customer’s VAT number must be made.

8.2 i nt and several liability

Joint and several liability exists for businesses that form part of a VAT group for the VAT debts of each
member of the VAT group for the entire period that the relevant entity was part of that VAT group.

If a company is registered with a fiscal representative, the company and the representative are jointly and
severally liable for payment of the VAT. The fiscal representative is not jointly liable for VAT in case EU
taxable persons or taxable persons from the Faroe Islands, Greenland, Iceland, Norway, UK and the Isle of
Man opt to appoint a fiscal representative.

Joint and several liability also exists in situations where, for example, there is carousel fraud.

9. Recovery of input VAT

Generally, taxable persons may deduct input VAT on their purchases unless the purchases are related to VAT-
exempt supplies not allowing input VAT deduction

‘Companies that have both VAT-taxable and VAT-exempt activities may deduct input VAT on purchases that
are used exclusively for the VAT-taxable activities. Conversely, companies may not deduct input VAT on
purchases that are used exclusively for VAT-exempt activities.

For those purchases which relate to both VAT-taxable and VAT-exempt activities, companies are entitled to a
partial VAT deduction.

With a partial VAT deduction, companies can deduct a proportional share of the input VAT corresponding to
the ratio between the company’s VAT-taxable turnover and the company’s total turnover (the VAT deduction
percentage). This partial deduction percentage is always rounded up to the nearest whole number.

For purchases that are used both in the VAT-taxable company and privately, companies are entitled to a partial
VAT deduction. When private use is also included, the partial VAT deduction cannot be calculated based on
aturmover distribution. The VAT deduction must instead be determined on the basis of an estimate of the VAT-
taxable use of the purchase in question. The company must be able to provide documentation for the chosen
estimate.

91 Non-recoverable input VAT

The input VAT is generally not recoverable in case of:

+ free meals for staff and owner of the company if not for business/meeting purposes;

+ business gifts of more than DKK 100;

+ housing expenses, kindergarten costs, holiday homes etc. for staff and the owner of the company;

+ entertainment and representation (except hotel and restaurant costs). It is possible to deduct 25% of the
VAT on restaurant costs if strictly for business purposes;

+ purchase and running costs for passenger cars. The VAT on long-term leasing (more than 6 months during
which the car is used in connection with taxable activities for more than 10% of the time) is however
partly recoverable (special rules).

Denmark | 161

--- Page 162 ---
9.1.1 Partially recoverable input VAT

Input VAT on services related to restaurant services strictly for business purposes is deductible for 25% of the
VAT. Input VAT on hotel (rooms only) strictly for business purposes is deductible for 100%.

9.2 Excess input VAT

Repayment of VAT according to the taxable person’s VAT filing is generally made by the Danish tax
authorities within one week of the filing deadline.

Refund applications must be filed electronically to the competent authority in the taxable person's home

Member State.

Mote information about this should be obtained from the taxable person’s home country authorities. For

‘The minimum period for making a claim for refund of Danish VAT is a calendar quarter, or the rest of the year
if the claim is filed aller the Ist of October. The maximum period for making a claim for refund of Danish
VAT is one calendar year, The annual minimum limit is DKK 400 (approximately EUR $4) and the quarterly
minimum limit is DKK 3,000 (approximately EUR 400).

The deadline is the 30" of September in the year following the year in which the cost was incurred.

The Danish tax authorities may request supporting documents and original invoices to support the claim.
Aclaimant should, in general, receive the Danish Tax Agency’s decision on the refund of VAT within four
months after the receipt of the application. Any repayment may be expected immediately if the claim is
accepted.

If additional information is requested this time frame may be prolonged.
