10. Administrative Obligations

10.1 Invoicing

10.1.1 Invoice content

The invoice must generally contain the following information:

«invoice number (sequential);

+ date at which the invoice is issued;

+ name, address and registration number (equals VAT identification number in Denmark) of the supplier;

* name and address of the customer;

+ aclear description of the goods or services supplied including weight and price:

+ the VAT amount;

+ the date at which the supply took place, if different, from the invoice date;

+ the VAT rate;

* the VAT base, price per unit excluding VAT, any discounts etc. that may not otherwise be included in the
price per unit;

+ name, address and VAT number of the fiscal representative if applicable;

* in the case of

+ self-billing a corresponding reference “selvfakturering” is required;

+ an intra-Community supply or the reverse charge, the invoice must state the VAT identification
number of the customer, and it should refer to the intra-Community supply or reverse charge (i.e. B2B
service: “Service not subject to Danish VAT according to Article 44 VAT Directive; VAT due by the
recipient of the service according to Article 196 VAT Directive”, “omvendt betalingspligt”);

+ the information necessary to determine whether there is a new means of transport; and
+ an indication of the fact that a margin scheme has been applied as well as an indication why the margin

scheme has been applied, e.g:

+ margin scheme - second-hand goods “fortjenstmargenordning -br_ugte genstande”

+ margin scheme - works of art “fortjenstmargenordning -k unstgenstande”

«= margin scheme - collector’s items and antiques “/ortjenstmargenordning -s amlerobjekter og antik”

* tour operator margin scheme “fortjenstmargenordning + ejsebureauer,

Further, all invoices must clearly state all VAT-exempt supplies, if any. In the case of both VAT-taxable and
VAT-exempt supplies, the invoice must clearly show which supplies are taxable with VAT. VAT-taxable and
VAT-exempt supplies must be stated separately and summed up in either VAT category.

10.1.2 Time limits

Generally, the invoice should be issued at the time of supply or soon thereafter, and at any case no later than
‘what is standard for the industry in question.

--- Page 164 ---
Tf payments are made up front, invoices for such payments must be issued.

For VAT-exempt intra-Community supplies, the invoice must be issued no later than by the 15th day of the
month following the month of supply.

10.1.3 Currency

The invoice may be issued in any currency. However, the VAT amount must be stated in either DKK or EUR.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

Either an ECB latest exchange rate or a customs rate (the average monthly rate) may be used. The chosen
exchange rate must be indicated in a memo note in the taxable person’s administration and will be binding for
a two-year period.

10.1.5 Self- 2

Self-billing is allowed if all the following conditions are met:
+ there must be a written agreement in place between the two parties;

+ the supplier must accept each invoice according to written procedures; and
+ the supplier must be registered for VAT purposes.

The self-billing invoice must clearly read “selt-billing” or “se/vfakturering” and the customer’s VAT number
must be stated on the invoice.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice must contain the following information:

+ date of issue;

+ name, address, and VAT number of the supplier;

+ invoice number (sequential);

+ aclear description of the goods or services supplied including weight and price; and

+ total amount payable and VAT amount (VAT amount can be indicated as 20% of the total amount VAT
incl.)

It is possible to issue a simplified invoice if the amount of the invoice is not higher than DKK 3,000 or the
invoice issued is a document or message which changes the initial invoice and a specific and unambiguous
reference to the initial invoice is made with a specific mention of the details that are amended.

10.1.7 Storage of invoices

Invoices must generally be kept available for 5 years. However, when relating to immovable property, the
retention period is 10 years. Also, the administration with respect to electronic-, telecom- and broadcasting
services must be kept for 10 years.

In general, accounting data (including invoices) may be stored electronically, for as long as it is possible to
print hard copies thereof within the retention period without further adjustment.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Electronic invoicing is allowed under the following conditions:

+ the company must by any means of its own choice ensure the authenticity of origin, integrity and
readability of the invoice from the time of issuance throughout the storage period; and

+ the recipient must accept receiving invoices electronically.

APDF file of the invoice sent via e-mail is sufficient.
All public organisations (such as Ministries, municipalities, regions and non-departmental public bodies)
are obliged to be able to receive and process e-Invoicing. E-Invoices can be submitted to the government by

using a EAN invoice. An EAN invoice is sent electronically through the NemHandelsregistret. It includes an
EAN number which identifies the public entity that will receive the invoice.

164 | Denmark

--- Page 165 ---
10.2 Compliance

10.2.1. VAT return period

VAT must be filed either monthly, quarterly, or twice a year, depending on the annual turnover of the

taxable person:

+ annual turnover, excluding VAT, of less than DKK 5,000,000 (approximately EUR 67,100) - half-yearly
VAT returns

+ annual turnover between DKK 5,000,000 (approximately EUR 61,100 ) and DKK 50,000,000
(approximately EUR 6,7 1,000) - quarterly VAT returns

+ annual turnover, excluding VAT, of more than DKK 50,000,000 (approximately EUR 6,7 1,000) -
monthly VAT returns

Notwithstanding this, newly registered companies are generally required to file VAT returns on a quarterly
basis at the beginning of their VAT-taxable activities in Denmark. However, if the annual turnover in
Denmark, excluding VAT, is expected to exceed DKK 50,000,000 (approximately EUR 6,7 1,000), the
company is immediately required to file monthly.

Electronic filing of VAT returns is mandatory.

10.2.2 Filing of a VAT return and payment
‘The deadline for filing of VAT returns for both local and non-resident companies depends on the filing period.

The deadline for filing a monthly VAT return is, generally, the 25" of the month after the end of the VAT-
period. The filing deadline has one exception for the June VAT return. The tax authorities announce the June
deadline each year and it is usually around 18 August. This is due to the summer vacation period.

The deadline for filing quarterly VAT returns is, generally, the first day of the third month following the end of
the VAT period.

The deadline for filing half-yearly VAT returns is, generally, the first day of the third month following the end
of the VAT period.

If the VAT returns fall due on a Saturday, Sunday or a holiday, the deadline will be the next business day.
Any VAT due should be paid by the date of the filing deadline for the VAT return.
It is possible to file VAT returns electronically via

https://tinyurl.com/3b3r53rk

10.2.3. European sales listing

‘The European sales listing for both goods and services must be filed monthly. There is no threshold for the
filing of the European sales listing, not for goods and not for services.

Enropean sales listings must be submitted electronically with the same deadlines (monthly) and via the same
link (see section 10.2.2 above), where the VAT return is submitted.

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement

A national recapitulative statement does not need be filed.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

The statute of limitations in Denmark is, in general, 3 years but may be extended to 10 years in cases of gross
negligence or criminal intent of the business etc.

Denmark | 165

--- Page 166 ---
Further the limitation period is suspended until such time SKAT has sufficient knowledge to make a claim for
payment of VAT or repayment of refunded VAT.

10.3 Penalties

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
Normally there are no penalties for late registration.

10.3.2 Default penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing in time

If VAT retums are not filed on time, the Danish tax authorities will issue a preliminary assessment for the
company. A fee of DKK 800 (approximately EUR 105) will be charged to the taxable person, who is still
liable to file a correct VAT return for the VAT period in question,

If the European sales listing is not filed on time, a reminder fee of DKK 65 (approximately EUR 8,50) might
be charged to the company.

Further, in both cases, fincs in the amount of DKK 1,000 (approximately EUR 135) and upwards may be
imposed depending on the misconduct and repetition thereof.

10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the VAT authorities

Penalties for late payment of VAT to the Danish tax authorities are:

+ late payment interest (in 2024: 0,% monthly) calculated and charged daily until the amount has been
paid; and
+ a fee of DKK 65 (approximately EUR 8.50) per reminder.

Other penalties/fines up to DKK 10,000 (approximately EUR 1,350) may be imposed.

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

If VAT is not reported correctly, i.e. VAT is reported for less than the outstanding amount, the same penalties
as outlined in paragraph 10.3.2 may be imposed, as well as criminal penalties in case of fraud.

10.3.5 Offence penalties

In case of gross negligence or intention, the taxable person may be subject to a fine of one to two times of the
total evaded amount, detention or imprisonment for up to two years.

Some penalties may be waived if the correction is made on the taxable person’s own initiative, provided that
the violation had not been committed intentionally.

10.3.5.1 Interest on VAT claims due

From 1 July 2023, the Danish Tax Agency charge interest from companies, that are already correctly VAT
registered, in the following situations:

+ the company is registered late (registration with retroactive effect) and thus submits its first VAT return
late;

+ when the company declares and pays VAT late;

+ when the company, on its own initiative, makes corrections in previous VAT declarations, where the VAT
base is increased (in favor of the Danish Tax Agency); or

* when the Danish Tax Agency makes corrections, where the VAT base is increased (in the Danish Tax
Agency’s favor).

‘The interest is 11.35% per year of the VAT claim due and is calculated from the original reporting deadline
until the correct amount is paid.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

‘The Intrastat threshold for arrivals (imports) from other EU-countries and Northern Ireland is in 2025 more

‘The Intrastat threshold for dispatches (exports) to other EU-countries and Northern Ireland is in 2025 more
than DKK 11,300,000 (approximately EUR 1,506,666).

Please note, these thresholds are changed on a yearly basis.

Companies with annual dispatches (exports) between DKK 11,300,000 and DKK 16,500,000 (approximately
EUR 1,506,666 — EUR 2,200,000) and an annual import of less than DKK 41,000,000 (approximately

EUR 5,466,666) can report their dispatches simplified. A simplified report means, that it will be voluntary

to report the product code, the product's country of origin, the nature of the transaction, weight and any
supplementary unit (see paragraph 10.4.2 below),

+ the line number;

+ the commodity/tariff code or customs classification code of the goods;
a code indicating the EU Member State of departure of the goods dispatched to Denmark, when filing
adeclaration for import;

+ acode indicating the EU Member State of destination of the goods dispatched from Denmark, when filing
a declaration for export;

* atransaction code;

+ the weight of the goods or the number of the specified units;

+ the value in DKK;

+ the VAT ID of the trading partner; and

+ the country of origin for dispatches.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

‘There are no additional requirements.

Estonia

The European Union (EU) VAT Directive is implemented in Estonia through the Value Added Tax (VAT) Act
(in Estonian: Kaibemaksuseadus). VAT in Estonian is “kaibemaks”.

‘The non-official translation of the Estonian VAT Act into English (in force on 1 January 2025) can be found at:
hitps.//tinyurl.com/ktxc3aut

is Taxable persons

il Registration threshold & SME Scheme for domestic taxable persons

Ifthe turnover exceeds EUR 40,000 as calculated from the beginning of a calendar year, an obligation to

register as a taxable person for VAT purposes will arise for the person as of the date on which the supply

reaches that amount. As of 1 January 2025, the following supplies, for which the place of supply is Estonia,

are relevant for registration threshold purposes:

+ taxable supply of goods and services, including the taxable supply subject to the zero VAT rate, excluding
the transfer of fixed assets;

+ wansactions with immovable property, excluding the transfer of fixed assets and occasional transactions;
and

+ supply of insurance services and financial services, excluding occasional services.

The registration obligation does not arise if all the supplies are VAT exempt and taxable with the zero VAT

rate, except for intra-Community supply of goods.

The application should be presented to the Estonian Tax and Customs Board within 3 working days.

However, a person may submit the application for registration as a taxable person to the tax authority even if
the registration obligation has not yet arisen.

The application form for VAT registration can be downloaded at: hitps.//tinyurl.com/33-447m3x

Application for VAT registration can be submitted at the service bureau of Estonian Tax and Customs Board,
sent per e-mail (if it is possible to digitally sign the form) or filed online in the e-services environment of
Estonian Tax and Customs Board.

‘The Estonian Tax and Customs Board may also ask for proof that business activities are performed or will be
performed (e.g. business plans, contracts, etc.). The VAT number is provided within 5 working days once all
the required documents have been submitted.

A retroactive registration is also possible but only if the date of obligatory registration has been passed, and
applies from the date on which the registration should have taken place according to the Estonian VAT Act.

12 Registration threshold & SME Scheme for foreign taxable persons

There is no registration threshold for foreign taxable persons, If'a foreign taxable person with no fixed
establishment in Estonia makes a taxable supply in Estonia and the reverse charge does not apply,

the foreign person is obliged to register as a taxable person for VAT purposes as of the date on which

the taxable supply was made (unless special SME scheme can be used, see below). The VAT registration
application must be submitted within 3 working days of the date on which the VAT registration obligation
arises.

Anon-established business that has a fixed establishment in Estonia must register for VAT under the same
conditions as Estonian resident businesses.

--- Page 170 ---
‘The registration obligation does not arise if all taxable supplies of the foreign taxable person are taxable
with the zero VAT rate, except in case of an intra-Community supply of goods and the supply to an online
marketplace which is preceded by a transfer of goods.

Non-established businesses must apply for a VAT registration with Estonian Tux and Customs Board

in person. However, an authorised person may acl on behalf of the taxable person, provided proof of
authorisation is submitted. Forcign persons are registered as taxable persons by the Tallinn service bureau

From | January 2025, small businesses from other Member States can request the application of the SME
scheme in Estonia, whereby they are relieved of the registration obligation in Estonia, provided (hey meet the
following criteria:

+ the total annual turnover in all of the Member States (including the Member State of establishment) in the
current and previous calendar year does not exceed EUR 100,000 and
+ the annual turnover in Estonia in the current and previous calendar year does not exceed EUR 40,000.

Application to apply the SME scheme in Estonia is filed in the Member State of establishment. Upon
approval, a registration number (‘EX number)’ is issued to the applicant. Applying the SME scheme is
voluntary.

From the time of exceeding the threshold, application of SME scheme is suspended for a period of 1 calendar
year, Re-application can be filed thereafter provided the respective threshold requirements are met.

13 VAT grouping

|A VAT group may consist of entities which are engaged in business in Estonia and are:

+ a parent company and its subsidiaries within the meaning of the Estonian Commercial Code; or

+ economically and organisationally related taxable persons, where, at least, 50% of the shares, holding
or votes of each enlity are owned by one and the same person, or if the persons are bound by a franchise
agreement.

VAT grouping is voluntary, which means that a taxable person may choose whether or not to register a VAT
group or whether or not to join a VAT group if all the conditions of the VAT grouping are fulfilled. If it has
been decided to become a VAT group, one is not bound by a specific time limit to dissolve the VAT group
again.

A taxable person may belong to only one VAT group at any one time.

Transactions between the VAT group members are not considered as supplies. Members of the VAT group
must submit VAT returns and European sales listings together (i.e. single reporting) and will be jointly and
severally liable for payment of VAT by the due date.

‘The VAT group members are granted only one VAT number for transactions with non-group members. The
VAT group must appoint one member as its representative to communicate with the tax authorities, The
personal VAT registration number granted to each member (except the VAT number of the representative of
the VAT group) will be suspended until such time as the VAT group is deregistered.

170 | Estonia

--- Page 171 ---
1.4 Fiscal representative

A taxable person from another EU country may appoint a fiscal representative, but it is not obligatory. A
taxable person from a non-EU country (other than from a non-EU country with which a mutual assistance
agreement has been concluded with), who has no fixed establishment in Estonia, must appoint a fiscal
representative when registering for VAT.

A fiscal representative needs to be licenced by the Estonian Tax and Customs Board. The fiscal representative
can be a legal person established in Estonia or a branch of a foreign legal person entered in the Estonian
Commercial Register. A fiscal representative must be solvent, have an impeccable reputation and must not
have any tax arrears.

All rights and obligations of the foreign taxable person extend to the fiscal representative. The fiscal
representative is required to ensure that the principal's monetary and non-monetary obligations concerning
taxes are fulfilled within the set term and in full, i.e. is jointly and severally liable for all the tax liabilities of
the foreign taxable person it represents. As a general rule, the fiscal representative must post a bank guarantee.
The tax authorities determine the amount of the guarantee.

2. Taxable transactions

21 Transfer of a business

According to Estonian VAT Act, a transfer of a business or a part thereof within the meaning of the Law of
Obligations Act, is not a taxable transaction. This does not apply in case of a transfer of a single asset.

2.2 Supplies free of charge
Estonia generally applies the principles laid down in Articles 16 and 26 of the EU VAT Directive

Based on the VAT Act, gifts which are provided for advertising purposes and whose value does not exceed
EUR 10 (excluding VAT) does not constitute a supply of goods.

According to the VAT Act, the use ofa car for purposes other than business, shall not be a deemed self-supply
(instead a limitation on the recovery of input VAT is applied), except where the car is mainly used for the
carriage of passengers for a charge or for driving lessons.

2.3. Importation

2.3.1 Importation followed by intra~-Community supply

It is possible to apply a VAT exemption on the importation of goods into Estonia if it is followed by an intra-

Community supply, provided that te following conditions are met:
the importer of the goods - or a customs agency acting as a representative thereof - is an Estonian taxable
person;

+ immediately after the goods have been imported, they are transported in the same condition, to a taxable
person in another Member State;

+ an intra~Community supply is created as a result of the transport of the goods to another EU Member
State;

+ upon importation of the goods, the importer of the goods - or a customs agency acting as a representative
thereof - proves the intention to transport the goods to a taxable person in another Member State and, after
the goods have been transported, provides the customs authority with documentation that proves the intra-
Community supply of the goods; and

*  asecurity is provided in order to secure the performance of the tax liability which may arise as a result of
failure to perform the tax obligation.

2.3.2 Postponed accounting on importation
The VAT Act allows the deferment of VAT on the importation of goods for all registered taxable persons

Estonia | 171

--- Page 172 ---
(foreign taxable persons as well) in Estonia when applied for, When using the import VAT deferment, import
VAT will be declared by the taxable person in the regular VAT return,

In order to apply for the deferment license, the taxable person must submit an application to the Estonian Tax
and Custom Board. Additionally, the following criteria must all be fulfilled:

+ the taxable person must have been VAT registered for at least the preceding 12 consecutive months;

+ the taxable person has not failed to submit tax returns on time within the preceding 12 months; and

+ the taxable person has not had any tax arrears in the previous 12 months.

The Estonian Tax and Customs Board will verify the compliance with the conditions within 30 days as of the
receipt of the written notice. Continued compliance with the respective conditions is subsequently checked by
the tax authority on a monthly basis.

3. Place of supply

3.1 Consignment and call-off stock

‘The transfer of goods by a VAT registered taxable person from one EU Member State to Estonia to create a
stock of goods from which their customer can ‘call-off? (use and pay for) the goods as and when they require
them, can be described as call-off stock. If certain conditions are met, the supplier does not perform a transfer
of own goods followed by a local supply, but an intracommunity supply to his customer at the time the goods
are called off.

The Estonian VAT legislation is in line with the provisions relating to call off stock in the BU VAT Directive
as introduced with the Quick Fixes package as of | January 2020.

3.2 _ Installation supplies

Under the Estonian VAT Act, a supply with installation means that the goods are transferred to a particular
EU Member State and installed or assembled by or on behalf of the transferor in that state, and the cost
of installation or assembly exceeds 5% of the taxable value of the transaction. If the above conditions are
fulfilled, then such goods are treated as supplied in the country where they are assembled or installed.

The domestic reverse charge may be applicable if the goods are installed or assembled in Estonia by a taxable
person from another EU Member State for a VAT registered customer.
3.3. Distance sales threshold

Estonia has implemented the changes of the Directive regarding distance sales as of 1 July 2021. The distance
selling threshold for sales to non-taxable persons (B2C) in other member States is EUR 10,000 in a calendar
year. This threshold covers sales to all Member States and also sales of electronically supplied services,
telecommunication services and broadcasting services.

Once the threshold is exceeded, the foreign company can register for VAT in Estonia or in their country of
establishment via One Stop Shop (OSS) regime to account for Estonian VAT for their distance sales to Estonia.

3.4 Simplified triangulation rules

Under Estonian VAT legislation, the simplified triangulation rules have been implemented in line with the EU
VAT Directive. In addition to this, the simplified triangulation rules can be applied if:

+ Estonia is the country of arrival for the goods; and
+ the middleman (B) is VAT registered in the Member State of dispatch.

However, the simplified triangulation rules cannot be applied if Estonia is the country of arrival of the goods
and the middleman is registered and/or established in Estonia.

172} Estonia

--- Page 173 ---
‘Therefore, where Estonia is the country of arrival of the goods, it is not possible to apply the triangulation
rules if middleman B is VAT registered in Estonia.

3.5 Acquisition threshold

The threshold for the acquisition of goods by non-taxable legal persons from suppliers established in another
Member State is EUR 10,000 calculated from the beginning of a calendar year. The obligation to register as a
taxable person with limited liability arises as of the date on which the threshold was exceeded. The threshold
does not apply on excise goods and new means of transport. This obligation does not apply to persons who
are not engaged in business.

Ifa foreign person engaged in business without a fixed establishment in Estonia, except a foreign person
making use of the tax exemption based on SME scheme, engages in an intra-Community acquisition of goods
in Estonia, the obligation to register as a taxable person with limited liability arises as of the date of the intra-
Community acquisition of the goods. This provision does not apply to intra~Community acquisition of goods
which is exempt from tax.

3.6 _Effective use and enjoyment
Estonia has not implemented any effective use and enjoyment rules based on Article 59a of the EU VAT
Directive.

4. Chargeable event & chargeability of VAT

41 Principles

‘Asa general rule, the time of supply

+ when the right to dispose of the goods was handed over to the purchaser or when the services have been
carried out; or

+ when full or partial payment is received for the goods or services.

The time of supply is determined proceeding from either of the conditions above which has been met first.

4.2 Advance payments

VAT can be chargeable before the time the goods are put at the disposal of the purchaser or the service is
completed. This is the case when the price has been totally or partially received before the goods are put at the
disposal of the purchaser or the services are supplied.

43 Continuous supplies

If the provision of services continues for longer than a period of taxation, the services are deemed to have
been provided and received during the taxable period in which the provision of the services is completed.

When services are rendered or regular transfers of goods are made to the same purchaser, the time at which
the goods are dispatched/made available to the purchaser or the time at which the services are provided /
received is deemed to be the taxable period entailing the end of the period for which an invoice is issued or
during which payment has been agreed to be made. This however cannot be later than twelve calendar months
from commencement. If the provision of services lasts for more than a year and the reverse charge is applied
then the service is deemed to have been received on 31 December of each calendar year (from commencing
the provision of the service) provided the services have not been paid for or completed earlier in the year.

4.4 Cash accounting

A taxable person whose turnover, the place of supply of which is Estonia, did not exceed EUR 200,000
in the previous calendar year nor as of the beginning of the current calendar year, may apply the special
arrangement for cash accounting for VAT.

Estonia | 173

--- Page 174 ---
A person shall notify in writing the Estonian Tax and Customs Board about the taxation period from which
the person shall commence using the cash accounting arrangement, either upon registration as a taxable
person, or at the latest during the taxation period prior to implementation of the special arrangement, The
calculation of the threshold of the taxable supply does not take into account the transfer of fixed assets and the
incidental transfer of immovable as good.

5. Taxable amount

5.1 Bad debt relief

A supplier can reclaim the VAT for a bad debt if all of the following conditions are met:

+ a VAT invoice has been issued;

+ VAT amount has been declared in the VAT return;

+ atleast 12 months, but not more than 3 years, have passed from the due date of payment of that VAT
invoice (except in the case of claim that exceeds EUR 30,000);

+ if the claim exceeds EUR 30,000, the court settlement to support the claim must have entered into force;
* the claim has been written off in accounting despite the efforts of the taxable person to make every effort
to collect the claim or the expenses incurred for its recovery exceed the estimated income receivable;

+ the debt claim has not been transferred;

+ the purchaser of goods or the recipient of services is not a related person within the meaning of the
Income Tax Act; and

+ the taxable person has informed the recipient of goods and services in writing about the write off in the
month of the write off and has specified the VAT amount therein.

The VAT reclaim can be made through the periodical VAT return.
Taxable persons must keep record of bad debts written off.

5.2 Cash discounts

Assume that the supplier sends the following invoice:

“Supply of goods EUR 1,000
22% VAT EUR 220
Total to be paid EUR 1,220

If payment is received by us within the next 14 days, you can subtract 2% of the invoice as a cash discount”.

Payment is indeed received within 14 days. In that case the supplier has to pay EUR 220 in his VAT return.
The customer can deduct EUR 220 in his VAT return, Therefore, the supplier paid too much VAT and the
customer deducted too much. Explanations on the website of the Estonian Tax and Customs Board state that
it is not possible to amend the taxable value of goods or services without annulling the original invoice or
issuing a credit invoice in cases the discount is given after the supply.

5.3 Open market value

Based on the Estonian VAT Act, the tax authority has the right to determine the taxable amount according to
market value where the supplier and recipient are related persons for the purposes of the Income Tax Act and
where the fee payable for the transfer of the goods or provision of the services is:

+ lower than the market value and the acquirer of the goods or the recipient of the services has no right for
deduction of input value added tax in full;

+ lower than the market value and the transferor of the goods or the provider of the services has no right for
deduction of input value added tax in full and the transfer of the goods or the provision of the service is
exempt from tax; or

«higher than the market value and the transferor of the goods or the provider of the services has no right
for deduction of input value added tax in full

174 | Estonia

--- Page 175 ---
54 Returnable packaging

With reference to Article 92 of the EU VAT Directive, the Estonian VAT Act entails that the value of
returnable packaging on which a deposit has been established pursuant to the Packaging Act, is not included
in the taxable value of goods if the taxable person does not supply the returnable packaging. However, if the
returnable packaging is not returned to the taxable person within a calendar year, a supply is deemed to be
effected on 31 December of that year. The supply shall be equal to the total sum of the deposits of returnable
packages not returned during a calendar year.

6. VAT rates and exemptions

6.1 Standard rate
The standard VAT rate in Estonia is 22% (as of 1 July 2025, it will be 24%).

6.2 Reduced rate

The reduced VAT rate of 9% is applicable to certain goods and services, for example:

* books and educational literature, both on physical medium and electronically (with the exception of
certain learning materials);

+ press publications, both on a physical medium and electronically; and

+ certain qualifying medicinal products and medical devices

As of I January 2025, accommodation services or accommodation services with breakfast (excluding goods
or services accompanying such services) are subject to the 13% VAT rate.

6.3 Super-reduced rate and zero rates

Estonia did not introduce super reduced rates. For zero rates, see chapter 7 below.

7. Exemptions

7.1 Exemptions with right to deduction (zero rate)

Based on the Estonian VAT Act, the zero rate is applicable to certain goods and services, for example to:

+ export of goods;

+ intra-Community supply of goods;

* seagoing vessels navigating in international waters, except pleasure craft used for purposes other than
business;

+ aircraft used by an air cartier operating mostly on international routes and equipment, spare parts, fuel
and other supplies used on such aircraft and goods to be transferred to passengers for consumption on
board, except goods sold on board of such aircraft during intra-Community passenger transport to be
taken away;

* goods transferred and delivered to another Member State to diplomatic representatives, consular agents
(except honorary consuls), representatives or representations of special missions or international
organisations recognised by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, headquarters of an international organisation,
a diplomatic representation, a consular post, a special mission or a Union institution or an agency or body
established under the EU law;

+ non-Union goods placed under the customs procedures of customs warehousing, free zone;

+ Union goods transferred and delivered to a free zone for export purposes and Union goods placed in a
free zone which are exported directly from the free zone within two months as of the transportation to the
free zone;

+ excise goods under excise duty suspension arrangement placed in an excise warehouse;

* goods listed in Annex V of the Council Directive 2006/112/EC placed in a VAT warehouse; and

* gold sold to Bank of Estonia.

Estonia | 175

--- Page 176 ---
7.1.1 Intra-Community supplies

In order to substantiate the application of the 0% VAT rate to intra~Community supplies, the supply needs to
be evidenced by documents confirming the transfer and the transport of goods to another Member State (e.g.
delivery notes, transport documents etc). Based on the Estonian VAT Act, the 0% VAT rate cannot be applied
to such supplies if there is no valid VAT identification number of the purchaser in another Member State or
such an intra~Community supply has not been reported in the recapitulative statement on intra~Community
supply of goods.

Article 45a of Council Implementing Regulation (EU) No 282/201] entails a list of documentation with
which a taxable person if necessary must prove the transport of goods to another Member State.

7.1.2 — Export supplies
In order to substantiate the application of 0% VAT rate on export of goods, the supply needs to be evidenced

by documents confirming that the goods have left the EU (e.g. customs declaration, delivery notes, transport
documentation, proof of payment).

7.1.3. Warchousing

7.1.3.1 VAT warehouse

Estonia has implemented a VAT warehouse regime under which VAT is not applicable to the supply of
goods placed in a VAT warehouse. The location of the warehouse must be approved by the Estonian Tax and
Customs Board.

The VAT warehouse regime can only be applied in case of the supply of certain unfinished products/ bulk
goods stipulated in Annex V of the EU VAT Directive 2006/1 12/EC (e.g. goods that need further processing
in order to be sold for final consumption). These goods include for example:

* cocoa beans:

* mineral products;

+ certain chemical products;

+ raw sugar;

+ cereals, etc.

The supply of goods which are brought to or are located in the VAT warehouse are zero rated, VAT becomes
due when the goods are released from the warehouse. The owner of the goods who initiates the release is held
liable for the VAT. The VAT applicable on the sale of the goods must be paid either by the supplier or by the
customer if a domestic reverse charge applies.

Participants in transactions within the VAT warehouse need not be established or registered for VAT purposes
in Estonia.

7.13.2 Excise warehouse

Excise goods under suspension of excise duties can be stored in an excise warehouse. The supply of excise
goods which are located in an excise warehouse is subject to the 0% VAT rate if the transaction does not
involve taking the goods out of the excise warehouse, except transporting the excise goods from one excise
warehouse to another,

7.1.3.3 Bonded (customs) warehouse
It is possible to store goods coming from outside the EU in a bonded warehouse before import occurs.

If the goods coming from a third country are directly placed under a bonded warehouse regime, no customs
duties and VAT become due. When the goods are released from the warehouse for free circulation, duties and
VAT become due. The owner of the goods who declares the goods into free circulation becomes responsible
for the payment of the VAT.

‘The release of goods from a bonded warehouse regime does not constitute an importation if the goods are
subsequently placed under another customs regime.

176 | Estonia

--- Page 177 ---
The sale of goods, while still in bond, is subject to 0% VAT in Estonia. Evidence that the sales took place
in the bonded warehouse should be kept in the recordkeeping (i.e. list of the transactions maintained by the
warehouse keeper, customs documents, agreements, invoices, etc.).

7.2 Exemptions without right to deduction

7.2.1 Exemptions in the public interest

Certain goods and services of a social nature are VAT exempted without a right to input VAT deduction, for

example (additional conditions may apply):
health care services;

+ services linked to welfare and social security;

* postal services;

+ services provided by a non-profit association to its members free of charge or for a membership fee, and
services provided by a non-profit association or foundation to natural persons relating to the use of sports
facilities or sports equipment; and

+ certain educational services (e.g. pre-school, basic, vocational, secondary and higher education, private
tuition relating to general education and other training services, except other training services provided
for business purposes).

7.2.2 Other exemptions

VAT is also not imposed on the supply of the certain other goods and services, for example (additional

conditions may apply):

* certain supplies of immovable property;

* insurance services, including reinsurance and insurance mediation;

+ certain financial services, including the granting of credit, certain banking services;

+ the leasing or letting of immovables or parts thereof, establishment of a usufruct on immovables or parts
thereof (tax exemption is not applied on the provision of accommodation services, the leasing or letting of
or establishment of a usufruct on multi-storey car parks and premises for parking vehicles, and the hiring
or leasing of or establishment of a usufruct on permanently installed equipment or machinery or safes);

* postage stamps;

+ lottery tickets and gambling; and

+ supply of investment gold.

7.3. Option for taxation

A taxable person is allowed to add VAT to the taxable amount of the following goods and services if the

person has notified the Estonian Tax and Customs Board before the supply is effected:

+ the leasing or letting of immovables or parts thereof, except dwellings, and establishment of a usuftuct on
immovables or parts thereof;

+ immovables and parts thereof, except dwellings;

+ financial services, except in cases where the service is provided to a taxable person or a taxable person
with limited deduction right in another Member State:

* — securities transactions, except certain transactions; and

* investment gold under certain conditions.

Ifa taxable person adds VAT to the taxable value of services under the optional taxation, such supply shall be
taxed for at least two years following the start of the first taxable period.

8. _ Liability

8.1 Reverse Charges

Cross-border services supplied to an Estonian VAT registered person that is engaged in an economic activity
will be, as a general rule, subject to a reverse charge. This reverse charge applies in all EU Member States and
follows from Articles 44 and 196 EU VAT Directive.

Estonia | 177

--- Page 178 ---
There is also reverse charge for the supply of natural gas and electricity supplied by non-resident suppliers to
“taxable dealers” in Estonia (Article 195 EU VAT Directive).

In addition, Estonia has implemented a general reverse charge for goods and services supplied by a non-
resident supplier (see 8.1.1) as well as a domestic reverse charge applicable to certain items (see 8.1.2).

8.1.1 General reverse charge mechanism - non-resident suppliers (Article 194 EU VAT Directive)

All taxable persons established in Estonia are obliged to calculate VAT under the reverse charge mechanism
for the goods and services supplied by a non-resident supplier. The general reverse charge mechanism applies
to all supplies of goods and services which are subject to VAT in Estonia. The reverse charge does not apply
if the non-established supplier performs a domestic supply of goods/servives to a non-taxable legal person in
Estonia. Furthermore, the reverse charge docs not apply if the non-resident supplier has a VAT number and/or
a fixed establishment in Estonia.

On the invoice, reference to the application of the reverse charge is required.

8.1.2 Specific domestic reverse charges (Article 199 EU VAT Directive)

Estonia has implemented domestic reverse charge rules that apply to:

+ supply of immovable property or its parts (where option to tax has been exercised);

+ scrap metal;

+ investment gold (where option to tax has been exercised);

+ precious metals (certain exceptions apply); and

+ supply of metal products with CN-codes 7208-7220 (except welding wire and welding rods) 7222,
7225, 7226, 7228 (except welding rods), 73011000, 730300-7306 (certain exceptions apply), 73081000,
73082000, 73121061, 73121069, 731420 and 73143900,

The domestic reverse charge on the above items applies only if both the supplier and the customer are
registered for VAT in Estonia. On the invoice, reference to the application of the reverse charge is required.
8.2 di nt and several liability

Members of a VAT group will be jointly and severally liable for payment of VAT by the due date.

The fiscal representative appointed by a non-resident company, is required to ensure that the principal’s

monetary and non-monetary obligations concerning taxes are fulfilled within the set term and in full, ic. is
jointly and severally liable for all the tax liabilities of the foreign taxable person it represents

Under Estonian law, a legal representative (a member of the management body of the company) can be held
jointly and severally liable for tax arrears of the company when certain conditions are met.

9, Recovery of input VAT

9.1 Non recoverable input VAT

In Estonia, input VAT cannot be recovered if it is:

* related to goods or services accruing from the reception of guests or the provision of meals or
accommodation for employees (with the exception of accommodation provided during business trips); or

* not related to a taxable person’s taxable business.

Only 50% of VAT is recoverable on the purchase and lease of passenger cars (incl. maintenance, fuel etc.), if

not used exclusively for business purposes.

9.2 Excess input VAT

If the VAT liability calculated for a taxable period is less than the amount of input VAT deductible by the
taxable person during the same period, the overpaid amount of VAT may be carried forward to the following

178 | Estonia

--- Page 179 ---
tax periods or may be recovered. The excess VAT can be recovered by presenting an application to the
Estonian Tax and Customs Board. Generally, the excess VAT will be refunded by the tax authorities within 30
days of the receipt of an application. This period may be extended for up to 90 days if the tax authorities have
justified reasons to further check the circumstances of the VAT refund application.

9.3. Adjustment periods

The period for adjustment of input VAT (adjustment period) is:
* 10 calendar years for immovable property and goods and services relating thereto; and
+ 5 calendar years for other fixed assets and goods and services relating thereto.

The period of time between the date of registration in the accounting documents of fixed assets or goods
acquired or services received for the fixed assets as fixed assets in use and the last day of the current calendar
year is deemed to be the first calendar year.

94 VAT refunds to EU taxable persons

The application for VAT refund must be submitted electronically to the Estonian tax authority through the tax
authority of the country of establishment of the taxable person. The annual minimum limit is EUR 50. The
interim minimum limit is EUR 400, The minimum period for making a claim is 3 months. The maximum
period for making a claim is one calendar year. The deadline for submitting the application is no later than
30 September of the calendar year following the period of refund. It is not possible to submit the application
afier the deadline.

The application must contain general data (applicant’s name, address, e-mail, bank account, VAT number) and
the description of the applicant’s field of operation for which goods or services were bought in Estonia, The
applicant must also confirm that it did not have any taxable supplies in Estonia (except zero rated supplies
and supplies subject to a reverse charge). Original invoices or copies of invoices are not required. Electronic
copies must be submitted of more substantial invoices (in the case of fuel if the net amount is at least EUR
250 and in other cases if the net amount is at least EUR 1,000).

The time limit for the Estonian Tax and Customs Board to decide is 4 months or, in the event of the
authority’s requesting additional information, 6 to 8 months after the receipt of the application. [f the
application is approved, the VAT must be refunded no later than 10 working days after notifying the taxable
person of the decision. An appeal can be filed within 30 days starting from the date of notification or of
delivery of the administrative act.

General rules apply to the non-refundability of VAT (please see paragraph 9.1).

95 VAT refunds to non-EU taxable persons

Estonia requires reciprocity when refunding the VAT to non-BU established taxable persons. Currently the list
of qualifying jurisdictions is comprised of the UK, Norway, Iceland, Israel and Switzerland,

However, there is an exception for a non-EU person engaged in business who applies the One Stop Shop
regime for the supply of B2C services and distance sales, Such a person is entitled to a refund of the input
VAT relating to such supply, irrespective of whether or not an Estonian person is entitled to a refund of VAT
in the person’s country of residence. Furthermore, this is applicable regardless of whether the person is a
taxable person for VAT purposes in the country in which the person is located.

The application must be submitted by the non-EU taxable person directly to the Estonian Tax and Customs
Board.

The application form can be downloaded at: hifps://inyurl.com/3ndS4zrw

‘The application must be submitted on paper and must be signed by a natural person, a head of a legal entity
or by an authorized representative, and it must contain the number of the bank account and a note as to where
and in whose name the VAT should be refunded.

Estonia | 179

--- Page 180 ---
The annual minimum limit is EUR 320. Non-EU taxable persons may submit the application only once per
calendar year, The minimum and maximum period for submitting an application is one calendar year. The
deadline for submitting the application is no later than 30 September of the calendar year following the period
of the refund, It is not possible to submit the application after the deadline.

The application must contain original invoices or documents confirming the VAT payment on imporiation
(these will be marked and sent back to applicant). In addition, a certificate from the tax authorities that the
applicant is liable to pay VAT in the country of habitual residence must be attached to the application.

if the application and supporting documents are fulfilling the requirements, VAT will be refunded within 6
months from the date of submission of the application and required documents, An appeal can be filed within
30 days starting from the date of notification of or delivery of the administrative act.

General rules apply to the non-recoverability of VAT (see paragraph 9.1).

10. Administrative obligations

10.1 Invoices

10.1.1 Invoice content

A VAT invoice should contain the following data:

+ the sequential number;

+ the date the invoice is issued;

+ full name and address of the supplier and its VAT identification number;

+ full name and address of the customer of the goods or services;

+ the VAT identification number of the customer of the goods of services in the case of an intra-Community
supply or the customer is liable to pay the VAT due;

* name or description of the goods or services;

+ quantity of the goods or extent of the services;

+ date of dispatch of the goods or provision of the services and/or an earlier date of receipt of full or partial
payment for the goods or services if this date can be determined and differs from the date of issue of the
invoice;

+ price of the goods or services exclusive of VAT and any discounts, if these are not included in the price;

+ taxable amount broken down by different VAT rates together with the applicable VAT rates or the amount
of supply exempt from tax;

* amount of VAT payable in EUR;

+ details of a tax representative, if any;

* _ in case of self-billing, a corresponding reference “endale arve koostamine” is required;

+ the indication for the application of an exemption, zero rate or reverse charge. For instance:

*  intra-Community supply: “Zero rated intra-Community supply” “tihendusesisene kaive”; export
supply: “Zero rated export supply” “ekspordikaive”; and

* B2B service: “Service not subject to Estonian VAT according to Article 44 VAT Directive; VAT due
by the recipient of the service according to Article 196 VAT Directive”, “pédrdmaksustamine”.

+ the information necessary to determine whether there is a new means of transport and also a reference to
clause 15 (3) 2) of VAT Act; and

+ an indication of the fact that a margin scheme has been applied as well as an indication why the margin
scheme applied:

* margin scheme - second-hand goods “kasuminormi maksustamise kord - kasutatud kaubad”;

* margin scheme - works of art “kasuminormi maksustamise kord — kunstiteosed”;

* margin scheme - collector’s items and antiques “kasuminormi maksustamise kord — kollektsiooni- ja
antiikesemed”; and

* tour operator margin scheme ~ “kasuminormi maksustamise kord — reisibiirood”.

180 | Estonia

--- Page 181 ---
10.1.2 Time limits

An invoice should generally be issued within seven calendar days from the date on which the goods have
been dispatched or made available to the customer or the services have been provided. Electricity, heating and
water suppliers invoice their clients in general on a monthly basis.

For an intra-Community supply of goods or upon the provision of services to a taxable person or taxable
person with limited liability in another Member State, a taxable person shall issue an invoice by the fifteenth
day of the month following the month in which the goods are dispatched or made available, or the service is
provided.

10.1.3 Currency
Invoices are accepted in all currencies but the amount of VAT must be stated in EUR.

10.1.4 Conversion and exchange rate

Where information required for the calculation of the amount of VAT on the importation of goods is
expressed in a foreign currency, the exchange rate must be determined in accordance with the provisions of
the Community Customs Code governing the calculation of value for customs purposes. When information
required for the calculation of the amount of VAT on a transaction other than an import transaction is
expressed in a foreign currency, the exchange rate to EUR applies as determined by the European Central
Bank on the day when the supply of the goods or services or intra~-Community acquisition takes place.

10.1.5 Self-billing

The Estonian VAT Act allows invoices to be issued by the customer on the condition that, before the supply
is effected, there is a written agreement between the two parties stating that an invoices are issued by the
customer and the supplier accepts it. An agreement must contain the procedure for the acceptance of invoices
by a supplier. A self-billing invoice must make a reference to self-billing.

10.1.6 Simplified invoice

A simplified invoice may be issued, provided that the amount indicated in the invoice does not exceed EUR
160 (exclusive of VAT), in the following cases:

«the provision of transport services for passengers;

+ inthe case of invoices printed by parking meters, automated petrol stations and other similar machines.

The following information must be included on a simplified invoice:
+ the date of issue of the invoice;

+ the name of the supplier and its VAT registration number:

» the name or a description of the goods or services;

+ the taxable amount; and

+ the amount of VAT to be paid

A simplified invoice issued to a taxable person must include the name of the taxable person and its VAT
registration number.
10.1.7 Storage of invoices

The minimum term for the storage of invoices is 7 years (except for goods and services provided through
online marketplace in which case 10 years applies).

Invoices can be stored in electronic form or on paper.

A taxable person may choose the place at which invoices are preserved and the manner thereof on the
condition that the person makes the invoices or information preserved therein immediately available at the
request of the tax authority. Therefore, invoices can be stored anywhere, but immediate availability must be
guaranteed.

10.1.8 Electronic invoicing

Invoices can be issued in an electronic form and must contain the same information as a paper invoice. The
receiver of the invoice must agree that the invoice is issued electronically. Usually, a written agreement

Estonia | 181

--- Page 182 ---
between the parties provides for this. Information on whether the company accepts electronic invoices or not
is made publicly available in the Estonian Commercial Register.

Electronic invoicing is mandatory in Estonia for business-to-government (B2G) supplies, in line with EU
Directive 2014/55/EU.

10.2 VAT compliance

10.2.1 VAT return period
In general, VAT returns must be filed monthly. The first period for a taxable person or taxable person with
limited liability is the period from the date of VAT registration until the end of the same calendar month. If the
number of calendar days in the first taxable period is less than 15, it is allowed to declare the supplies over the
first period together with the supplies over the following period and submit one return for both periods.

On the basis of a reasoned request made by a taxable person, the tax authority may establish, at their
discretion, a taxable period longer than one calendar month (e.g. quarter).

10.2.2 ing of the VAT return and payment

VAT returns should be submitted to the tax authority and VAT must be paid by the 20" day of the month
following the period of taxation. If the due date falls on a weckend or on a public holiday, it is extended until
the next working day.

VAT returns are submitted electronically if the person has been a taxable person for at least 12 months or
more than five invoices are included in the appendix to the VAT return. On the basis of a reasoned request
made by a taxable person the Estonian Tax and Customs Board may allow the submission of a VAT return on
paper.

The form of the VAT return (Vorm KMD) and its annex (KMD TNF) can be downloaded at:
https://inyurl.com/mrxuap43

10.2.3. European sales listing

The deadline for the submission of the monthly European sales listing is the 20 day of the month following
the month of supply.

‘The form of the EU sales list (Vorm VD) can be downloaded at:
hittps:/tinyurl.com/mrxuap43

10.2.4 National recapitulative statement
There are no national recapitulative statements in Estonia.

10.2.5 Tax assessment period

The limitation period for making an assessment of tax is three years. In the event of intentional failure to pay
or withhold a tax, the limitation period for making an assessment of tax is five years.

A limitation period begins to run on the due date for the submission of the tax return which was not submitted
or which contained information which caused the amount of tax to be calculated incorrectly.
10.3. Penalties

Estonian VAT Act does not contain specific provisions on penalties which means that the general rules on
penalties in tax matters apply to VAT.

10.3.1 Default penalties for late registration
The tax authorities may issue a penalty of up to EUR 3,200 for failure to register in time.

10.3.2 Defauit penalties for not filing a VAT return or a European sales listing on time
Penalty for not submitting a tax return or any other declaration is punishable by a fine of up to FUR 3,200.

182 | Estonia

--- Page 183 ---
10.3.3 Default penalties for not paying VAT on time to the tax authorities

fa taxable person fails to pay VAT by the due date, he is required to pay interest on the amount of tax
outstanding computed from the due date. The interest rate is 0.06% per day charged on amounts of VAT
underpaid or paid late

10.3.4 Default penalties on VAT assessments when VAT is not reported correctly

Ifa taxable person fails to pay VAT by the due date, he is required to pay interest on the amount of tax
outstanding computed from the due date. The interest rate is 0.06% per day charged on amounts of VAT
underpaid or paid late.

Failure to submit information to a tax authority intentionally or submission of false information to decrease
VAT obligation or increase a VAT refund is punishable by a fine of up to EUR 32,000 for a legal person.

10.3.5 Offence penalties
The violation of the tax liability may be punishable as a misdemeanour, as well as a criminal offense.

Criminal offences are defined in the Estonian Penal Code. It is possible to punish both legal persons (with a
fine) and natural persons (with a fine or imprisonment for up to 7 years).

10.4 Tntrastat
Intrastat reports must be submitted on a monthly basis to the Statistics Estonia.

10.4.1 Intrastat threshold

For 2025, the annual threshold for Intrastat reporting for arrivals and was abandoned in 2025. The threshold
for dispatches is EUR 350,000.

10.4.2 Intrastat data to be reported

The information to be reported is:

+ commodity code of the goods;

+ the EU Member State of origin/destination;

* country of origin;

* nature of the transaction;

+ quantity and supplementary unit;

+ net weight in kilograms;

+ the invoice value of the goods; and

+ VAT identification number of the counterparty with the Member State code.

10.5 Additional compliance requirements

There is an obligation for Estonian VAT registered persons to complete Annex KMD INF to the monthly VAT
return if transactions with any domestic counterparty exceed EUR 1,000 in one month. The details of all the
relevant sales and purchase invoices related to that counterparty shall be declared in the Annex KMD INF A
and B to the VAT return,

Further instructions on the Annex KMD INF can be found at: Aitps-//tinyurl.com/mrxuap43
